# Rigveda Samhita — Volume 16 (Kannada → English translation)

### Printed p. 2

*[Running head, p. 2: Sāyaṇabhāṣyasahitā, Maṃ. 3, A. 1, Sū. 7.]*

*[The window begins in the middle of Sāyaṇa's introduction to Sūkta 7 (the earlier part is on p. 1, outside this window). The Sanskrit fragment below is copied from kn.md as supplied; the first word is cut at the start of the page.]*

⟦ಪಿಜವನಃ ಪುನಃ ಸ್ಪರ್ಧನೀಯಜವೋ ವಾಮಿಶ್ರೀಭಾವಗತಿರ್ವಾ ॥ ಸ ವಿತ್ತಂ ಗೃಹೀತ್ವಾ
ವಿಪಾಟ್ ಛುತುದ್ರೋಃ ಸಂಭೇದಮಾಯಯೌ । ಅನುಯಯುರಿತರೇ । ಸ ವಿಶ್ವಾಮಿತ್ರೋ
ನದೀಸ್ತುಷ್ಟಾಪ-ಗಾಧಾ ಭವತ-ಇತಿ । ಆಪಿ ದ್ವಿವದಪಿ ಬಹುವತ್ ತದ್ಯದ್ದ್ವದುಪರಿಷ್ಟಾತ್ತ-
ದ್ವ್ಯಾಖ್ಯಾಸ್ಯಾಮಃ ॥⟧ (printed సి. 2-24, the abbreviation in Telugu script as supplied)

Gist: the passage gives the story of Viśvāmitra, who takes the wealth, comes to the confluence of the Vipāś and the Śutudrī, and praises the rivers so that they become fordable; the other (Bharatas) follow him.

**Anuvāda (Kannada).** Thus an old story is told. "Viśvāmitra" means that this seer is a friend to all, or that all are his friends; this is the etymological sense of the word. This seer was the priest ([sic] the supplied text reads पुरोतनागिद्दनु (*purotanāgiddanu*), evidently for *purohita*) of the king named Sudāsa, the son of Pijavana. Once, taking the wealth given by that king and setting out to go to his hermitage, he came to the confluence of the rivers Vipāś and Śutudrī (the present Beas and Sutlej of the Punjab). Thieves who wished to seize the wealth he carried were following him. Knowing this, Viśvāmitra tried by some means to cross these rivers that lay across his path, but they were very deep with the water of the flood and he could not. Then the seer prayed to the rivers and praised them, that they should reduce their water and make it convenient for him to pass; this is said in the 33rd sūkta (Ṛ. Saṃ. 3-33) and in verses 9-11 of Ṛ. Saṃ. 3-53.

This seer Viśvāmitra in the sūkta above, that is, in Ṛ. Saṃ. 3-33-5, calls himself the son of Kuśika. In the Rāmāyaṇa, the Bhārata and other Purāṇas it is said that there was enmity and ill-will between Viśvāmitra and the seer Vasiṣṭha. There is some support for this in a few places in the Ṛgveda ([sic] the supplied text reads आधांवू (*ādhāṃvū*), evidently for आधारवू (*ādhāravū*), "support"). That will be discussed in the Pīṭhike (introduction) of this part.

The names of Viśvāmitra and of this seer's descendants are read in the mantras Ṛ. Saṃ. 3-1-21; 3-18-4; 3-53-13; 10-89-17; Atharvaveda 18-3-6; 18-4-54; Jaiminīya Upaniṣad Brāhmaṇa 3-15-1 and others. In the story of Śunaḥśepa the part of Viśvāmitra is very important; we have described it at length in Part 2 of the Ṛgveda Saṃhitā. That Viśvāmitra is a principal seer is described in Aitareya (printed ऐतरेय वर्णक (*aitareya varṇaka*), "Aitareya varṇaka") 2-2-1; Ai. Brā. 6-18-1; 6-20-3; Tai. Saṃ. 2-2-1-2; 3-1-7-3; 5-2-3-4; Kāṭhaka Saṃhitā 16-19; 20-9; Maitrāyaṇīya Saṃhitā 2-7-19; Kauṣītaki Brāhmaṇa 15-1; 26-14; 28-1,2; 29-3; Pañcaviṃśa Brāhmaṇa 14-3-12; Bṛ. U. 2-2-4; Jaiminīya Upaniṣad Brāhmaṇa 3-3-13; 3-15-1 and other places.

In the Purāṇas and the like Viśvāmitra is said to have been a king who became a Brāhmaṇa by austerity. In the Ṛgveda there is no support at all for this. But in the Nirukta (Ni. 2-24) it is said that Viśvāmitra was the son of a king named Kauśika; and in the Pañcaviṃśa Brāhmaṇa also (Paṃ. Vi. Brā. 21-12-2) Viśvāmitra is said to have been a king. In the Ṛgveda alone there is no support for this.

### Printed p. 3

*[Running head, p. 3: [Maṃ. 3, A. 1, Va. 1] Ṛgvedasaṃhitā 3.]*

Heading as printed: "The fifth sūkta in the third maṇḍala" (ಮೂರನೆಯ ಮಂಡಲದಲ್ಲಿ ಐದನೆಯ ಸೂಕ್ತವು); the Anuvāda below and the head-lines say seventh (7). The printed word "fifth" is kept as supplied.

## Sūkta 7 (Maṇḍala 3)

**Sāyaṇa's introduction to the sūkta** (Sanskrit, transcribed; see the Anuvāda below for the sense):

**॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥**

> सोमस्येत्यनवाके द्वादश सूक्तानि । तत्र प्र य आरुरित्येकादशर्चं स-
> प्तमं सूक्तं , मंडलद्रष्टा विश्वामित्र ऋषिः ' अनुक्तत्वात् त्रिष्टुप्छंदः । मं-
> डलादिपरिभाषयाग्निर्देवता ! प्रातरनुवाकाश्विनशस्त्रयोः सूक्तत्रयस्य प्रत्यग्निरुषस-
> स्त्रीणीति विनियोग उक्तः । तदेकं तृतीयं सूक्तं ॥

> *somasyetyanavāke dvādaśa sūktāni | tatra pra ya ārurityekādaśarcaṃ sa-*
> *ptamaṃ sūktaṃ , maṃḍaladraṣṭā viśvāmitra ṛṣiḥ ' anuktatvāt triṣṭupchaṃdaḥ | maṃ-*
> *ḍalādiparibhāṣayāgnirdevatā ! prātaranuvākāśvinaśastrayoḥ sūktatrayasya pratyagniruṣasa-*
> *strīṇīti viniyoga uktaḥ | tadekaṃ tṛtīyaṃ sūktaṃ ‖*

**Anuvāda (Kannada).** In the third maṇḍala of the Ṛgveda Saṃhitā, in the first anuvāka, beginning with सोमस्य (*somasya*), there are twelve sūktas. Among them the sūkta beginning प्र य आरुः (*pra ya āruḥ*) is the seventh; it has eleven ṛks (as printed here; the head-lines below give 20). As Viśvāmitra is the seer of this whole third maṇḍala, he is the seer of this sūkta also. The metre is Triṣṭubh. The deity is Agni. As it has been said before that the three sūktas beginning प्रत्यग्निरुषसः (*pratyagniruṣasaḥ*) (the 5th, 6th and 7th sūktas) are applied to the recitation of the Prātaranuvāka mantras and the Āśvina śastra mantras, and as this is the third of those three sūktas, the same application given above holds for this sūkta also.

Sūkta—7. Maṇḍala—3. Anuvāka—1. Sūkta—7. Aṣṭaka—3. Adhyāya—1. Varga—1, 2. Number of ṛks in the sūkta—20. Ṛṣi—Viśvāmitra. Devatā—Agni. Chandas—Triṣṭubh.

#### Ṛk 1

**॥ संहितापाठः ॥**

> प्र य आ॒रुः शि॑तपृ॒ष्ठस्य॑ धा॒सेरा मा॒तरा॑ विविशुः स॒प्त वाणीः॑ ।
> प॒रि॒क्षिता॑ पि॒तरा॒ सं च॑रेते॒ प्र स॑स्र्राते दी॒र्घमायुः॑ प्र॒यक्षे॑ ॥

**॥ पदपाठः ॥**

> प्र । ये । आरुः । शित्तिपृष्ठस्य । धासेः । आ । मातरा । विवितुः । सप्त । वाणीः ।
> परिक्षिता । हितरा । सं । चरेते इति । प्र । सर्स्राते इति । दीर्घं । आयुः ।
> प्रयक्षे ॥ १ ॥

> *pra | ye | āruḥ | śittipṛṣṭhasya | dhāseḥ | ā | mātarā | vivituḥ | sapta | vāṇīḥ |*
> *parikṣitā | hitarā | saṃ | carete iti | pra | sarsrāte iti | dīrghaṃ | āyuḥ |*
> *prayakṣe ‖ 1 ‖*

*Gist (mine): Agni's rays have entered heaven and earth and the flowing rivers; heaven and earth, surrounding, move together with Agni and spread long life or abundant food for the sake of sacrifice; or they give the sacrificer long life.* The grammatical tail of the bhāṣya (accent and formation of आरुः (*āruḥ*), शितिपृष्ठस्य (*śitipṛṣṭhasya*), धासेः (*dhāseḥ*), मातरा (*mātarā*), विविशुः (*viviśuḥ*), सप्त (*sapta*), क्षिता (*kṣitā*), चरेते (*carete*), सस्राते (*sasrāte*), आयुः (*āyuḥ*), प्रयक्षे (*prayakṣe*), citing Uṇ. 2-96, Uṇ. 1-56 and Pā. 1-3-54) is noted here but not explained.

**प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)**

*[Before the Pratipadārtha the supplied text has the stray letters क, ति, स, व, ध, ग (*ka, ti, sa, va, dha, ga*), kept in kn.md as supplied. The first word is printed तिशिपृष्ठस्य (*tiśipṛṣṭhasya*) in the Pratipadārtha, but शितिपृष्टस्य (*śitipṛṣṭasya*) in the bhāṣya; both are kept as supplied.]*

Śitipṛṣṭhasya (printed so): of the blue-backed one; dhāseḥ: of Agni, the support of all; ye raśmayaḥ: whichever rays; pra āruḥ: go forth upward abundantly; (te): those rays; mātarā: the parents, the mother and father, heaven and earth; ā viviśuḥ: have entered from all sides; (and) sapta: of flowing nature; vāṇīḥ: the worship-worthy rivers also (have entered); parikṣitā: surrounding; pitarā: heaven and earth, standing in the place of parents; saṃ carete

### Printed p. 5

*[Running head, p. 5: [Maṃ. 3, A. 1, Va. 1] Ṛgvedasaṃhitā 5.]*

(with Agni) deal well together; (and) prayakṣe: for the sake of abundant performance of sacrifice; dīrghaṃ āyuḥ: (to Agni) long life [or abundant food]; prayakṣe: abundantly and constantly, for the sake of performing sacrifices; dīrghaṃ āyuḥ: (to the sacrificer) long life [or abundant food]; pra sasrāte: spread (grant ... the supplied text breaks off here with an unfinished word, ऒदगि (*òdagi*), with no closing bracket).

**भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)**

The rays of Agni, the blue-backed and all-supporting, going out from Agni, have pervaded everywhere the world and the parents, व्याघ्रापृथिविगळन्नु (*vyāghrāpṛthivigaḷannu*) ([sic] as supplied; evidently *dyāvāpṛthivī*, heaven and earth); in the same way the flowing and worship-worthy rivers also are pervaded. The all-pervading heaven and earth, so that sacrifices may go on in abundance, grant long life to Agni and abundant food to the sacrificer, and सिद्धरिसुत्तवॆ (*siddharisuttavè*) (as supplied; the sense seems to be "work together") with Agni.

**Printed English translation**

The (rays) of the blackbacked, all-sustaining (Agni) have arisen, and pervaded the parents (earth and heaven) and the flowing rivers ; the surrounding parents co-operate with him, and bestow long life for the sake of assiduous worship.

**विशेषविषयगळु (Viśeṣaviṣayagaḷu)**

धासेः— सर्वस्य धारयितुः । (*dhāseḥ— sarvasya dhārayituḥ |*) The author of the Nirukta reads the word धासिः (*dhāsiḥ*) among the twenty-eight names of food (Ni. 3-9). Sāyaṇa has explained the sense as "Agni bears everything". He also gives the meaning that the rays of this Agni shine greatly.

मातरा विविशुः सप्तवाणीः (*mātarā viviśuḥ saptavāṇīḥ*): the word वाणी (*vāṇī*) is read in the Nirukta among the twenty-seven names of speech (Ni. 2-23). Here the meaning "rivers" is given, and it is explained that the rays of Agni pervade the rivers. The sense that the word सप्त (*sapta*) is not a numeral is indicated here; the word is explained as "of pervading nature", and the nature of the rays is described. Though the word मातरा (*mātarā*) is not stated among the names of heaven and earth in the Nirukta, here the word is explained as heaven and earth, and it is described that the rays of Agni pervade heaven and earth (earth and the middle region). The fact that heaven and earth, like mother and father, are the nourishers of all is stated in the ṛks.

परिक्षिता पितरा—परितो वर्तमाने पितरौ द्यावापृथिव्यौ । (*parikṣitā pitarā—parito vartamāne pitarau dyāvāpṛthivyau |*) The meaning is: heaven and earth (earth and the middle region), who are the mother and father of the world, pervade everything, and they co-operate in the world with Agni.

### Printed p. 6

*[Running head, p. 6: 6 Sāyaṇabhāṣyasahitā [Maṃ. 3. A. 1. Sū. 7].]*

(Special Topics of Ṛk 1, continued.) Regarding the sentence प्रसर्स्राते दीर्घमायुः प्रयक्षे (*prasarsrāte dīrghamāyuḥ prayakṣe*), Sāyaṇa has explained two kinds of meaning. In the first meaning, heaven and earth are described as bringing about the abundant food needed for the sacrifice, so that the sacrifice may proceed without obstacle, and Agni as bringing about a proper state and continuance while the sacrifice is going on. The word आयुः (*āyuḥ*) is read among the twenty-eight names of food (Ni. 3-9); from this Sāyaṇa gives the meaning "abundant food". In the second meaning, introduced by the Sanskrit words below, it is explained that heaven and earth grant to the sacrificer who performs the sacrifice at all times an increase of आयुस्सु (*āyussu*) (lifetime), so that he may live for a long time:

द्यावापृथिव्यौ प्रकर्षेण सततं यष्टुं यजमानस्य दीर्घमायुः संपादयतः (*dyāvāpṛthivyau prakarṣeṇa satataṃ yaṣṭuṃ yajamānasya dīrghamāyuḥ saṃpādayataḥ*) (as supplied: प्रक-र्षेण (*praka-rṣeṇa*) is split across a line end).

The author of the Nirukta, on the matter of heaven and earth being mother and father, cites the following ṛk (the reference is printed य. सं. (*ya. saṃ.*) "Ya. Saṃ." 1-164-33, as supplied):

दौर्मे पिता जनिता नाभिरत्र बंधुर्मे माता पृथिवी महीयं । (*daurme pitā janitā nābhiratra baṃdhurme mātā pṛthivī mahīyaṃ |*)
उत्यासयोश्चमै ३ र्योनिरंतरत्रा पिता दुहितुर्गर्भमाधात् ॥ (*utyāsayoścamai 3 ryoniraṃtaratrā pitā duhiturgarbhamādhāt ‖*)

Durgācārya (comments):

उडकं हि द्युलोकात् पतितं पार्थिवेन धातुना संपृक्तं ओषधीभावमागम्य शरीरभावेन अवतिष्ठते इत्येतदपेक्ष्य सर्वभूतानां द्यावापृथिव्यौ मातापितरौ उच्येते (*uḍakaṃ hi dyulokāt patitaṃ pārthivena dhātunā saṃpṛktaṃ oṣadhībhāvamāgamya śarīrabhāvena avatiṣṭhate ityetadapekṣya sarvabhūtānāṃ dyāvāpṛthivyau mātāpitarau ucyete*) (as supplied).

The meaning is explained thus: the water that falls from the heaven-world (the firmament) onto the earth in the form of rain, joining with the earth, takes the form of plants and the like and then the form of the bodies of living beings; therefore the earth and the firmament (heaven and earth) have the character of mother and father to all beings. Having explained this, he cites the ṛk (Ṛ. Saṃ. 10-88-15):

द्वे स्रुती अश्वणवं पित्थाणांमहं देवानामुत मर्त्यानां (*dve srutī aśvaṇavaṃ pitthāṇāṃmahaṃ devānāmuta martyānāṃ*)
ताभ्यामिदं विश्वमेजत्समेति यदंतरा पितरं मातरं च ॥ (*tābhyāmidaṃ viśvamejatsameti yadaṃtarā pitaraṃ mātaraṃ ca ‖*)

and (continues):

तत्कृतस्य उपकारस्याभावे सतीतरौ मातापितरावकिंचित्करावेव भवतः । तस्मादुपपद्यते द्यावापृथिव्योः मातापितृभावः । एवं द्यावापृथिवीभ्यां अंतरिक्षेण च अवकाशदानेन अनुगृह्यमाणेन एतानि अस्मदादीनि भूतान्युत्पद्यंत इति अनेक मंत्रेण मंत्रदैशा स्थापितं (*tatkṛtasya upakārasyābhāve satītarau mātāpitarāvakiṃcitkarāveva bhavataḥ | tasmādupapadyate dyāvāpṛthivyoḥ mātāpitṛbhāvaḥ | evaṃ dyāvāpṛthivībhyāṃ aṃtarikṣeṇa ca avakāśadānena anugṛhyamāṇena etāni asmadādīni bhūtānyutpadyaṃta iti aneka maṃtreṇa maṃtradaiśā sthāpitaṃ*) (as supplied).

The meaning is explained thus: in the world, if there were not the help of this heaven and earth, the mother and father who are the cause of the birth of living beings would be doers of no kind of work whatever. Because heaven and earth have granted space, the bodies of living beings come into being; the seer who saw the mantra has understood the mantra teaching this meaning directly and stated it here.

Sāyaṇa's gloss, quoted here: द्यावापृथिव्यौ यॆष्टुं (दीर्घमायुः) जीवनं प्रभूतं अन्नं वा (प्रसारयतः) संपादयतः (*dyāvāpṛthivyau yèṣṭuṃ (dīrghamāyuḥ) jīvanaṃ prabhūtaṃ annaṃ vā (prasārayataḥ) saṃpādayataḥ*) (as supplied; यॆष्टुं (*yèṣṭuṃ*) for the bhāṣya's यष्टुं (*yaṣṭuṃ*)). Heaven and earth produce abundant food and lifetime so that the sacrifice may go on. In this sense the author of the Nirukta cites

### Printed p. 7

*[Running head, p. 7: [Maṃ. 3, A. 1, Va. 1] Ṛgvedasaṃhitā 7. The supplied text prints "ಕ." where "ಮಂ." (Maṃ.) is expected; kept as supplied.]*

*[Two lines in Tamil script stand at the top of p. 7 in the supplied text (apparently a stray ornament or filler of the printed page; not translated, kept in kn.md as supplied).]*

the ṛk

द्यावा नः पृथिवी इमं सिध्रमद्य दिविस्पृतं । (*dyāvā naḥ pṛthivī imaṃ sidhramadya divispṛtaṃ |*)
यज्ञं देवेषु यच्छतां ॥ (*yajñaṃ deveṣu yacchatāṃ ‖*)

and explains: द्यावापृथिव्यौ न इमं साधनमद्य दिविस्कृतं यज्ञं देवेषु नियच्छतां (*dyāvāpṛthivyau na imaṃ sādhanamadya diviskṛtaṃ yajñaṃ deveṣu niyacchatāṃ*) (as supplied). "May these heaven and earth make our sacrifice, the means of welfare, such that the gods are pleased." Since in this ṛk it is described that Agni enters heaven and earth, the purport is indicated that heaven and earth, pervaded by the rays of Agni, are beneficial to the world.

**Vyākaraṇa-prakriyā**

Grammar pages (pages 7 and 8), noted briefly. Words treated, in order: प्र (*pra*) (as निपात (*nipāta*), initial-acute, citing Phiṭ-sūtra 80 as printed), आरुः (*āruḥ*) (root ऋ (*ṛ*), perfect, third plural, ending उस् (*us*); the accent-suppression is blocked by the connection with the relative; Pā. 8-1-66), शितिपृष्ठस्य (*śitipṛṣṭhasya*) (root पृषु सेचने (*pṛṣu secane*), थक् (*thak*)-ending; compound accent), धासेः (*dhāseḥ*) (root डुधाञ् (*ḍudhāñ*) or धेट् (*dheṭ*), Uṇādi सि (*si*)), मातरा (*mātarā*) (Uṇ. 2-252 as printed; dual, ऋतो जि सर्वनामस्थानयोः (*ṛto ji sarvanāmasthānayoḥ*) Pā. 7-3-110; सुपांसुलुक् (*supāṃsuluk*) Pā. 7-1-34), विवितुः (*vivituḥ*) (as supplied, for the विविशुः (*viviśuḥ*) of the bhāṣya; root विश प्रवेशने (*viśa praveśane*), perfect; Pā. 1-2-5; Pā. 8-1-28).

Grammar page continued, noted briefly: सप्त (*sapta*) (root सृप्ल् गतौ (*sṛpl gatau*); Uṇādi कनिन् (*kanin*) ending and तुट् (*tuṭ*) augment by बहुल (*bahula*); Uṇ. 1-155 as printed here, a different number from the one in the bhāṣya tail of Ṛk 1), परिक्षिता (*parikṣitā*) (root क्षि निवासगत्योः (*kṣi nivāsagatyoḥ*), क्विप् (*kvip*); Pā. 3-2-76; Pā. 6-1-30 as printed; Pā. 6-2-139), पितरा (*pitarā*) (as मातरा (*mātarā*)), चरेते (*carete*) (root चर गतिभक्षणयोः (*cara gatibhakṣaṇayoḥ*) with सम् (*sam*); middle voice by Pā. 1-3-54), सर्स्राते (*sarsrāte*) (root सृ (*sṛ*), with inner causative sense; Vedic perfect with extra र (*ra*) in the reduplication; dual in ए (*e*) is प्रगृह्य (*pragṛhya*)), आयुः (*āyuḥ*) (root इण् गतौ (*iṇ gatau*), Uṇ. 2-275 as printed, उसिन् (*usin*); Pā. 6-1-197), प्रयक्षे (*prayakṣe*) (root यज (*yaja*), सेन् (*sen*) ending in the sense of the infinitive, Pā. 3-4-9; Pā. 1-1-39; Pā. 2-4-82; the form is treated as an indeclinable).

#### Ṛk 2

**॥ संहितापाठः ॥**

> दि॒वक्ष॑सो धे॒नवो॒ वृष्णो॒ अश्वा॑ दे॒वीरा त॑स्थौ॒ मधु॑म॒द्वहं॑तीः ।
> ऋ॒तस्य॑ त्वा॒ सद॑सि क्षेम॒यंतं॒ पर्येका॑ चरति वर्त॒निं गौः ॥ २ ॥

> *divakṣaso dhenavo vṛṣṇo aśvā devīrā tasthau madhumadvahaṃtīḥ |*
> *ṛtasya tvā sadasi kṣemayaṃtaṃ paryekā carati vartaniṃ gauḥ ‖ 2 ‖*

*[The supplied text has the stray letters पवनः तक (*pavanaḥ taka*) between the Saṃhitā and the Pada; kept in kn.md as supplied, not part of either block.]*

**॥ पदपाठः ॥**

> दिवक्षसः धेनवः वृष्णः । आश्वाः । देवीः । आ । तस्मौ । मधुमत् । वहंतीः ।
> यतस्य । त्वा सदसि क्षेमयंतं । परि एका : चरति । वर्तनिं । गौः ॥ २ ॥

> *divakṣasaḥ dhenavaḥ vṛṣṇaḥ | āśvāḥ | devīḥ | ā | tasmau | madhumat | vahaṃtīḥ |*
> *yatasya | tvā sadasi kṣemayaṃtaṃ | pari ekā : carati | vartaniṃ | gauḥ ‖ 2 ‖*

*[Readings differ between Saṃhitā and Pada/bhāṣya: Saṃhitā तस्थौ (*tasthau*) against Pada तस्मौ (*tasmau*); Saṃhitā ऋतस्य (*ṛtasya*) against Pada यतस्य (*yatasya*); Saṃhitā वृष्णो (*vṛṣṇo*) against the Pada and bhāṣya वृष्णः (*vṛṣṇaḥ*). Kept as supplied.]*

**॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥**

> वृष्ण: कामानां वर्षितुरग्नेरश्वा वाहनरूपा रोहिता दिवक्षसो दिवं व्या-
> प वर्तमाना धेनवोऽग्नेः प्रीणयित्तो भवंति । सोयमग्निर्मधुमत् मा-
> धुर्योपेतमुदकं वहंतीर्देवीर्द्योतनशीला नदीरातस्थौ । आतिष्ठति । प्राप्नोती-
> त्यर्थः । गोशब्दो माध्यमिकवाग्वाचे माध्यमिकां वाचमिति नैरुक्ता इत्यभिधा-
> नात् । एकागौः काचिदुक्तविधा वाक् ऋतस्योदकस्य सदसि स्थानेंतरिक्षे क्षेमयंतं
> क्षेममावासमिच्छंतं वर्तनिं ज्वालाः प्रवर्तयंतं त्या हे अग्ने त्वां परिचरति ।
> सेवते ॥ दिवक्षसः । अक्षू व्याप् । दिवं व्याप्वंतीति दिवक्षसः । वक्ष शोषे । रो-
> षार्थोऽप्ययं धातुरत्र व्याप् वर्तते । दिवं वक्षंति व्याप्वंतीति । असुन्नित्यसु-
> न् प्रत्ययः । लोपो व्योर्वलीति वलोपः । कृदुत्तरपदप्रकृतिस्वरत्वं । धेनवः ।
> नुरितनुवृत् धेट इच्चेति अनुप्रत्ययः । इकारस्य गुणः । प्रत्ययस्वरः । वृष्णः ।
> वृष सेचने । कनिन्युवृषितक्षिराजीत्यादिना किनिन् ! कित्वादुदुणाभावः । नित्वादाद्युदा-
> त्तः । अश्वाः । आतू व्याप्, अशिपुषिलटिकणेत्यादिना क्विन् । नित्वादाद्युदात्तः ।
> तस्था । ष्ठा गतिनिवृत् छंदसि लुळ्ळिट्ट अति वर्तमाने लिट्, आत औ णल इत्याकारः ।
> निघातः। मधुमत् । निदित्यनुवृत् फलिपाटिनमिमनिजनामित्यादिना मनेरुप्रत्ययः ।
> तत्सन्नियोगेन नकारस्य धकारः नित्वादाद्युदात्तः । त्वा । युष्कृच्छबस्य द्वितीयैक-
> वचनांतस्य त्वामाविति त्वादेशः । अनुदात्तश्च । क्षेमयंतं । क्षि निवासगत्योः ।
> आर्तिसुसुहुसृधृक्षीत्यादिना मन् । क्षेममावासमात्मन इच्छंतं सुप आत्मनः क्यजिति
> क्यच् । क्यचि जेतीत्वं अकृत्सार्धातुकयोर्दिर्घइति दीर्घत्वं च न छंदस्सपु-
> त्रस्यॆति प्रतिषिध्यते । क्यचश्चित्वादंतोदात्तः । तदंताच्छतृप्रत्ययः । शप् । शपः
> पित्वादनुदात्तत्वं शतुश्च सार्वधातुकस्वरेण । तयोः क्यचा सहैकादेशे कृते एका-

### Printed p. 10

*[Running head, p. 10: 10 Sāyaṇabhāṣyasahitā [Maṃ. 3. A. 1. Sū. 7.]]*

> देश उदात्तेनोदात्त इत्येकादेश उदात्तः । अं । सुप्त्वादनुदात्तः । उदात्ता-
> दनुदात्तस्यॆत्यमः स्वरितत्वं । एका । इण् गतौ । इण्कापेत्यादिना कन् । तदंता-
> दाप् । नित्स्वरः । वर्तनिं । वृतु वर्तने । अंतर्भावितण्यर्थः । वृतेश्च । उ.
> २-१०७ । इत्यनि: । गुणः । व्यत्ययेनांतोदात्तत्वं । गौः । गम्लृ गतौ गमे-
> र्डोः । उ. २-६७ । इति डोप्रत्ययः । डित्वाट्टलोपः । गोतो णिदिति सुपो
> णित्वाद्वृद्धिः । प्रत्ययस्वरः ॥

> *deśa udāttenodātta ityekādeśa udāttaḥ | aṃ | suptvādanudāttaḥ | udāttā-*
> *danudāttasyètyamaḥ svaritatvaṃ | ekā | iṇ gatau | iṇkāpetyādinā kan | tadaṃtā-*
> *dāp | nitsvaraḥ | vartaniṃ | vṛtu vartane | aṃtarbhāvitaṇyarthaḥ | vṛteśca | u.*
> *2-107 | ityani: | guṇaḥ | vyatyayenāṃtodāttatvaṃ | gauḥ | gamlṛ gatau game-*
> *rḍoḥ | u. 2-67 | iti ḍopratyayaḥ | ḍitvāṭṭalopaḥ | goto ṇiditi supo*
> *ṇitvādvṛddhiḥ | pratyayasvaraḥ ‖*

*Gist (mine): Agni's red flame-horses, pervading the sky, are his milch cows; he reaches the shining, sweet-water-bearing rivers; a single voice of the firmament waits on you, Agni, who desire a dwelling in the waters' abode.* The grammatical tail of the bhāṣya (formation and accent of दिवक्षसः (*divakṣasaḥ*), धेनवः (*dhenavaḥ*), वृष्णः (*vṛṣṇaḥ*), अश्वाः (*aśvāḥ*), तस्था (*tasthā*), मधुमत् (*madhumat*), त्वा (*tvā*), क्षेमयंतं (*kṣemayaṃtaṃ*), एका (*ekā*), वर्तनिं (*vartaniṃ*), गौः (*gauḥ*), citing Uṇ. 2-107 and Uṇ. 2-67 as printed) is noted here but not explained.

**प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)**

Vṛṣṭaḥ (वृष्टः (*vṛṣṭaḥ*) as supplied; the bhāṣya has वृष्णः (*vṛṣṇaḥ*)): of Agni, the bestower of desires; aśvāḥ: the red-coloured flames, serving as vehicles; divakṣasaḥ: pervading the firmament; dhenavaḥ: they become the givers of delight to Agni; (such Agni) madhumat: water full of sweetness; vahantīḥ: carrying; devīḥ: luminous rivers; ā tasthau: reaches. (He agne: O Agni) ekā gauḥ: one (praise-form) utterance; ṛtasya: of the water; sadasi: in the abode, the firmament; kṣemayantam: seeking a dwelling; vartanim: sending out flames; tvā: you; pari carati: serves.

**भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)**

The red-coloured flames of Agni, the bestower of desired boons, serving as his vehicles, pervade even the firmament and give delight to Agni; this Agni resorts to the rivers, which carry sweet water and are luminous. Thus, O Agni, a voice in the form of praise serves you, who desire to dwell in the firmament, the abode of the waters, and who are the cause of flames (we make you known by praises).

**Printed English translation**

The sky-traversing steeds of the showerer (of benefits) are the milch cows (of Agni); as he attains the divine (rivers), bearers of sweet (water). One sacred sound glorifies you (Agni), who are desirous of repose, pecifying [sic] your flames) in the abode of the water (the firmament),

**विशेषविषयगळु (Viśeṣaviṣayagaḷu)**

वृष्णः— कामानां वर्षितु: (*vṛṣṇaḥ— kāmānāṃ varṣitu:*) (*vṛṣṇaḥ: of the showerer of desires*). The meaning is that Agni fulfils all the wishes of those who perform sacrifice.

अश्वा—वाहनरूपाः रोहिताः (*aśvā—vāhanarūpāḥ rohitāḥ*) (*aśvā: vehicle-like red ones*). The authors of the Nirukta (say):

हरी इंद्रस्य । रोहितोऽग्नेः । हरित आदित्यस्य । रासभावश्विनोः । अजाः (*harī iṃdrasya | rohito 'gneḥ | harita ādityasya | rāsabhāvaśvinoḥ | ajāḥ*)
पूष्णः । पृषश्यो मरुतां । अरुण्यो गाव उषसः । श्यावाः सवितुः । विश्व (*pūṣṇaḥ | pṛṣaśyo marutāṃ | aruṇyo gāva uṣasaḥ | śyāvāḥ savituḥ | viśva*)
रूपा बृहस्पतेः । नियुतो वायोः । (*rūpā bṛhaspateḥ | niyuto vāyoḥ |*) (Ni. 2-28)

(Gloss mine and tentative: the two bay horses are Indra's; the red one is Agni's; the green ones are the Sun's; the two asses belong to the Aśvins; the goats to Pūṣan; the spotted ones to the Maruts; the ruddy cows to Uṣas; the dark ones to Savitṛ; the many-formed ones to Bṛhaspati; the नियुत् (*niyut*) team to Vāyu. The passage is quoted as supplied, with the विश्व-रूपा (*viśva-rūpā*) split across a line end; the Special Topics of Ṛk 2 continue on the next page.)

### Printed p. 11

*[Running head, p. 11: Ṛgvedasaṃhitā 11 [3. A. 1. Va. 1.]]*

(Special Topics of Ṛk 2, continued from p. 10.) Since the vehicles of the respective deities are thus indicated, the vehicle of Agni is known to be the red one (रोहित (*rohita*)). Sāyaṇa has explained it here as रोहिताः अश्वाः (*rohitāḥ aśvāḥ*) ("the red horses").

धेनवः (*dhenavaḥ*)— अग्नेः प्रिणयित्र्यः (*agneḥ priṇayitryaḥ*) (delighters of Agni). The word धेनु (*dhenu*) is read among the twenty-eight names of speech (Ni. 2-23; the supplied text prints the abbreviation as "ಸಿ", evidently Ni., as printed). Just as words give delight in the world, the red horses, the vehicles of Agni, give delight to Agni; thus the meaning is explained (resorting to a figurative usage).

ऋतस्य (*ṛtasya*)—उदकस्य (*udakasya*). The word ऋत (*ṛta*) is read among the names of water (Ni. 2-24) and also in the sense of a particular time. Here the meaning is explained as water.

क्षेमयंतं (*kṣemayaṃtaṃ*) — क्षेमं आवासमिच्छंतं (*kṣemaṃ āvāsamicchaṃtaṃ*). The meaning is that Agni, imagining (and pervading the water to make) a pleasant dwelling-place in the rivers that bear sweet (tasty) waters, abides there. By this both the Aurva fire (the submarine fire) and the lightning-fire that is in the midst of the clouds are made known.

गौः (*gauḥ*)—माध्यमिका वाक् (*mādhyamikā vāk*) (the speech of the firmament). The word गो (*go*) is stated among the twenty-one names of the earth (Ni. 2-5). The Nirukta sentence गौरिति पृथिव्या नामधेयं (*gauriti pṛthivyā nāmadheyaṃ*) explains this meaning. अथापि पशुनामेह भवत्वेतस्मादेव (*athāpi paśunāmeha bhavatvetasmādeva*): since the word गौः (*gauḥ*) is derived from a root (given in the supplied text as गाज् गत् (*gāj gat*)), it conveys the meaning "animal"; the meaning is cows.

In the Ṛk

सुपर्णं वस्ते मृगो अस्या दंतो गोभिस्सन्नद्धा पतति प्रसूता यत्रा नरः सं च वि च द्रवंति तत्रास्मभ्यमिषवः शर्म यंसन् (*suparṇaṃ vaste mṛgo asyā daṃto gobhissannaddhā patati prasūtā yatrā naraḥ saṃ ca vi ca dravaṃti tatrāsmabhyamiṣavaḥ śarma yaṃsan*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 6-75-11)

it is described that the word गो (*go*) means sinew and phlegm.

In the Ṛk

वृक्षेवृक्षे नियता मीमयद्दौस्ततौ वयः प्र पर्ता पूरुषादः । अथेदं विश्वं भुवनं भयात इंद्राय सुन्वदृषये च शिक्षत् (*vṛkṣevṛkṣe niyatā mīmayaddaustatau vayaḥ pra partā pūruṣādaḥ | athedaṃ viśvaṃ bhuvanaṃ bhayāta iṃdrāya sunvadṛṣaye ca śikṣat*) (the supplied text prints the abbreviation as "ಮಿ. ಸಂ-", evidently Ṛ. Saṃ., as printed: 10-27-22)

the meaning "bowstring" (ज्या (*jyā*)) is given for the word गो (*go*).

In

उतादः परुषे गवि सूरश्चक्रं हिरण्ययं । नैरयद्रथीतमः (*utādaḥ paruṣe gavi sūraścakraṃ hiraṇyayaṃ | nairayadrathītamaḥ*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 6-56-3)

since the meaning is given as आदित्योऽपि गौरुच्यते (*ādityo 'pi gaurucyate*) ("the sun too is called gauḥ"), the authors of the Nirukta say that the sun also is a meaning of the word गो (*go*).

In

ता नां वास्तून्युष्मसि गमध्यै यत्र गावो भूरिशृंगा अयासः । आत्राह तदुरुगायस्य वृष्टः परमं पदमव भाति भूरि (*tā nāṃ vāstūnyuṣmasi gamadhyai yatra gāvo bhūriśṛṃgā ayāsaḥ | ātrāha tadurugāyasya vṛṣṭaḥ paramaṃ padamava bhāti bhūri*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-154-6)

the meaning "ray" is given for the word गो (*go*) with the words सर्वॆऽपि रश्मयः गाव उच्यंते (*sarvè 'pi raśmayaḥ gāva ucyaṃte*) ("all the rays are called gāvaḥ"). Since Durgācārya has commented सुषुम्मो रश्मिरेक एव गौः इति उच्यते (*suṣummo raśmireka eva gauḥ iti ucyate*) ("the one ray Suṣumna alone is called gauḥ"; सुषुम्मो (*suṣummo*) as supplied), the ray of the Suṣumnā (nāḍī) etc. is also a meaning of the word गो (*go*). The word गो (*go*) has the earth, cows,

### Printed p. 12

*[Running head, p. 12: 12 Sāyaṇabhāṣyasahitā [Maṃ. 3. A. 1. Sū. 7.]]*

sinew, phlegm, Maurvī (bowstring), the sun, rays, and the ray of the Suṣumnā nāḍī, among many meanings. Sāyaṇa, having commented

गोशब्दो माध्यमिकवाग्वाची माध्यमिकां वाचमिति नैरुक्ता इत्यभिधानात् । एका गौः काचिदुक्तविधा वाक् वर्तनिं ज्वालाः प्रवर्तयंतं त्वां परिचरिति सेवते (*gośabdo mādhyamikavāgvācī mādhyamikāṃ vācamiti nairuktā ityabhidhānāt | ekā gauḥ kāciduktavidhā vāk vartaniṃ jvālāḥ pravartayaṃtaṃ tvāṃ paricariti sevate*)

has made the meaning that the speech of the firmament serves Agni. In regard to the utterance of speech by living beings, the Vaiśvānara fire, impelled by the vital breath (prāṇa-vāyu), is the producer of the manifestation of speech: the meaning is thus described in an involved manner.

#### Ṛk 3

**॥ संहितापाठः ॥**

> आ सी॑मरोहत्तु॒यमा॒ भवं॑तीः॒ पति॑श्विकि॒त्वा न्र॑यि॒विद्र॑यी॒णां ।
> प्र नील॑पृष्ठो अत॒सस्य॑ धा॒सेस्ता अ॑वासयत्पुरु॒धप्र॑तीकः ॥ ३ ॥

**॥ पदपाठः ॥**

> आ । सीं । अरोहत् । सुयमाः : भवंतीः । पति: चिकित्सान् । रयिऽवित् ।
> रयीशं
> प्र । नीलसृष्टः । अतसस्य । धासेः । ताः ' आवासयत् । पुरुधप्रतीकः । ३ ॥

> *ā | sīṃ | arohat | suyamāḥ : bhavaṃtīḥ | pati: cikitsān | rayi 'vit |*
> *rayīśaṃ*
> *pra | nīlasṛṣṭaḥ | atasasya | dhāseḥ | tāḥ ' āvāsayat | purudhapratīkaḥ | 3 ‖*

*Note on the supplied Pada: it reads चिकित्सान् (*cikitsān*) and नीलसृष्टः (*nīlasṛṣṭaḥ*) and has the isolated word रयीशं (*rayīśaṃ*) after the first line, while the bhāṣya below reads चिकित्वान् (*cikitvān*) and नीलपृष्ठः (*nīlapṛṣṭhaḥ*); the Saṃhitā has न्रयिविद् (*nrayivid*) run together. Left as supplied (probable machine-reading slips; for the user's decision).*

**॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥**

> रयीणां रयिवित् धानानां मध्यॆ विशिष्टधनस्य लंभयिता चिकित्वान् ज्ञान-
> वान् पतिरश्वानां पतिरग्निः सीं सर्वतः सुयमाः सुखेन नियंतुं शक्या भवंतीर्या
> वडवा आरोहत् आरोहयति स्म नीलपृष्ठः पुरुधप्रतीको बहुधाविक्षिप्तांगः
> सोऽग्निरतसस्य सततगमनस्य धासेः पोषणार्थं ता अश्वाः प्रावासयत् ! प्रकर्षण
> वासयति स्म । निवासेन श्रमेऽपनीते सततगमनं पोषयितुं शक्यमित्यर्थः ॥ सीं ।
> जादयोऽनुदात्ता इत्यनुदात्तः, अरोहत् रुह प्रादुर्भावे । लजः रूपं ।
> निघातः सुयमाः । यम उपरमे ! आस्माच्छक्यार्थ ईषद्दु:सुष्टिति खल् । कृदु-
> त्तरपदप्रकृतिस्वरत्वं। पतिः । पा रक्षणे : पातेर्डतिरिति डित्ता ट्टलोपः प्रत्ययस्वरः ।
> चिकित्वान् । चिति संज्ञाने । क्सस् रूपं । रयिवित् । विद्य लाभे क्सिप् । रयीणां ।
> नामन्यतरस्यामिति विभक्तेरुदात्तत्वं । नीलपृष्ठः । नीलशब्दो वृषादित्वादाद्युदात्तः ।
> बहुव्रीहौ पूर्वपदप्रकृतिस्वरत्वं । आतसस्य । अत सातत्यगमन इत्यस्मादत्यविचमित-
> मिनमिरभिलभिनभितपिपतिपनिपणिमहिभ्यः । उ-११७ । इत्यसच् । चित्सरः । आवासयत् ।
> वस निवासे । हेतुमतीति णिच् । लुळ्ल्यज्श्वडित्यडागमः . तिङ्ङतिज इति निघातः ।

> *rayīṇāṃ rayivit dhānānāṃ madhyè viśiṣṭadhanasya laṃbhayitā cikitvān jñāna-*
> *vān patiraśvānāṃ patiragniḥ sīṃ sarvataḥ suyamāḥ sukhena niyaṃtuṃ śakyā bhavaṃtīryā*
> *vaḍavā ārohat ārohayati sma nīlapṛṣṭhaḥ purudhapratīko bahudhāvikṣiptāṃgaḥ*
> *so 'gniratasasya satatagamanasya dhāseḥ poṣaṇārthaṃ tā aśvāḥ prāvāsayat ! prakarṣaṇa*
> *vāsayati sma | nivāsena śrame 'panīte satatagamanaṃ poṣayituṃ śakyamityarthaḥ ‖ sīṃ |*
> *jādayo 'nudāttā ityanudāttaḥ, arohat ruha prādurbhāve | lajaḥ rūpaṃ |*
> *nighātaḥ suyamāḥ | yama uparame ! āsmācchakyārtha īṣaddu:suṣṭiti khal | kṛdu-*
> *ttarapadaprakṛtisvaratvaṃ| patiḥ | pā rakṣaṇe : pāterḍatiriti ḍittā ṭṭalopaḥ pratyayasvaraḥ |*
> *cikitvān | citi saṃjñāne | ksas rūpaṃ | rayivit | vidya lābhe ksip | rayīṇāṃ |*
> *nāmanyatarasyāmiti vibhakterudāttatvaṃ | nīlapṛṣṭhaḥ | nīlaśabdo vṛṣāditvādādyudāttaḥ |*
> *bahuvrīhau pūrvapadaprakṛtisvaratvaṃ | ātasasya | ata sātatyagamana ityasmādatyavicamita-*
> *minamirabhilabhinabhitapipatipanipaṇimahibhyaḥ | u-117 | ityasac | citsaraḥ | āvāsayat |*
> *vasa nivāse | hetumatīti ṇic | luḷlyajśvaḍityaḍāgamaḥ . tiṅṅatija iti nighātaḥ |*

*Gist (mine): Agni, the finder of the best wealth among riches and the knowing lord of horses, mounts the easily controlled mares; the blue-backed, many-formed one makes them rest to nourish his ceaseless going.* The grammatical tail of the bhāṣya (formation and accent of सीं (*sīṃ*), अरोहत् (*arohat*), सुयमाः (*suyamāḥ*), पतिः (*patiḥ*), चिकित्वान् (*cikitvān*), रयिवित् (*rayivit*), रयीणां (*rayīṇāṃ*), नीलपृष्ठः (*nīlapṛṣṭhaḥ*), आतसस्य (*ātasasya*), आवासयत् (*āvāsayat*), citing Uṇ. 117 as printed) is noted here but not explained. The Pratipadārtha and the rest of Ṛk 3 follow on the next page.

### Printed p. 15

*[Running head, p. 15: [Maṃ. 3. A. 1. Va. 1.] Ṛgvedasaṃhitā 15]*

The bhāṣya of Ṛk 3 concludes (its last lines, on the formation of पुरुधप्रतीकः (*purudhapratīkaḥ*)):

> पुरुधप्रतीकः । पुरुशब्द इह संख्यावाची । संख्याया विधार्थे धेति धाप्रत्ययः ।
> बहुव्रीहौ पूर्वपदप्रकृतिस्वरत्वं । ह्रस्वश्चांदसः ॥

**प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)**

rayīṇām — (in the midst) of riches; rayivit — the one who supplies the best wealth; cikitvān — the wise one; patiḥ — the lord of horses, Agni; sīm — in every way; suyamāḥ — easily controlled; bhavantīḥ (yāḥ) — you who are mares (O horses), whom; ā arohat — he mounts; tāḥ — those horses; nīlapṛṣṭhaḥ — he whose back is dark blue; purudhapratīkaḥ — he whose limbs are scattered in many ways (Agni); atasasya — of the ceaseless going; dhāseḥ — for the nourishment (of it); pra āvāsayat — makes them rest.

(Printed as supplied: शीं (*śīṃ*) for sīm, पुरुषप्रतीकः (*puruṣapratīkaḥ*) for purudhapratīkaḥ, अतसस्य- सतत (*atasasya- satata*) with a stray hyphen; left as supplied.)

**भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)**

O mares, the vehicles of Agni! Agni, who bestows the most excellent wealth, who is wise, and who is the lord of horses, mounts you in every way, you who are easily controlled. But that dark-backed Agni, of scattered limbs, in order that the nourishment needed for the unwearied going from you may reach you, in advance makes you rest.

**Printed English**

Their lord has mounted on his well-trained mares; the sagacious selector of treasures, the blackbacked, manylimbed (Agni) has granted them a place of rest for the cherising [sic] of (their) rapid speed.

**विशेषविषयगळु (Viśeṣaviṣayagaḷu)**

रयीणां रयिवित् (*rayīṇāṃ rayivit*) — धनानां मध्यॆ विशिष्टधनस्य लंभयिता (*dhanānāṃ madhyè viśiṣṭadhanasya laṃbhayitā*). The word *rayi* denotes water (Ni. 2-24) and also wealth (Ni. 3-9); and since it is said, at "nū ca purā ca sadanaṃ rayīṇām", "*rayi* is a name for wealth, from *rā*, in the sense of giving" (Ni. 4-17), it conveys the meaning wealth. The meaning is that Agni obtains for those who perform the sacrifice all that is known as wealth.

पुरुधप्रतीक: (*purudhapratīka:*) — पुरुशब्द इह संख्यावाची । बहुधा विक्षिप्तांगः (*puruśabda iha saṃkhyāvācī | bahudhā vikṣiptāṃgaḥ*). It is explained that Agni pervades all places.

धासेः ताः प्र अवासयत् (*dhāseḥ tāḥ pra avāsayat*) — पोषणार्थं आश्वा: प्रकर्षेण वासयेति स्म (*poṣaṇārthaṃ āśvā: prakarṣeṇa vāsayeti sma*). Because Agni is the lord of the horses, he is described as the helper by whom the horses (for their nourishment) may grow,

### Printed p. 16

*[Running head, p. 16: 16 Sāyaṇabhāṣyasahitā [Maṃ. 3. A. 1. Sū. 7.]]*

and, having removed their weariness, may move about always, and may also be at ease.

चिकित्त्वा (*cikittvā*) (as printed; the bhāṣya has चिकित्वान् (*cikitvān*)) — knowing. The meaning is that Agni has spiritual knowledge.

सीं (*sīṃ*) — everywhere, in all regions. The authors of the Nirukta, on the word सीं (*sīṃ*), say सीमिति परिग्रहार्थियो वा पादपूरणे वा (*sīmiti parigrahārthiyo vā pādapūraṇe vā*): the particle *sīm* is used in the sense of "taking in all round" and also as a verse-filler. Then, at विशीमतः सुरुचो-वेन आवः व्यवृणोत् सर्वत आदित्यः (*viśīmataḥ suruco-vena āvaḥ vyavṛṇot sarvata ādityaḥ*) (as supplied), they explain *sīm* as सर्वतः (*sarvataḥ*) (on all sides), and सीम्न: सीमतः सीमतो मर्यादातः सीमा मर्यादा (*sīmna: sīmataḥ sīmato maryādātaḥ sīmā maryādā*) as limit, boundary.

**Vyākaraṇa-prakriyā (grammar page, noted briefly)**

Words treated: सीम् (*sīm*) (sīm; read among the *cādi* particles, hence unaccented by चादयोऽनुदात्ताः (*cādayo 'nudāttāḥ*), Phiṭ. Sū. 84), अरोहत् (*arohat*) (root *ruh*; laṅ third singular; all-unaccented after a non-finite form), सुयमाः (*suyamāḥ*) (root *yam*; khal ending by ईषद्दुः सुषु (*īṣadduḥ suṣu*), Pā. 3-3-126; accent of the compound by the *kṛdutta-rapada* rule), पतिः (*patiḥ*) (root *pā*; ḍati ending by पातेर्दतिः (*pāterdatiḥ*), Uṇ. 4-497 as printed; loss of the ṭi; ending accented first), चिकित्वान् (*cikitvān*) (root *kit*; kvasu replacing liṭ by Pā. 3-2-107; reduplication; ending-accent), रयिवित् (*rayivit*) (root *vid*; kvip by Rā. Sū. 3-2-76 [sic, as printed]), रयीणाम् (*rayīṇām*) (genitive plural; ending accented by नामन्यतरस्याम् (*nāmanyatarasyām*), Pā. 6-1-177), नीलपृष्ठः (*nīlapṛṣṭhaḥ*) (*nīla* is in the *vṛṣādi* list, hence initial accent by वृषादीनांच (*vṛṣādīnāṃca*), Pā. 6-1-213; accent of the first member in the bahuvrīhi by बहुव्रीहौ प्रकृत्या पूर्वपदम् (*bahuvrīhau prakṛtyā pūrvapadam*), 6-2-1), दासेः (*dāseḥ*) (as printed; said to be explained already in the first Ṛk).

### Printed p. 17

*[Running head, p. 17: [Maṃ. 3. A. 1. Va. 1.] Ṛgvedasaṃhitā 17]*

Grammar page concluded, noted briefly: अतसस्य (*atasasya*) (root *at*, in the sense of continuous going; asac ending by the sūtra beginning अत्यविचमित… (*atyavicamita…*), Uṇ. 3-397 as printed; final accent by चितः (*citaḥ*), Pā. 6-1-163; genitive singular), अवासयत् (*avāsayat*) (root *vas* "to dwell"; ṇic by हेतुमतिच (*hetumatica*), Pā. 3-1-26; vṛddhi of the penultimate by अतउपधायाः (*ataupadhāyāḥ*), Pā. 7-2-116; laṅ third singular; all-unaccented after a non-finite form), पुरुधप्रतीकः (*purudhapratīkaḥ*) (*puru* here denotes number, so dhā ending in the sense of manner by संख्याया-विधार्थे धा (*saṃkhyāyā-vidhārthe dhā*), Pā. 5-3-42; first-member accent in the bahuvrīhi, *purudhā* being end-accented by its ending; shortening of the first member is Vedic).

#### Ṛk 4

**॥ संहितापाठः ॥**

> महि॑ त्वा॒ष्ट्रमू॒र्जयं॑तीरजु॒र्यं स्त॑भू॒यमा॑नं व॒हतो॑
> वहंति ।
> व्यंगे॑भिर्दिद्युता॒नः स॒धस्थ॒ एका॑मिव॒ रोद॑सी॒ आ वि॑वेश - ॥ ४ ॥

**॥ पदपाठः ॥**

> महि । त्वाष्टं । ऊर्जयंतीः । अजुर्यं । स्तभुयमानं । वहतः । वहंति ।
> वि । अंगेभिः । दिद्युतानः । सधस्थे । एकांइव । रोदसी इति । आ । विवेश ॥ ४ ॥

**॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥**

> उर्जयंतीरबलं बलिनं कुर्वंत्यो वहतो वहनपरा नद्यो महि
> महांतं त्वाष्ट्रं त्वष्टुः सुतमजुर्यं जरयितुमनर्हं स्तभूयमानं
> लोकानां स्तंभनं धारणमिच्छंतमग्निं वहंति । धारयंति । सधस्थेऽपां समीपेऽ-
> गेभिरवयवैर्दिद्युतानो दीप्यमानः सन् अग्नी रोदसी द्यावापृथिव्यौ व्याविवेश ।
> विविधं प्रविष्टवान् । रोदस्योर्दृष्टांतः । एकामिव । यथा पुमानेकां स्त्रियं
> प्रविशति । तद्वत् ॥ त्वाष्ट्रं । त्वष्टुरपत्यमित्यर्थे तस्यापत्यमित्यण् । प्रत्ययस्वरः ।
> स्तभूयमानं । स्तभु स्तंभनं । तदिच्छंतं । सुप आत्मनः क्यच् । अकृत्सार्वधातु-
> कयोरिति दीर्घः । तदंताद्व्यत्ययेन शानच् । अकारांतत्पादाने मुक् । शानचो

> *urjayaṃtīrabalaṃ balinaṃ kurvaṃtyo vahato vahanaparā nadyo mahi*
> *mahāṃtaṃ tvāṣṭraṃ tvaṣṭuḥ sutamajuryaṃ jarayitumanarhaṃ stabhūyamānaṃ*
> *lokānāṃ staṃbhanaṃ dhāraṇamicchaṃtamagniṃ vahaṃti | dhārayaṃti | sadhasthe 'pāṃ samīpe '-*
> *gebhiravayavairdidyutāno dīpyamānaḥ san agnī rodasī dyāvāpṛthivyau vyāviveśa |*
> *vividhaṃ praviṣṭavān | rodasyordṛṣṭāṃtaḥ | ekāmiva | yathā pumānekāṃ striyaṃ*
> *praviśati | tadvat ‖ tvāṣṭraṃ | tvaṣṭurapatyamityarthe tasyāpatyamityaṇ | pratyayasvaraḥ |*
> *stabhūyamānaṃ | stabhu staṃbhanaṃ | tadicchaṃtaṃ | supa ātmanaḥ kyac | akṛtsārvadhātu-*
> *kayoriti dīrghaḥ | tadaṃtādvyatyayena śānac | akārāṃtatpādāne muk | śānaco*
> 3

*Gist (mine): the mighty rivers, making the strengthless strong, carry Agni, son of Tvaṣṭṛ, who wishes to support the worlds; blazing with his limbs by the water's side, he entered heaven and earth as a man enters a woman.* The bhāṣya is cut off at the end of printed p. 17 in the middle of its grammatical tail (on त्वाष्ट्रं (*tvāṣṭraṃ*) and स्तभूयमानं (*stabhūyamānaṃ*)); the stray character "3" at its end in the supplied text is left as supplied. The Pratipadārtha and the rest of Ṛk 4 follow on the next page.

### Printed p. 18

*[Running head, p. 18: 18 Sāyaṇabhāṣyasahitā [Maṃ. 1. A. 1. Sū. 7.] (as supplied; the other pages read Maṃ. 3)]*

**Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya** (continued from p. 17; the grammatical tail on the words दिद्युतानः (*didyutānaḥ*), एकामिव (*ekāmiva*) and विवेश (*viveśa*), not explained)

> लसार्वधातुकस्वरेणानुदात्तत्वे कृते क्यजंताद्दातुस्वरः शिष्यते । अंगेभिः । बहुलं
> छंदसीति भिस ऐसादेशाभावः । बहुवचने झल्यॆदित्येत्वं। दिद्युतानः । द्युत दीप्तौ ।
> छंदसि लिट् । तस्य कावजादेशः । द्विर्वचनं, हलादिशेषे कृते द्यु इत्यस्याभ्यासस्य
> द्युतिस्वाप्योः संप्रसारणं । पा. ७-४ ६७ । इति संप्रसारणं । संप्रसारणाच्चेति
> पूर्वरूपत्वं । चित्वादंतोदात्तत्वं । एकामिव । इवेन विभक्त्यलोपः पूर्व-
> पदप्रकृतिस्वरत्वं चेति वचनात्पूर्वपदप्रकृतिस्वरः । विवेश विश प्रवेशन इत्यस्माल्लिट
> रूपं । निघातः ॥

**प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)**

Ūrjayantīḥ — those that make the strengthless strong; vahataḥ — rivers that are intent on carrying; mahi — the great one; tvāṣṭram — the son of Tvaṣṭṛ; ajuryam — one not liable to old age; stabhūyamānam — one who wishes to support the world: Agni; vahanti — they bear; sadhasthe — near the water; aṅgebhiḥ — (together) with his limbs; didyutānaḥ — Agni, shining; rodasī — heaven and earth; ekām iva — (as a man enters) a single woman (in many forms); vi ā viveśa — enters in manifold forms.

**भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)**

The rivers, which give strength to the weak and are intent on carrying, bear Agni, the great one, the son of Tvaṣṭṛ, not liable to old age, who wishes to support the world. Thus, shining with all his limbs kindled near the water, Agni enters heaven and earth in many forms, as a man approaches a single woman in different forms.

**Printed English**

The flowing (rivers) invigorating him, bear along the great son of Twashtri, the undecaying upholder (of the world), radiant with various forms in the vicinity (of the firmament): Agni is associated with heaven and earth, as (a husband with) one only wife.

**विशेषविषयगळु (Viśeṣaviṣayagaḷu)**

ऊर्जयंतीः (*ūrjayaṃtīḥ*) — अबलं बलिनं कुर्वत्यः (*abalaṃ balinaṃ kurvatyaḥ*) (making the strengthless strong). The word ऊर्क् (*ūrk*) (*ūrk*) is read among the names of food (Ni. 3-9). The word *ūrja* is also used in the sense of strength. For example—

### Printed p. 19

*[Running head, p. 19: [A. 3. A. 1. Va. 1.] Ṛgvedasaṃhitā 19 (as supplied)]*

देवी ऊर्जाहुती इषमूर्जमन्यावक्षत् सद्धिं सपीतिमन्या नवेन पूर्वं दयमानाः स्याम पुराणेन नवं तामूर्जमूर्जाहुती ऊर्जयमाने आधातां वसुवने वसुधेयस्य वीतां यज (*devī ūrjāhutī iṣamūrjamanyāvakṣat saddhiṃ sapītimanyā navena pūrvaṃ dayamānāḥ syāma purāṇena navaṃ tāmūrjamūrjāhutī ūrjayamāne ādhātāṃ vasuvane vasudheyasya vītāṃ yaja*) (Yaj. Vā. Saṃ. 28-16). Durgācārya has commented that in this mantra the word *ūrja* is used in the sense of strength. Sāyaṇa has explained the meaning as: the rivers produce strength even for those who have no strength.

वहतः स्तभूयामानं वहंति (*vahataḥ stabhūyāmānaṃ vahaṃti*) — वहनपराः नद्यः लोकानां धारणमिच्छंतमग्निं धारयंति (*vahanaparāḥ nadyaḥ lokānāṃ dhāraṇamicchaṃtamagniṃ dhārayaṃti*): the rivers, flowing ever with abundant currents, pervade all the worlds and bear Agni, who desires to protect everything. By the Śruti अग्नेरापः (*agnerāpaḥ*) ("the waters are from Agni"), the waters originate from Agni; therefore Agni's entry is in the waters. This is the meaning.

अजुर्यं-जरयितुमनॆर्हं (*ajuryaṃ-jarayitumanèrhaṃ*) (as supplied) — the meaning is that Agni is not fit to be consumed (quenched) by anyone.

रोदसी एकामिव आ विवेश (*rodasī ekāmiva ā viveśa*) — द्यावापृथिव्यॆ विविधं प्रविष्टर्वा यथा पुर्मा एकां स्त्रीयं तद्वत् (*dyāvāpṛthivyè vividhaṃ praviṣṭarvā yathā purmā ekāṃ strīyaṃ tadvat*) (as supplied): Agni, having entered the waters, has pervaded heaven and earth in many ways. Just as in the world a man, attached to a woman and in many ways becoming the object of her affection, pervades her mind, so the entry of Agni into heaven and earth is stated here by way of simile. The word रोदसी (*rodasī*) is read among the twenty-four names of heaven and earth (Ni. 3-22). And—

रथं नु मारुतं वयं श्रवस्युमा हुनामहे । आ यस्मिन् तस्थौ सुरहानिबिभ्रती सचा मरुत्सु रोदसी ॥ (*rathaṃ nu mārutaṃ vayaṃ śravasyumā hunāmahe | ā yasmin tasthau surahānibibhratī sacā marutsu rodasī ‖*) (Ma. Saṃ. 5-6-8, as printed).

Yāska, citing this Ṛk, explains here that रोदसी (*rodasī*) means the wife of Rudra; Durgācārya explains it as the manifestation (विभूतिः (*vibhūtiḥ*)) of Rudra.

**Vyākaraṇa-prakriyā (grammar page, noted briefly)**

Words treated: त्वाष्ट्रम् (*tvāṣṭram*) (aṇ ending in the sense of offspring by तस्यापत्यम् (*tasyāpatyam*), Pā. Sū. 4-1-92; initial vṛddhi by तद्धितेष्वचामादेः (*taddhiteṣvacāmādeḥ*); yaṇ substitution; final accent by the ending; accusative singular), ऊर्जयंतीः (*ūrjayaṃtīḥ*) (root *ūrj* in the sense of strength and life; śatṛ after the ṇijanta; ṅīp in the feminine; ṇic accent remains; lengthening by the sūtra सुपांसुलुक्… (*supāṃsuluk…*)).

### Printed p. 20

*[Running head, p. 20: 20 Sāyaṇabhāṣyasahitā [Maṃ. 3. A. 1. Sū. 7.]]*

#### Ṛk 5

**॥ संहितापाठः ॥**

> जा॒नंति॒ वृष्णो॑ अरु॒षस्य॒ शेव॑मु॒त ब्र॒धन्स्य॒ शास॑ने रणंति ।
> दि॒वो॒रुच॑ सु॒रुचो॒ रोच॑माना॒ इळा॒ येषां॒ गण्या॒ माहि॑ना॒
> गीः ॥ ५ ॥

**॥ पदपाठः ॥**

> जानंति । वृष्णः । अरुषस्य । शेवं । उत । ब्रधन्स्य । शासने । रणंति ।
> दिवरुचः । सुरुचः । रोचमानाः । इळा । येषां । गण्या । माहिना । गीः ॥५ ॥

**॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥**

*Gist (mine): people know the comfort of Agni, the showerer, free of enemies; they delight under his command; those whose praise of him is honoured become radiant light-givers of heaven.* The bhāṣya (Sanskrit, not rendered) continues with its grammatical tail on जानंति (*jānaṃti*), अरुषस्य (*aruṣasya*), शेवं (*śevaṃ*), शासने (*śāsane*), दिवोरुचः (*divoruchaḥ*), सुरुचः (*suruchaḥ*), इळा (*iḷā*), गण्या (*gaṇyā*), माहिना (*māhinā*) and गीः (*gīḥ*), not explained; it also cites a mantra (ma 1-60-2, as printed).

> वृष्णः कामानां वर्षितुररुषस्य रुषा हिंसकाः । तद्रहितस्य । शत्रुराहित्येन
> रोचमानस्येत्यर्थः ' तथाविधस्याग्नेः शेवमाश्रयविषयं सुखं जना जानंति । उत
> अपि च जानंतस्ते ब्रध्नस्य महतोऽग्नेः शासन आज्ञायां सर्वे जना रणंति ।
> रमंते । तथा च मंत्रः । म १-६०-२ । अस्य शासुरुभयासः सचंते हविष्मंत
> उशिजो ये च मर्ता इति अपि च । एषां मनुष्याणामग्नि विषया माहिना मह-

> रोचकाः सुरुचः शोभनदीप्य यो रोचमाना देदीप्यमाना भवंति ॥ जानंति ।
> ज्ञा अवशोधने । क्रादित्वात्तस्य ज्ञा जनोर्जॆति जावेशः । प्रत्ययस्वरः । अरुषस्य ।
> रुष रिष हिंसार्थाः । रोषंतीति रुषा हिंसकाः : इगुपधज्ञॆति कः । न संति रुषा
> यस्यासावरुषः । नञ्सुभ्यामित्युत्तरपदांतोदात्तत्वं । शेवं । शीज् स्वप्ने ।

> *vṛṣṇaḥ kāmānāṃ varṣituraruṣasya ruṣā hiṃsakāḥ | tadrahitasya | śatrurāhityena*

> *yasyāsāvaruṣaḥ | nañsubhyāmityuttarapadāṃtodāttatvaṃ | śevaṃ | śīj svapne |*
> *iṇśījñyāṃ vanniti van , nitsarvaḥ | śāsane śāsu anuśiṣṭau | āsmādbhāve lyuṭ |*
> *liṭīti pratyayātpūrvaskodāttatvaṃ raṇaṃti , nighātaḥ | divorucaḥ | ruca dīptau |*

> *saṃkhyāne | svārthe ṇyaṃtādaco yat | yato 'nāva ityādyudāttatvaṃ | māhinā |*
