### Printed p. 22

*[Running head, p. 22: 22 Sāyaṇabhāṣyasahitā [Maṃ. 3. A. 1. Sū. 7.] (as supplied; other pages read Maṃ. 1)]*

**प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)**

Vṛṣṇaḥ — of the one who showers desires; aruṣasya — of the one without enemies (and therefore shining), Agni; śevam — the comfort that has him as refuge; jānanti — (people) come to know; uta — and (those people who know that comfort); bradhnasya — of Agni, of great majesty; śāsane — under the rule; raṇanti — rejoice (and); yeṣām — whichever men; māhinā — greatness-possessing; iḷā gīḥ — (Agni's) speech in the form of praise; gaṇyā — worthy of honour; te — they; divorucaḥ — (dear to heaven) those who bring heaven into light; surucaḥ — of fine brilliance; rocamānāḥ — shining brightly (become).

**भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)**

People come to know the comfort that arises from taking refuge in Agni, who grants what is wished for and has no enemies; coming under the rule of that Agni of great majesty, they live happily. Those who regard as worthy of honour the praise relating to Agni, full of great majesty, become exceedingly radiant and guides toward the attainment of heaven.

**Printed English**

Men comprehend the service of the uninjurable showerer (of benefits) and exult in the commands of the mighty (AGNI) : their frequent and earnest hymns of praise, bright and radiant, are illuminating heaven.

**विशेषविषयगळु (Viśeṣaviṣayagaḷu)**

अरुषस्य (*aruṣasya*) — रोषंतीति रुषा हिंसकाः (*roṣaṃtīti ruṣā hiṃsakāḥ*) : the word *ruṣāḥ*, formed from the root *ruṣ*, which conveys "to injure", is explained as "those who injure"; since there are no such injuring enemies, Agni shines. The word *aruṣam* is read among the names of form (Ni. 3-13). Sāyaṇa has explained the meaning as "one free of those who injure".

शेवं (*śevaṃ*) — आश्रयविषयं सुखं (*āśrayaviṣayaṃ sukhaṃ*): the word *śeva* is read among the names of happiness (Ni. 3-13). Nirukta (10-17) says: शेव इति सुखनाम शिष्यतेर्वकारो नामकरणो अंतस्थांतरोपलिंगी विभाषितगुणः शिवमित्यप्यस्य भवति (*śeva iti sukhanāma śiṣyatervakāro nāmakaraṇo aṃtasthāṃtaropaliṃgī vibhāṣitaguṇaḥ śivamityapyasya bhavati*) (the text is given as supplied and is not rendered). The word is formed from the root *śiṣ*: in place of the *ṣ* of *śiṣ* a *v* comes, guṇa follows, and the form is *śeva*. The meaning is that those who take refuge in Agni know the comfort arising from that refuge.

ब्रध्नस्य शासने रणंति (*bradhnasya śāsane raṇaṃti*) — महतो अग्नेः आज्ञायां सर्वॆ जनाः रमंते (*mahato agneḥ ājñāyāṃ sarvè janāḥ ramaṃte*). The word *bradhna* is read among the names of the horse (Ni. 2-27); it is also read among the names of the great (Ni. 3-13)

### Printed p. 23

*[Running head, p. 23: A. 3. A. 1. Va. 1. Ṛgvedasaṃhitā 23 (as supplied; "Ā." for "Maṃ.")]*

(the passage continues from p. 22) Sāyaṇa explains the meaning thus: all people who follow the command of Agni, filled with a greatness fit to be honoured by all, live happily; and he describes this matter as also established by the Ṛk

अस्य शासुरुभयासस्सचंते हविष्मंत उशिजो ये च मर्ताः (*asya śāsurubhayāsassacaṃte haviṣmaṃta uśijo ye ca martāḥ*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-60-2).

येषां माहिना इळा गीः (*yeṣāṃ māhinā iḷā gīḥ*) — येषां मनुष्याणां अग्नि विषया महती स्तूतिरूपा वाक्, ते गण्याः (*yeṣāṃ manuṣyāṇāṃ agni viṣayā mahatī stūtirūpā vāk, te gaṇyāḥ*): those who recite the praise that is full of the greatness (found in Agni) are honoured, make the heaven-world shine and have abundant brilliance; thus the glory of Agni is revealed. The word *iḷā* is read among the names of the earth (Ni. 2-5), of speech (Ni. 2-23) and of food (Ni. 3-9).

अभि न इळा यूथस्य मातास्मन्नदीभिरुर्वशी ना गृणातु (*abhi na iḷā yūthasya mātāsmannadībhirurvaśī nā gṛṇātu*) (Mi. Saṃ. 5-41-19, as printed): here *iḷā* (or *iḍā*) is explained as a deity of the middle region. Sāyaṇa has explained it as speech in the form of praise.

**Vyākaraṇa-prakriyā (grammar page, noted briefly)**

Words treated: जानंति (*jānaṃti*) (root *jñā* "to know", kryādi, laṭ third plural; śnā by Pā. Sū. 3-1-81; substitution of *jā* for the root by Pā. Sū. 7-3-79; loss of the ā of the vikaraṇa by Pā. Sū. 6-4-112; being at the start of a pāda, no nighāta accent, the suffix accent remains), अरुषस्य (*aruṣasya*) (root *ruṣ/riṣ* "to injure"; ka suffix by Pā. Sū. 3-1-135; negative compound, final accent of the latter member by *nañsubhyām*, Pā. Sū. 6-2-172; genitive singular), शेवं (*śevaṃ*) (root *śīṅ*, "to sleep"; van suffix, Uṇ. Sū. 1-150; guṇa; initial accent; accusative singular), शासने (*śāsane*) (root *śās* "to instruct", adādi; lyuṭ in the sense of the action; *ana* by Pā. Sū. 7-1-1; accent before the suffix by Pā. Sū. 6-1-193).

### Printed p. 24

*[Running head, p. 24: 24 Sāyaṇabhāṣyasahitā [Maṃ. 3. A. 1. Sūkta 7.] (the supplied text reads "सायुज…" for "Sāyaṇa", left as supplied; the page is merged into p. 23 in the supplied text)]*

Grammar page continued, noted briefly: रणंति (*raṇaṃti*) (root *raṇ*; laṭ third plural; nighāta accent after the finite verb ahead), दिवोरुचः (*divoruchaḥ*) (root *ruc* "to shine"; kvip; no luk of the genitive in *divaḥ* by Pā. Sū. 6-2-172 as printed; accent of the latter member), सुरुचः (*suruchaḥ*) (bahuvrīhi: "those whose lights are fine"; accent by *nañsubhyām*, Pā. Sū. 6-2-172), रोचमानाः (*rocamānāḥ*) (śānac for laṭ; mugāgama; the root accent remains since the sārvadhātuka is unaccented), इळा (*iḷā*) (root *īḍ* "to praise"; ap suffix; ṭāp; the long ī shortened by Vedic usage, as *vācā* is formed from *vāc*), गण्या (*gaṇyā*) (root *gaṇ* "to count"; yat suffix; initial accent by Pā. Sū. 6-1-213; ṭāp in the feminine), माहिना (*māhinā*) (root *mah* "to honour"; inaṇ, Uṇ. Sū. 2-214; vṛddhi of the penultimate; ṭāp; initial accent by vyatyaya), गीः (*gīḥ*) (root *gṛ* "to sound", kryādi; kvip; *i* for *ṛ*, Pā. Sū. 7-1-100; lengthening of the penultimate; accent of the nominal stem).

#### Ṛk 6

**॥ संहितापाठः ॥**

> उ॒तो पि॒तभ्यां॑ प्र॒विदानु॒ घोषं॑ म॒हो म॒हद्भा॑मनयंत॒
> शूषं ।
> उ॒क्षा ह॒ यत्र॒ परि॒ धान॑म॒क्तोरनु॒ स्वं धाम॑ जरि॒तुर्व॒वक्ष॑ ॥ ६ ॥

**॥ पदपाठः ॥**

> उतो इति । पितृभ्यां । प्रविदा । अनु । घोषं । महः । महत्स्भ्यां । अनयंत।
> शूषं ।
> उक्षा । ह । यत्र । पं । धानं । अक्तोः । अनु । स्वं । धाम । जरितु: । ववक्ष ॥ ६ ॥

> *uto iti | pitṛbhyāṃ | pravidā | anu | ghoṣaṃ | mahaḥ | mahatsbhyāṃ | anayaṃta|*
> *śūṣaṃ |*
> *ukṣā | ha | yatra | paṃ | dhānaṃ | aktoḥ | anu | svaṃ | dhāma | jaritu: | vavakṣa ‖ 6 ‖*

*(Note: the supplied text reads उतो पितभ्यां (*uto pitabhyāṃ*) in the Saṃhitā where the Pada has पितृभ्यां (*pitṛbhyāṃ*), and the Pada has a second misreading in the word-division of "mahadbhyām"; left as supplied. The bhāṣya and the rest of Ṛk 6 follow on printed p. 25.)*

### Printed p. 25

*[Running head, p. 25: Ā. 3. A. 1. Va. 2. Ṛgvedasaṃhitā 25 (as supplied; "Ā." for "Maṃ.")]*

**॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥**

> उतो आपि च महो महद्भ्यां महतो महद्भ्यां पितृभ्यां द्यावापृ-
> थिवीभ्यां प्रपिदा प्रवेदनेनानु घोषमनु घुष्यमाणं शूषं सुखं । शूषं शुन-
> मिति सुखनामसु पाठात् । तत्सुखं यजमाना अग्निमनयंत । अग्निं प्रापितवंतः ।
> सोग्निर्यत्र यजमानेषूक्षा वृष्टुत्पादनेनोदकस्य सेक्ताक्कोः । आकुशब्दो
> रात्रिमाचष्टे शर्वरी अक्कुरिति तन्नामसु पाठात् अज्यते सिच्यते नीहारेण जगद-
> स्यामिति व्युत्पत्तेश्च । तस्या रात्रेः परि धानं परितो धारकं स्वमात्मियं धाम
> तेजो जरितुः स्तोतुः स्तुतिश्रवणार्थमनु ववक्ष ह । समीपे वहति खलु । सन्निहितं
> करोतीत्यर्थः ॥ उतो । ऊदिति प्रगृह्यसंज्ञा । एवमादित्वादंतोदात्तः ।
> घोषं ' घुषिर् शब्दे आस्मात्कर्मणि घञ् । लघूपधगुणः । जित्स्वरः । महः ।
> अकारतकारयोर्लोपश्चांदसः । बृहन्महतोरुपसंख्यानमिति विभक्तेरुदात्तत्वं ।
> अनयंत णीञ् प्राहण इत्यस्य लङ् रूपं , निघातः । उक्षा । उक्ष सेचने । श्व-
> न्नु क्षन्नित्यादिना कनिप्रत्ययांतत्वेनोक्षन्‌शब्दो निपातितः । व्यत्ययेनांतोदात्तः ।
> ह चादित्वादनुदात्तः । यत्र । लितीति प्रत्ययात्तूर्वस्योदात्तत्वं । अक्टोः ।
> अंजू व्यक्तिगतिम्रक्षणेष्टित्यस्मात् व्याज्यर्तिभ्यः क्तुरिति क्तुः । कित्त्वादनिदितामिति
> नलोपः । प्रत्ययस्वरः । ववक्ष । वह प्रापणे । छंदसि लुज्लज्लिट इति वर्त-
> माने लिट् । सकारश्चांदसः । तिपो णलादेशः । लितीति प्रत्ययात्तूर्वस्यो-
> दात्तत्वं :

*Gist: the worshippers bring to Agni the delight proclaimed by heaven and earth; Agni, the rain-shedder, brings near to the praiser his own radiance that invests the night. The grammatical tail (accents of ghoṣam, mahaḥ, anayanta, ukṣā, aktoḥ, vavakṣa) is transcribed, not explained.*

**प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)**

Uto — and. Yatra — among whichever worshippers. Ukṣā — Agni, the one who sprinkles water (by producing rain). Aktoḥ — of the night. Pari dhānam — (like a covering) the holder. Svaṃ dhāma — his own radiance. Jaritum — of the praiser (for hearing the praise). Anu vavakṣa ha — (makes it present) brings it near. (Te — those worshippers.) Maho mahadbhyāṃ — greater than the great. Pitṛbhyāṃ — the parents, heaven and earth. Pravidā — by making known. Anu ghoṣaṃ — that is proclaimed. Śūṣaṃ [sic: the supplied text reads *Kūṣaṃ*] — the delight. Anayanta — (to Agni) caused to reach.

**भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)**

Agni, who causes water to be sprinkled by means of rain, becomes present, with the purpose of hearing the praise of the praiser, in the form of his own radiance that, like a covering, holds the night. The praisers too, by glorifying heaven and earth, greatest among the great, bring delight to Agni.

### Printed p. 26

*[Running head, p. 26: 26 Sāyaṇabhāṣyasahitā [Maṃ. 3. A. 1. Sūkta 7.]]*

**Printed English**

Verily (men) bring great delight to Agni by glorifying aloud the mighty parents (heaven and earth), when the shedder of rain approximates his own radiance to the worshipper, (wherewith) to invest the night.

**विशेषविषयगळु (Viśeṣaviṣayagaḷu)**

महः महद्भ्यां (*mahaḥ mahadbhyāṃ*)—महतो महद्भ्यां (*mahato mahadbhyāṃ*). The word *mahaḥ* is also read among the names of water (21-24).

⟦ಪಿತುಂ ನು ಸ್ತೋಷಂ ಮಹೋ ಧರ್ಮಾಣಂ ತವಿಷೀಂ ।
ಯಸ್ಯ ತ್ರಿತೋ ವ್ಯೋಜಸಾ ವೃತ್ರಂ ವಿಪರ್ವಮರ್ದಯತ್ ॥⟧ (Pu. Saṃ. 1-187-1, as supplied). In this Ṛk, since the meaning of *mahaḥ* is explained as महतः बलस्य धारयितारं (*mahataḥ balasya dhārayitāraṃ*) ("the holder of great strength", one who bears exceeding strength), the word *mahaḥ* is understood in the sense *mahat* (superior).

⟦ತ್ವಷ್ಟಾ ದುಹಿತ್ರೇ ವಹತುಂ ಕೃಣೋತೀತೀದಂ ವಿಶ್ವಂ ಭುವನಂ ಸಮೇತಿ ।
ಯಮಸ್ಯ ಮಾತಾ ಪರ್ಯುಹ್ಯಮಾನಾ ಮಹೋ ಜಾಯಾ ವಿವಸ್ವತೋ ನನಾಶ ॥⟧ (Ma. Saṃ. 10-17-1, as supplied). In this Ṛk also, since the meaning is explained as महः महतः जाया एवस्वतः (*mahaḥ mahataḥ jāyā evasvataḥ*) ("the wife of the sun, filled with greatness"), the word *mahaḥ* is used here in the sense *mahat*.

पितृभ्यां (*pitṛbhyāṃ*)—द्यावापृथिवीभ्यां (*dyāvāpṛthivībhyāṃ*). Yāska explains the word *pitṛ* as पिता पाता ना पालयिता वा (*pitā pātā nā pālayitā vā*) (Ni. 4-21), that it conveys the sense "protector". Here the meaning is taken as heaven and earth.

शूषं (*śūṣaṃ*)—सुखं (*sukhaṃ*). The word *śūṣa* is stated both among the twenty-eight names of strength (Ni. 3-9) and among the twenty names of happiness beginning with *śimbātā*, *śatarā* (as supplied) (Ni. 3-13). Here the meaning is happiness.

Here, महद्भ्यां पितृभ्यां प्रविदा अनुघोषं शूषं (*mahadbhyāṃ pitṛbhyāṃ pravidā anughoṣaṃ śūṣaṃ*) is explained thus: heaven and earth, filled with greatness, well knowing the abundance of the happiness arising from Agni, proclaim it; and the sacrificing worshippers, having obtained such happiness through the worship of Agni, express the wish that the same happiness may come to Agni who bestowed happiness on them, and thus have caused it to come to Agni.

### Printed p. 27

*[Running head, p. 27: Ā. 3. A. 1. Va. 2. Ṛgvedasaṃhitā 27 (as supplied; "Ā." for "Maṃ.")]*

उक्षा (*ukṣā*)—वृष्ट्यत्पादनेन उदकस्य सेक्ता (*vṛṣṭyatpādanena udakasya sektā*) ("the sprinkler of water by producing rain"). The word *ukṣā* is stated among the twenty-five names of the great, beginning with *mahat*, *bradhnaḥ* (Ni. 3-13). And Yāska, citing the Ṛk

⟦ವೃಷಾಕಪಾಯಿ ರೇವತಿ ಸುಪುತ್ರ ಆದು ಸುಸ್ನುಷೇ ।
ಘಸತ್ತ ಇಂದ್ರ ಉಕ್ಷಣಃ ಪ್ರಿಯಂ ಕಾಚಿತ್ಕರಂ ಹವಿರ್ವಿಶ್ವಸ್ಮಾದಿಂದ್ರ ಉತ್ತರಃ ॥⟧ (Ma. Saṃ. 10-86-13),

explains उक्षणः (*ukṣaṇaḥ*) as उक्षतेर्वृद्दिकम=णः, उक्षंत्युदकेनेति वा (*ukṣatervṛddikama=ṇaḥ, ukṣaṃtyudakeneti vā*) (as supplied): to the word *ukṣā*, formed from the root *ukṣ* that conveys growth, he gives the meaning "one who grows", or "one who pours water, hence *ukṣā*". Here Sāyaṇa explains that Agni too is denoted by the word *ukṣā* because he produces rain. The purport is that Agni produces rain to the advantage of those who perform the sacrifice.

अक्तुः (*aktuḥ*)—रात्रेः (*rātreḥ*): night. The word *aktuḥ* is stated (Ni. 2-18) among the twenty-three names of night beginning with *śyāvī*, *kṣapā*. Sāyaṇa explains: अक्तुशब्दो रात्रिमाचष्टे अज्यते सिच्यते नीहारेण जगदस्यामिति व्युत्पत्तेश्व (*aktuśabdo rātrimācaṣṭe ajyate sicyate nīhāreṇa jagadasyāmiti vyutpatteśva*) — night is called *aktu* because it wets the whole world with mist.

जरितुः (*jarituḥ*)—स्तोतुः (*stotuḥ*): of the praiser. The word *jaritṛ* is explained by Yāska as जरिता—गरिता (*jaritā—garitā*) (Ni. 1-7). It is read among the names of the praiser as रेभः जरिता (*rebhaḥ jaritā*) (Ni. 3-19).

From the sentence उक्षा अक्टोः परिधानं स्वं धाम जरितुः ववक्ष (*ukṣā akṭoḥ paridhānaṃ svaṃ dhāma jarituḥ vavakṣa*) the meaning is explained thus: Agni makes his radiance, which covers the night, present (visible) for those who praise him (for the hearing of the praise).

**Vyākaraṇa-prakriyā (grammar page, noted briefly)**

Words treated: उतो (उतो (*uto*)) (ending in o, a particle; pragṛhya designation by Pā. Sū. 1-1-15, *ot*; prakṛtibhāva by the rule *plutapragṛhyā aci nityam*; being among the *evādi* words, final accent by *evādīnām antaḥ*, Phi. Sū. 82), प्रविदा (प्रविदा (*pravidā*)) (root *vid* "to know"; kvip; prādi compound; accent of the stem by the rule for the latter member being a kṛt; instrumental singular), घोषम् (घोषम् (*ghoṣam*)) (root *ghuṣ*, "to sound"; ghañ in the object sense; guṇa of the light penultimate, Pā. Sū. 7-3-86 as printed).

### Printed p. 28

*[Running head, p. 28: 28 Sāyaṇabhāṣyasahitā [Maṃ. 3. A. 1. Sūkta 7.]]*

Grammar page continued, noted briefly: प्रत्यय इत्तादुदरिंद (*pratyaya ittādudariṃda*) the initial accent of the stem (by Pā. Sū. 6-1-197, *ādirnityam*); the form is accusative singular. मह: (*mahaḥ*) (for *mahataḥ*; Vedic loss of *a* and *t*; the case-ending takes the udātta by the statement *bṛhanmahator upasaṅkhyānam*). अनयंत (*anayanta*) (root *nī* "to lead"; laṅ third plural; all-anudātta after the preceding word). उक्षा (*ukṣā*) (root *ukṣ* "to sprinkle"; kanin suffix as given in the Uṇādi rule 1-157, *śvannukṣan*; final accent by vyatyaya; nominative singular). ह (*ha*) (read among the *cādi* particles; anudātta by Phi. Sū. 84). यत्र (*yatra*) (tral suffix in the locative sense, by *saptamyās tral*; accent before the suffix by Pā. Sū. 6-1-193, the suffix having an indicatory *l*). धानम् (*dhānam*) (root *ḍudhāṇ* "to hold, to nourish"; lyuṭ suffix in the agent sense by *bahulam*; replacement *ana*; accent before the suffix). अक्तोः (*aktoḥ*) (root *añj*; ktu suffix; loss of the penultimate nasal, Pā. Sū. 6-4-24; *ku*-substitution, Pā. Sū. 8-2-30; *cartva*; genitive singular; final accent by the suffix). धाम (*dhāma*) (manin suffix, Pā. Sū. 3-2-74 as printed; initial accent). जरितुः (*jarituḥ*) (root *jṛṣ* "to grow old", here used in the sense of praising; tṛc suffix in the agent sense; final accent). ववक्ष (*vavakṣa*) (root *vah* "to carry"; liṭ in the present sense by *chandasi luṅlaṅliṭaḥ*; ṇal for the third person singular; Vedic *s* added to the root, then the changes of *h*; because of the connexion with *yatra* the lowering of accent is prevented by Pā. Sū. 8-1-30, and by Pā. Sū. 6-1-193 the accent falls before the suffix).

### Printed p. 29

*[Running head, p. 29: Ā. 3. A. 1. Va. 2. Ṛgvedasaṃhitā 29 (as supplied; "Ā." for "Maṃ.")]*

#### Ṛk 7

**॥ संहितापाठः ॥**

> अ॒ध्व॒र्युभिः॑ पं॒चभिः॑ स॒प्त विप्राः॑ प्रि॒यं र॑क्षंते॒ निहि॑तं प॒दं वेः।
> प्रांचो॑ मदंत्यु॒क्षणो॑ अजु॒र्या दे॒वा दे॒वाना॒मनु॒ हि व्र॒ता
> गुः ॥ ७ ॥

**॥ पदपाठः ॥**

> अध्वर्युभिः । पंचभिः । सप्त । विप्राः । प्रियं । रक्षंते । निहितं । पदं । वे-
> रिति वेः ।
> प्रांचः । मदंति । उक्षणः । अजुर्याः । देवाः । देवानां । अनु । हि । व्रता ।
> गुरिति गुः ॥ ७ ॥

**॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥**

> उद्गातृवर्गव्यतिरिक्ता द्वादशर्त्विजो भवंति । तत्र सप्त वषट्कर्तारः ।
> पंचान्वेऽध्वरं नयंति । तैः पंचभिरध्वयुभिः सह सप्त विप्रा मेधाविनो
> होत्रका वेगमनस्वभावस्याग्नेः प्रियं निहितमाहवनीयाख्यं पदं रक्षंते । पाल-
> यंति । प्रांचः सोमपानार्थं प्राङ्मुखा गच्छंतोऽजुर्या जरयितुमशक्या
> उक्षणः सोमरसस्य सेक्तारो देवाः स्तोतारो मदंति । हृष्यंति । किंच ते देवा
> देवानां संबंधीनि व्रता व्रतानि यज्ञाननुगुः । अन्वगच्छन् । हि प्रसिद्धौ । अग्नेः
> प्रसादाद्देवा यज्ञान् लब्धवंत इत्यर्थः॥ रक्षंते । रक्ष पालने । व्यत्ययेनात्मने-
> पदं । निघातः । निहितं । डुधाण् धारणपोषणयोरित्यस्य कर्मणि निष्ठायां दधा-
> तेर्हिरिति हिरादेशः । गतिरनंतर इति गतेः प्रकृतिस्वरत्वं । वेः । वी कांतिगत्यादिशु ।
> अस्माद्विच् । सार्वधातुकार्थधातुकयोः । सा. ७-३-८४ । इति गुणः । जसिजसोश्चेशि
> पूर्वरूपत्वं । प्रांचः i अनिगंतोऽंचताविति पूर्वपदप्रकृतिस्वरत्वं । मदंति ।
> मदी हर्षॆ । निघातः । गुः i इण् गतावित्यस्माल्लुजि रूपं । इणो गा लुजीति
> गादेशः । आत इति झेर्जुसादेशः । बहुलं छंदसीत्यडभानः । हितब्दयोगान्न
> निहन्यते ॥

*Gist: the twelve priests other than the Udgātṛ group: seven offer the vaṣaṭ, five lead the sacrifice; together they guard Agni's dear station, the Āhavanīya, and rejoice, following the gods' rites. The grammatical tail (rakṣante, nihitam, veḥ, prāñcaḥ, madanti, guḥ) is transcribed, not explained.*

**प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)**

Pañcabhiḥ adhvaryubhiḥ — (accompanied) by five priests of the Adhvaryu group. Sapta viprāḥ — seven wise priests of the Hotṛ group. Veḥ — to Agni, of swift-moving nature.

### Printed p. 30

*[Running head, p. 30: 30 Sāyaṇabhāṣyasahitā [Maṃ. 3. A. 1. Sūkta 7.]]*

Priyam — dear. Nihitaṃ padam — the prepared station (called Āhavanīya). Rakṣante — protect. Prāñcaḥ — those facing east (for drinking the Soma), ajuryāḥ — not subject to old age, ukṣaṇaḥ — sprinklers of the Soma juice. Devāḥ — the praisers (priests). Madanti — rejoice (and those priests). Devānām — (belonging) to the gods. Vratā — the rites (sacrifices). Anu guḥ — follow. Hi — it is well known.

**भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)**

The seven wise priests of the Hotṛ group, accompanied by the five priests of the Adhvaryu group, guard the station of the Āhavanīya fire, dear to the swift-moving Agni; the priests, facing east for the drinking of the Soma, not subject to old age, sprinklers of the Soma juice, rejoice, praising Agni; [distinguished by] the oblations favoured by the pleased Agni, they undertake the other rites of the gods.

**Printed English**

Seven sages with five ministering priests attend the station that is pre-pared [sic] for the rapid (Agni): the undecaying divine (sages), with their faces to the east, sprinkling (the libations), rejoice as they celebrate the worship of the gods.

**विशेषविषयगळु (Viśeṣaviṣayagaḷu)**

पंचभिः अध्वर्युभिः सप्त विप्राः (*pañcabhiḥ adhvaryubhiḥ sapta viprāḥ*): उद्गातृवर्गव्यतिरिक्ताः द्वादशर्त्विजो भवंति । तत्र सप्तवषट्कर्तारः । पञ्चान्ये अध्वरं नयंति : सप्त मेधाविनो होत्रकाः (*udgātṛvargavyatiriktāḥ dvādaśartvijo bhavanti; tatra saptavaṣaṭkartāraḥ; pañcānye adhvaraṃ nayanti; sapta medhāvino hotrakāḥ*) (the Sanskrit as supplied; the supplied text has *saptavaṣaṭkartāraḥ* and *pañcānye*, with a colon where a danda is expected). The people who are appointed to perform the duties necessary in a sacrifice while it is being performed are called priests (*ṛtvij*). Since the sacrifice is a rite carried on by the several priests who have studied the four Vedas, those who have studied each Veda perform the rites of that Veda; so the priests connected with the performance of the sacrifice fall into four groups: the Hotṛ group, the Adhvaryu group, the Udgātṛ group and the Brahman group. The priests of the Hotṛ group are those who have studied the Ṛgveda; with the respective mantras they invoke the respective gods. Those of the Adhvaryu group have studied the Yajurveda; in the manner prescribed they prepare the oblation-substances for the gods invoked by the Hotṛ, according to the order prescribed, and after hearing the Hotṛ's *vaṣaṭ* call they perform the respective offerings. Those of the Udgātṛ group have studied the Sāmaveda; in the respective sacrifice they

### Printed p. 31

*[Running head, p. 31: Ā. 3. A. 1. Va. 2. Ṛgvedasaṃhitā 31 (as supplied; "Ā." for "Maṃ.")]*

(Special Topics continued from p. 30.) ... perform the respective sāmans in the order prescribed. The priests of the Brahman group (the Upadraṣṭṛ and the others) see that the sacrifice is completed in the prescribed manner by carrying out the proper expiations for deficiencies and excesses in the necessary duties of the sacrifice. The four groups together make sixteen persons. Among these sixteen (leaving aside the priests of the Udgātṛ group, the Brahman, the priests of the Brahman group, and the sacrificer) seven priests belong to the group of those who utter the *vaṣaṭ*. Sāyaṇa has described this point by the words उद्धातृ वर्ग व्यतिरिक्ताः द्वादश ऋत्विजः (*uddhātṛ varga vyatiriktāḥ dvādaśa ṛtvijaḥ*), "twelve priests apart from the priests of the Udgātṛ group". Of these, the seven who make the *vaṣaṭ* call, including those of the Hotṛ group, he has explained by सप्त वषट्कर्तारः (*sapta vaṣaṭkartāraḥ*). पंचाध्वर्यवः (*paṃcādhvaryavaḥ*): the five, including the priests who assist in performing the work of the Adhvaryu, are described as Adhvaryus. In all, the twelve priests carry out their respective rites without break, in the prescribed manner of the sacrifice. The Nirukta says:

गायत्रमेको गायति शक्वरीषूद्घाता (*gāyatrameko gāyati śakvarīṣūdghātā*) ... ब्रह्मैको जाते जाते विद्यां वदति (*brahmaiko jāte jāte vidyāṃ vadati*) ब्रह्मा सर्वविद्यः सर्वं वेदितुमर्हति (*brahmā sarvavidyaḥ sarvaṃ veditumarhati*) ... यज्ञस्य मात्रां विमिमीत एकोऽध्वर्युः (*yajñasya mātrāṃ vimimīta eko 'dhvaryuḥ*) अध्वर्युरध्वरयुरध्वरं युनक्त्यध्वरस्य नेताध्वरं कामयत इति वा (*adhvaryuradhvarayuradhvaraṃ yunaktyadhvarasya netādhvaraṃ kāmayata iti vā*) ... अध्वर इति यज्ञनाम (*adhvara iti yajñanāma*) ध्वरतिर्हिंसाकर्मा तत्प्रतिषेधः (*dhvaratirhiṃsākarmā tatpratiṣedhaḥ*) (the passage is given with its ellipses and punctuation as supplied).

The root *dhvar* conveys the sense "to injure"; since the injury done in a sacrifice is directed to the deity, it is established that it is not injury. Hence the word *adhvara* denotes the sacrifice, the purifying rite. In this rite the so-called Udgātṛ sings the sāman called Gāyatra. The priest called Brahman is the one who knows all the rites performed in the sacrifice; he informs the other priests of the order in which the respective rites are performed. The priest called Adhvaryu is the one who carries out the rites of the sacrifice, or who carries the sacrifice forward, or who desires that the sacrifice proceed until it is completed; thus the Nirukta explains the duties of the Adhvaryu, Brahman and Udgātṛ.

Durgācārya: यदृचैव हौत्रं क्रियते (*yadṛcaiva hautraṃ kriyate*) (Ai. Brā. 5-5-8) एतत्तावदृगध्ययॆनकर्म तस्मिन् होतरि विनियुक्तं (*etattāvadṛgadhyayènakarma tasmin hotari viniyuktaṃ*). Since the Aitareya Brāhmaṇa passage teaches that the Hotṛ performs the rites of the sacrifice with the Ṛgveda itself, all the rites of the sacrifice that belong to the Ṛgveda are the concern of the priest called Hotṛ. साम्योद्गीथं (*sāmyodgīthaṃ*) (Ai. Brā. 5-5-8) — उद्गाता (*udgātā*) तस्मिन्नप्येतत्सामगानकर्म विनियुक्तं (*tasminnapyetatsāmagānakarma viniyuktaṃ*): the Udgītha is to be performed with the Sāmaveda; the one who performs this rite is called the Udgātṛ; the sāman-singing rite connected with the sacrifice is assigned to him.

### Printed p. 32

*[Running head, p. 32: 32 Sāyaṇabhāṣyasahitā [Maṃ. 3. A. 1. Sūkta 7.]]*

ब्रह्मा नाम एक ऋत्विक् जाते प्रायश्चित्ते विद्यां वदति इतरेभ्यः ऋत्विग्भ्यः इदमत्र कुरुतेति (*brahmā nāma eka ṛtvik jāte prāyaścitte vidyāṃ vadati itarebhyaḥ ṛtvigbhyaḥ idamatra kuruteti*): the priest called Brahman, when a deviation arises in the performance of the sacrificial rites, instructs the other priests in the manner of performing the expiation to remedy it. तत्रापि ह्येतत्कर्मविनियुक्तं (*tatrāpi hyetatkarmaviniyuktaṃ*): this duty is assigned to the Brahman among the rites of the sacrifice. अथ केन ब्रह्मत्वं क्रियत इति त्रय्या विद्यया (*atha kena brahmatvaṃ kriyata iti trayyā vidyayā*) (Ai. Brā. 5-5-8): the Aitareya Brāhmaṇa says that to perform the Brahman's office he must also have knowledge of the three Vedas. या काचिदिति कर्तव्यता यज्ञस्य, तां विमिमिते अध्वर्युः, नानाप्रकारं करोति (*yā kāciditi kartavyatā yajñasya, tāṃ vimimite adhvaryuḥ, nānāprakāraṃ karoti*): the commentary on the Nirukta explains that the Adhvaryu performs all the remaining necessary duties of the sacrifice, other than the rites to be performed by the Brahman, the Hotṛ and the Udgātṛ.

Sāyaṇa has repeated the word देवाः (*devāḥ*) and given the word *deva* two kinds of meaning. First: प्रांचः अजुर्याः उक्षणः देवाः मदंति (*prāṃcaḥ ajuryāḥ ukṣaṇaḥ devāḥ madaṃti*) — the praisers, going facing east for the drinking of the Soma, are joyful; this is the meaning explained. Second: देवाः ऋत्विजः देवानां व्रतानि अनु गुः (*devāḥ ṛtvijaḥ devānāṃ vratāni anu guḥ*) — the priests carry out the joy-giving works of the sacrifice that bring satisfaction to the gods. From this he has explained the meaning: आग्नेः प्रसादात् हनिर्लक्षणान्नवंतो देवाः यज्ञान् लब्धवंतः (*āgneḥ prasādāt hanirlakṣaṇānnavaṃto devāḥ yajñān labdhavaṃtaḥ*) — by Agni's favour the gods, who obtain food in the form of the oblation, obtain it through the sacrifice. प्रांचः मदंति—सोमपानार्थं प्राज्मुखा गच्छंतः (*prāṃcaḥ madaṃti—somapānārthaṃ prājmukhā gacchaṃtaḥ*): the seven Hotṛs and the five Adhvaryus, guarding the fire called Āhavanīya, go facing east for the drinking of the Soma; thus the meaning is explained.

**Vyākaraṇa-prakriyā (grammar page, noted briefly)**

Words treated (with the Pāṇinian sūtras as printed): रक्षंते (*rakṣaṃte*) (root *rakṣ* "to protect"; ātmanepada by the rule *vyatyayo bahulam*; laṭ third plural; nighāta accent after a finite verb), निहितम् (*nihitam*) (root *ḍudhāñ* "to hold, to nourish"; kta in the passive sense, *niṣṭhā* (Pā. Sū. 3-2-102); replacement *hi*, *dadhāter hiḥ* (Pā. Sū. 7-4-42); prefix accent by *gatiranantaraḥ* (Pā. Sū. 6-2-49)), वेः (*veḥ*) (root *vī* "to shine, to go"; vic suffix; guṇa by *sārvadhātukārdhadhātukayoḥ* (Pā. Sū. 7-3-84)

### Printed p. 33

*[Running head, p. 33: Ā. 3. A. 1. Va. 2. Ṛgvedasaṃhitā (the page number is not printed in the supplied text; the marker was inserted)]*

Grammar page continued, noted briefly: the guṇa of the root's *i* gives the stem *ve*; before the genitive singular ending *jas*, *jasijasoś ca* (Pā. Sū. 6-1-110) gives the earlier form; rutva and visarga; udātta accent. प्रांचः (*prāṃcaḥ*) (prefix *pra* with the root *añc* ending in kvin; prādi compound; the accent of the first member, *anigantoñcau* (Pā. Sū. 6-2-52)). मदंति (*madaṃti*) (root *madī* "to rejoice"; laṭ third plural; all-anudātta after a finite verb). उक्षणः (*ukṣaṇaḥ*) (nominative plural; optional lengthening of the penultimate, *pāpapūrvasya nigame* (Pā. Sū. 6-4-9)). व्रता (*vratā*) (neuter plural; *śi* replacement, then elision of *śi*, *śeś chandasi bahulam* (Pā. Sū. 6-1-70)). गुः (*guḥ*) (root *iṇ* "to go"; adādi; luṅ; replacement *gā*, *iṇo gā luṅi* (Pā. Sū. 2-4-45); luk of the sic, *gātisthāghupā* (Pā. Sū. 2-4-77); *jus* for *jhi*, *ata* (Pā. Sū. 3-4-110); the augment *aṭ* does not occur, *bahulaṃ chandasy* (Pā. Sū. 6-4-75); because of the connexion with *hi*, the lowering of accent is prevented, *hi ca* (Pā. Sū. 8-1-34), and the word is udātta).

#### Ṛk 8

**॥ संहितापाठः ॥**

> देव्या॒ होता॑रा प्रथ॒मा नृं॑जे स॒प्त पृ॒क्षासः॑ स्व॒धया॑ म-
> दंति ।
> ऋ॒तं शंसं॑त ऋ॒तमित्त आ॑हु॒रनु॑ व्र॒तं व्र॑त॒पा दीध्या॑नाः
> ॥ ८ ॥

**॥ पदपाठः ॥**

> दैव्या । होतारा । प्रथमा । नि । यंजे । सप्त । पृक्षासः । स्वधया । मदंति ।
> ऋतं । शंसंतः । ऋतं । इत् । ते । आहुः । अनु । व्रतं । व्रतःपाः । दीध्याणः ॥

> *daivyā | hotārā | prathamā | ni | yaṃje | sapta | pṛkṣāsaḥ | svadhayā | madaṃti |*
> *ṛtaṃ | śaṃsaṃtaḥ | ṛtaṃ | it | te | āhuḥ | anu | vrataṃ | vrataḥpāḥ | dīdhyāṇaḥ ‖*

*Note: the Saṃhitā has देव्या (*devyā*) and नृंजे (*nṛṃje*) where the Pada has दैव्या (*daivyā*) and नि यंजे (*ni yaṃje*); the Saṃhitā दीध्यानाः (*dīdhyānāḥ*) against the Pada दीध्याणः (*dīdhyāṇaḥ*); a stray "5" follows the Pada in the supplied text. All as supplied.*

### Printed p. 34

*[Running head, p. 34: 34 Sāyaṇabhāṣyasahitā [Maṃ. 3. A. 1. Sūkta 7.]]*

**॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥**

> दैव्या दैवौ देवतारूपौ होतारा होतार् होमनिष्पादकौ प्रथमा प्र-
> थमौ मुख्याविमं चामुं चाग्नी न्यृंजे । यंजतिः प्रसाधनकर्मेति यास्कः । मंत्र-
> द्रष्टा विश्वामित्रोऽहं प्रसाधयामि । सप्त पृक्षासः सप्त होत्रकाः स्वधया
> सोमेन मदंति । ह्यष्यंति । ऋतं स्तोत्रं शंसंतो व्रतपा व्रतस्य यज्ञ कर्म-
> णो रक्षितारो दीध्याना दीप्यमानास्ते होत्रका अनु व्रतं यज्ञॆ कर्मणि ऋत-
> मित् सत्यमेवाग्नि माहुः । वदंति ॥ यंजे । ऋजि भृजी भर्जने । निपूर्वोऽयं
> धातुः प्रसाधने वर्तते । निघातः । स्वधया । स्वं लोकं यजमानस्य दधाति विद-
> धातीति स्वधा सोमः । आतोनुपसर्गे क इति कः । कृदुत्तरपदप्रकृतिस्वरत्वं । आहुः ।
> ब्रूञ् व्यक्तायां वाचीत्यस्य लटि ब्रुवः पंचानामादित आहो ब्रुव इति झेरु-
> सादेशो ब्रुवश्च आहादेशः । निघातः । दीध्यानाः । दीधीज् दीप्ति देवनयोरित्यस्माच्छा
> नच् । तस्य चित्वादंतोदात्तत्वे प्राप्तेऽभ्यस्तानामादिरित्ताद्वयुदात्तत्वं ॥

*Gist: the two chief divine Agnis, the accomplishers of the oblation, are propitiated by the seer Viśvāmitra; the seven Hotṛs rejoice with Soma and, guarding the rite, declare Agni the truth in every rite. The grammatical tail (yañje, svadhayā, āhuḥ, dīdhyānāḥ) is transcribed, not explained.*

**प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)**

Daivyā — those of divine form. Hotārā — the accomplishers of the oblations. Prathamā — the chief; the two Agnis. Ni ṛñje — (I) praise. Sapta pṛkṣāsaḥ — the seven Hotṛs. Svadhayā — with the Soma oblation. Madanti — they satisfy. Ṛtam — (Agni's) praise. Śaṃsantaḥ — those praising, guardians of the sacrifice, radiant ones. Te — those priests. Anuvrataṃ — in every rite. Ṛtam it — Agni alone, of the nature of truth. Āhuḥ — they declare (as the aim of the sacrifice).

**भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)**

I praise the two chief Agnis, of divine form and accomplishers of the oblations; the seven Hotṛs gladden Agni with the Soma oblation; those priests, who praise Agni, guard the sacrifice and are radiant, declare in every rite that Agni, of the nature of truth, is the one aimed at.

**Printed English**

I propitiate the two first divine offerers of sacrifice ; the seven (priests) rejoice with the libation ; the illustrious celebrators of holi [sic] worship, reciting (his) praises, have called AGNI the true (object) of every rite.

**विशेषविषयगळु (Viśeṣaviṣayagaḷu)**

दैव्या होतारा (*daivyā hotārā*) — दैवौ देवतारूपौ होतार् होमनिष्पादकौ (*daivau devatārūpau hotār homaniṣpādakau*). The two priests called Hotṛ and Maitrāvaruṇa perform the oblations connected with the sacrifice, (the Special Topics continue on the next page).

### Printed p. 35

*[Running head, p. 35: Aṣṭaka 3, Adhyāya 1, Varga 2, Ṛgvedasaṃhitā 35]*

...has been described. Here the word दैव्या (*daivyā*) is stated as an adjective of the word होतारा (*hotārā*). The Nirukta-writers explain its sense thus: "दैव्या होतारा (*daivyā hotārā*): the two divine Hotṛs, Agni and the middle one (Vāyu)", and they cite the Ṛk "दैन्व्या होतारा प्रथमा सुवाचा … (*dainvyā hotārā prathamā suvācā …*)" (Ṛ. Saṃ. 10-110-7) and explain their greatness. (The supplied text prints the reference twice, once as "Ma. Saṃ." and once as "Ṛ. Saṃ.", both 10-110-7; the first is evidently a misreading.) Durgācārya explains the word होतारा (*hotārā*) as the Hotṛ and Maitrāvaruṇa priests, and since the word दैवौ (*daivau*) is their adjective, these Hotṛ-Maitrāvaruṇas are human Hotṛs. The divine Hotṛs who invoke the gods, Vāyu and Agni, are the chief ones. This priest called Maitrāvaruṇa, attaining identity with Vāyu, the divine Hotṛ, constantly urges Agni, like a Hotṛ, towards the sacrifice; therefore he explains: तावेव देवानामाह्वातारौ उच्चेते (*tāveva devānāmāhvātārau uccete*), "they urge the gods to the sacrifice."

प्रथमा नृइच्छॆ (*prathamā nṛicchè*) (as supplied) — प्रथमौ मुख्याविमं चामुं चाग्नी मंत्रद्रष्टा विश्वामित्रोऽहं प्रसाधयामि (*prathamau mukhyāvimaṃ cāmuṃ cāgnī maṃtradraṣṭā viśvāmitro 'haṃ prasādhayāmi*). Since the divine Hotṛs, Vāyu and Agni, are designated by the word Hotṛ, the Hotṛ-Maitrāvaruṇas who attain identity with them are meant here. It is described that the seer Viśvāmitra, who knows the greatness of this mantra, praises them to obtain the favour of these two, the chief priests among those who perform the sacrifice. The Nirukta-writer says: ऋञ्चितः प्रसाधनकर्मा (*ṛñcitaḥ prasādhanakarmā*) (Ni. 6-21), explaining that the root ऋञ्चित (*ṛñcita*) [sic, as supplied] conveys the sense of "propitiating" (making glad), and gives the example आव ऋञ्चसे ऊर्जां व्युष्टिषु (*āva ṛñcase ūrjāṃ vyuṣṭiṣu*). Even when connected with the prefix नि (*ni*), the root conveys the same meaning.

सप्त पृक्षासः स्वधया मदंति (*sapta pṛkṣāsaḥ svadhayā madaṃti*) — सप्तहोतृकाः सोमेन हृष्यंति (*saptahotṛkāḥ somena hṛṣyaṃti*). The word पृक्ष (*pṛkṣa*) is read among the names of food (Ni. 3-9); here its meaning is explained as Hotṛs. स्वं लोकं यजमानस्य दधाति विदधाति इति स्वधा सोमः (*svaṃ lokaṃ yajamānasya dadhāti vidadhāti iti svadhā somaḥ*): because Soma grants the sacrificer a dwelling in the world where he himself dwells, Soma is denoted by the word स्वधा (*svadhā*). The meaning is that the seven Hotṛs are joyful from drinking the Soma.

व्रतपाः (*vratapāḥ*) — व्रतस्य यज्ञकर्मणो रक्षितारः (*vratasya yajñakarmaṇo rakṣitāraḥ*). The Nirukta-writers: व्रतमिति कर्मनाम वृणोतीति सतः । इदमपीतरत् व्रतमेतस्मादेव निवृत्तिकर्म वारयतीति सतः । अन्न- (*vratamiti karmanāma vṛṇotīti sataḥ | idamapītarat vratametasmādeva nivṛttikarma vārayatīti sataḥ | anna-*)

### Printed p. 36

*[Running head, p. 36: 36 Sāyaṇabhāṣyasahitā [Maṃ. 3. A. 1. Sūkta 7]]*

मपि व्रतमुच्यते यदावृणोति शरीरं (*mapi vratamucyate yadāvṛṇoti śarīraṃ*) — thus they state the meaning of the word व्रत (*vrata*). From the root वृञ् वरणे (*vṛñ varaṇe*) the word व्रत (*vrata*) is formed in the agent sense: वृणोतीति व्रतं (*vṛṇotīti vrataṃ*). Because it attaches to the one who has done an act, whether good or bad, one kind of meaning of the word व्रत (*vrata*) is "action". Secondly, since it keeps off (prevents) others from approaching the fire (the altar), it may mean व्रत (*vrata*) in that sense; also, since it turns one away from pleasures of the senses such as intercourse with women, it is व्रत (*vrata*); or, since food, the cause of growth of the seven constituents of the body, covers the body, व्रत (*vrata*) is explained as food. Sāyaṇa has explained व्रत (*vrata*) as sacrificial action, and the word व्रतपाः (*vratapāḥ*) as the priests who protect it.

**Vyākaraṇa-prakriyā (grammar page, noted briefly)**

Words treated: दैव्या (*daivyā*) (suffix यञ् (*yañ*) in the sense of relation to a god by "देवाद्यञ्ञौ (*devādyaññau*)"; ādivṛddhi and initial-udātta accent; the dual ending replaced by ā by "सुपांसुलुक् (*supāṃsuluk*)"; likewise in होतारा (*hotārā*)). ऋंजे (*ṛṃje*) (root ऋजि भृजी भर्जने (*ṛji bhṛjī bharjane*); with the prefix नि (*ni*) the sense becomes "to adorn"; Yāska: ऋंजतिः प्रसाधनकर्मा (*ṛṃjatiḥ prasādhanakarmā*); augment नुम् (*num*) by इदितोनुम् धातोः (*iditonum dhātoḥ*) (Pā. Sū. 7-1-58); laṭ first person singular; finite-verb nighāta accent). पृक्षासः (*pṛkṣāsaḥ*) (आसुक् (*āsuk*) augment to जस् (*jas*) by अज्जसेरसुक् (*ajjaserasuk*) (Pā. Sū. 7-1-50)). स्वधया (*svadhayā*) (स्वं लोकं यजमानस्य दधाति विदधाति इति स्वधा सोमः (*svaṃ lokaṃ yajamānasya dadhāti vidadhāti iti svadhā somaḥ*), "Soma gives the sacrificer his divine world"; suffix क (*ka*) on the root धाज् (*dhāj*) by अतोनुपसर्गेकः (*atonupasargekaḥ*) (Pā. Sū. 3-2-3); elision of the root's ā; feminine टाप् (*ṭāp*); accent of the final member by गतिकारकोपपदात् कृत् (*gatikārakopapadāt kṛt*)). शंसंत: (*śaṃsaṃta:*) (root शंसु स्तुत् (*śaṃsu stut*); शतृ (*śatṛ*) for laṭ; the sārvadhātuka is anudātta by तास्यनुदात्तेत् (*tāsyanudāttet*), so the root accent remains; nominative plural). आहु: (*āhu:*) (root ब्रूञ् व्यक्तायां वाचि (*brūñ vyaktāyāṃ vāci*); laṭ third plural; उस् (*us*) substituted for झि (*jhi*) and अह (*aha*) for the root by ब्रुवः पंचानामादित अहो ब्रुवः (*bruvaḥ paṃcānāmādita aho bruvaḥ*) (Pā. Sū. 3-4-84); para-rūpa; all-anudātta after a non-finite word). व्रतपा: (*vratapā:*) (व्रतं पांति इति व्रतपाः (*vrataṃ pāṃti iti vratapāḥ*); root पा रक्षणे (*pā rakṣaṇe*), suffix विच् (*vic*); accent of the final member).

*[Running head, p. 37: Aṣṭaka 3, Adhyāya 1, Varga 2, Ṛgvedasaṃhitā 37]*

Grammar page continued, noted briefly: दी ध्यानाः (*dī dhyānāḥ*) (root दीधीज् दीप्तिदेवनयोः (*dīdhīj dīptidevanayoḥ*); for laṭ the substitute शानच् (*śānac*); because of the चित् (*cit*) marker the final-udātta accent is expected, but since the reduplicated class applies, the initial-udātta accent comes by अभ्यस्तानामादिः (*abhyastānāmādiḥ*) (Pā. Rū. [sic, as supplied] 6-1-189)).

#### Ṛk 9

**॥ संहितापाठः ॥**

> [figure: vol16/html/figures/fig_line2559.jpg]
> वृ॒ष्टा॒यंते॑ म॒हे अत्या॑य पू॒र्विर्वृष्णे॑ चि॒त्राय॑ र॒श्मयः॒॑
> सुयामाः ।
> देव॑ होतर्मं॒द्रत॑रश्तिकि॒त्वान्म॒हो दे॒वानोद॑सी॒ एह॑
> वक्षि ॥ ९ ॥

**॥ पदपाठः ॥**

> वृषयंते महे अत्याय पूर्विः वृष्णे चित्राय रश्मयः । सुयामाः ।
> देव । होतः । मंद्र-तरः चरित्वान् महः । देवान् । रोदसी इति । आ । इह ।
> वक्षि ॥ ९ ॥

**॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥**

> देव देदीप्यमाना होतर्देवानामाह्वातः हे अग्ने महॆ महतेs-
> त्याय सर्वानपतिक्रम्य वर्तमानाय चित्राय नानाविधवर्णाय । यद्वा
> चित्राय देवैश्चायनीयाय पूजाय । वृष्णे कामानां वर्षित्रे तुभ्यं त्वदर्थं
> पूर्वीःप्रभूताः सुयामा अतिशयेन विस्तृता रश्मयः सर्वतो प्याप्ता ज्वाला
> वृषयंते । वृषेव चरंति । सेचका इव भवंति होतारो ज्वालाभिः
> समिद्दमग्निंदृष्टा सोमाज्यपयःप्रभृतीनि हवींषि तत्र प्रक्षिपंतीति ज्वाला-
> नां वृषत्वं यद्वा षष्ट्यर्थे चतुर्थी । तथा च महतोऽत्यस्य चित्रस्य
> वृष्णोऽगीर्बहवो रश्मयो वृषेवाचरंति । वृष्टुत्पादनद्वारा पर्जन्यवदा-
> चरंतीत्यर्थः अपि च । मंद्रतरो यजमानस्य मादयितृतमश्चि कित्वान् ज्ञानो-
> पेतः स त्वं महो महतः पूज्यान् यष्टव्यान् देवान् रोदसी द्यावापृथिव्यौ
> चेह वैदिकेऽस्मिन्कर्मण्यावक्षि । अवह ॥ वृषायंते । उपमानादित्यनुवृत्तौ कर्तुः

*Gist: Agni, the great, wonderful one, is the showerer; his many extended rays act like rain-givers; the wise, most gladdening Hotṛ is asked to bring the great gods and heaven and earth to this rite. ### Printed p. 38

*[Running head, p. 38: 38 Sāyaṇabhāṣyasahitā [Maṃ. 3. A. 1. Sūkta 7]]*

**Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya (continued)**

> तत्वं । पूर्विः । पू पालनपूरणयोरित्यस्मात् कुरित्यनुवृत्तौ पूभिदिव्यधिगृधी-

> *tyādinā kupratyayaḥ | kitvādguṇapratiṣedhe udoṣyapūrvasyetyuttvaṃ | uraṇ*

> *nā chaṃdasīti savarṇadīrghaḥ | raśmayaḥ | aśū vyāptau | aśnu vate sarvaṃ jagaditi miri-*

> *todāttatvaṃ | deva | apādādāviti paryudāsātpāṣṭhikamādyudāttatvaṃ | hotaḥ |*
> *hvayatestācchilikastan | bahulaṃ chaṃdasīti saṃprasāraṇaṃ | apādādyāmaṃtritatvānni-*
> *ghātaḥ | maṃdrataraḥ | madi stutyādiṣu | sthāyitaṃcivaṃcītyādinā rak | iditvā-*
> *nnum | tarapaḥ pitvādanudāttātve rakṛtyayasvaraḥ | vakṣi | vaha prāpaṇa ityasya bahulaṃ*
> *chaṃdasīti śapo luk | ṭatvakatvaṣatvāni | nighātaḥ |*

*Note: the bhāṣya's grammatical tail continues (words treated include पूर्विः (*pūrviḥ*), रश्मयः (*raśmayaḥ*), सुयामाः (*suyāmāḥ*), देव (*deva*), होतः (*hotaḥ*), मंद्रतरः (*maṃdrataraḥ*), वक्षि (*vakṣi*)); transcribed, not explained.*

**प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)**

देव (*deva*) — the shining one; होतः (*hotaḥ*) — O Agni, who calls the gods to the sacrifices; महे (*mahe*) — the worshipful; अत्याय (*atyāya*) — who surpasses all; चित्राय (*citrāya*) — of many hues (worshipful to the gods); वृष्णे (*vṛṣṇe*) — the showerer of desires, (for you); सुयामाः (*suyāmāḥ*) — exceedingly extended; पूर्वीः रश्मयः (*pūrvīḥ raśmayaḥ*) — countless rays; व्यपायंते (*vyapāyaṃte*) [sic, as supplied] — act like Parjanya (by producing rain), and; मंद्रतरः (*maṃdrataraḥ*) — the best among those who gratify; चिकित्वान् (*cikitvān*) — possessed of knowledge, you; महः (*mahaḥ*) — the worshipful; देवान् (*devān*) — the gods; रोदसी (*rodasī*) — heaven and earth; इह (*iha*) — to this rite; आ वह (*ā vaha*) — bring.

**भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)**

O Agni, shining one, who calls the gods to the sacrifices! To you, of great glory, mighty, of many hues (worshipful), showerer of desires, the countless, greatly extended flames become sprinklers by providing room for the casting in of ghee and other oblations; you, the best among those who gladden the worshippers, the wise one, bring to this Vedic rite the gods who are worthy of worship in the sacrifices, and heaven and earth.

**Printed English translation of the Ṛk**

Divine invoker of the gods, the vast and widespread rays shed (moisture) for you the mighty, the victorious, the wonderful, the showerer (of benefits): you are all-knowing, joy-bestowing ; bring hither the great gods, and heaven and earth.

### Printed p. 39

*[Running head, p. 39: Aṣṭaka 3, Adhyāya 1, Varga 2, Ṛgvedasaṃhitā 39]*

**विशेषविषयगळु (Viśeṣaviṣayagaḷu)**

**देव होतः (*deva hotaḥ*)** — "the shining one, the invoker of the gods". Here Agni is described as blazing with abundant radiance and as the one who invites the gods to the sacrifice. In the passage "अस्य वामस्य फलितस्य होतुः (*asya vāmasya phalitasya hotuḥ*)" [as supplied] the word Hotṛ was explained as "the one who is invoked". For the word होतुः (*hotuḥ*) in "होतुश्चित्पूर्वे हविरद्यमाशत (*hotuścitpūrve haviradyamāśata*)" Durgācārya explains "होतुरपि अग्नेः (*hoturapi agneḥ*)": of Agni, who is in the form of a human Hotṛ; and (he states that) the greatness of the gods is described in four ways: the mantras are of the kinds पौरुषविध्यं (*pauruṣavidhyaṃ*), अपौरुषविध्यं (*apauruṣavidhyaṃ*), कर्मार्थात्मोभयविध्यं (*karmārthātmobhayavidhyaṃ*) and नित्यमौभयविध्यं (*nityamaubhayavidhyaṃ*) [all as supplied], and by these kinds the glory of the gods is made manifest. The gods are endowed with a power consisting of abundant lordship (aiśvarya) and act in the manner fitting their respective duties; the seers, beholders of the mantras, saw this directly and praised each in his own way; and the glory of the gods is described as mūrtā (having form), amūrtā (formless), ekadhā (of one kind), dvidhā (of two kinds) and bahudhā (of many kinds). Here Agni's power is described as his having attained identity with the human Hotṛ who invokes the gods.

**चित्राय (*citrāya*)** — "नानाविधवर्णाय यद्वा देवैश्चायनीयाय पूज्याय (*nānāvidhavarṇāya yadvā devaiścāyanīyāya pūjyāya*)". Sāyaṇa gives two meanings: first, that Agni has many kinds of colours through his rays; second, that Agni, receiving the oblation-portions even from the gods, is worshipful. The Nirukta-writers explain the word citra as "चायनीयं महनीयं पूजनीयं (*cāyanīyaṃ mahanīyaṃ pūjanīyaṃ*)", giving the sense of "worshipful, deserving honour".

**चित्रस्य रश्मयः वृषायंते (*citrasya raśmayaḥ vṛṣāyaṃte*)** — सर्वतः व्याप्ताः ज्वालाः वृषेवाचरंति सेचका इव भवंति । होतारः ज्वालाभिः समिद्धमग्निं दृष्ट्वा सोमाज्यपयःप्रभृतीनि हवींषि तत्र प्रक्षिपंतीति ज्वालानां वृषत्वं । यद्वा षष्ठ्यर्थे चतुर्थी । तथा च महतो अत्यस्य चित्रस्य वृष्टो अग्नेः बहवो रश्मयः वृषेवाचरंति । वृष्टुत्पादनद्वारा पर्जन्यवदाचरंति । (*sarvataḥ vyāptāḥ jvālāḥ vṛṣevācaraṃti secakā iva bhavaṃti | hotāraḥ jvālābhiḥ samiddhamagniṃ dṛṣṭvā somājyapayaḥprabhṛtīni havīṃṣi tatra prakṣipaṃtīti jvālānāṃ vṛṣatvaṃ | yadvā ṣaṣṭhyarthe caturthī | tathā ca mahato atyasya citrasya vṛṣṭo agneḥ bahavo raśmayaḥ vṛṣevācaraṃti | vṛṣṭutpādanadvārā parjanyavadācaraṃti |*) (The supplied text reads "ಚಿತ್ರಬ್ದಕ್ಕೆ" and "ಚಿತ್ರಸ್ಯ ರಶ್ಮಯಃ" at this point, and "ವೃಷ್ಟೋ ಅಗ್ನೇಃ" in the quotation; all as supplied.) This is the same bhāṣya passage already summarised under the Ṛk: the flames spread on all sides act as showerers (sprinklers), since the Hotṛs, seeing Agni kindled by the flames, cast Soma, ghee, milk and other oblations into it; or the dative is in the sense of the genitive, so that the many rays of the great, wonderful, showering Agni act like a rain-giver.

A verse on the seven tongues of Agni follows (as supplied, with a gap of dots in the first line):

काळी कराळीचॆ मनोजवाच..................सुधूम्रवर्णा । (*kāḷī karāḷīcè manojavāca..................sudhūmravarṇā |*)
स्पुलिंगिनी विश्वरुची च देवी लेलायमाना इति सप्त जिह्वाः ॥ (*spuliṃginī viśvarucī ca devī lelāyamānā iti sapta jihvāḥ ‖*)

### Printed p. 40

*[Running head, p. 40: 40 Sāyaṇabhāṣyasahitā [Maṃ. 3. A. 1. Sūkta 7]]*

The various flames of Agni, or the rays described in this mantra, spreading everywhere and fulfilling the wishes of the sacrificers, act like Indra, who fulfils the wishes of those who serve him. The Hotṛ and other priests, pouring Soma juice, ghee (clarified butter), oblations and the like into the well-kindled fire, obtain their desired ends; therefore Sāyaṇa explains, as one meaning, that the flames of Agni grant what is desired and, like Indra, bestow wishes. In the second meaning, the dative case in चित्राय (*citrāya*) stands in the sense of the genitive, so that it is चित्रस्य ज्वालाः (*citrasya jvālāḥ*): the flames of the worshipful Agni, shining in many ways and accepting the oblation-portions offered by the sacrificers, become rain-producers (since they cause rain to fall) and do the work of Indra, the production of rain. Hence he explains वृषायंते वृषेवाचरंति (*vṛṣāyaṃte vṛṣevācaraṃti*). Thus the sense is given in two ways: because Agni's flames, kindled and widely spread, accept the oblations offered by the sacrificers and fulfil their wishes, or because, endowed with many colours, they produce rain, they do for Agni and for the world the work of Indra, the fulfilment of desired ends.

**मंद्रतरः (*maṃdrataraḥ*)** — "यजमानस्य मादयितृतमः (*yajamānasya mādayitṛtamaḥ*)". Because Agni fulfils the wishes of the sacrificers, he makes them joyful and exhilarated; this is the meaning. It is said: "मंद्रा मदना हर्षकरी तर्पयित्री वा लोकस्य (*maṃdrā madanā harṣakarī tarpayitrī vā lokasya*)" (Ni. 11-30).

**महः देवा, रोदसी इह आ वह (*mahaḥ devā, rodasī iha ā vaha*)** — By this sentence, since Agni also performs the Hotṛ's function (inviting the gods to the sacrifice), he is prayed to invite to the sacrificial rite the gods who are worshipful and worthy of sacrifice, and also heaven and earth. The word रोदसी (*rodasī*) is read among the names of heaven and earth (Ni. 3-22).

**Vyākaraṇa-prakriyā (grammar page, noted briefly)**

वृषायंते (*vṛṣāyaṃte*): "वृषा इव आचरंति (*vṛṣā iva ācaraṃti*)"; from the sūtra "उपमानादाचारे (*upamānādācāre*)" with उपमानात् (*upamānāt*) continuing, the suffix क्यज् (*kyaj*) [as supplied] comes after the word वृष (*vṛṣa*) by "कर्तुः क्यज् सलोपश्च (*kartuḥ kyaj salopaśca*)" (Pā. Sū. 3-1-11); lengthening of the preceding vowel by "अकृत् सार्वधातुकयोर्दिर्घः (*akṛt sārvadhātukayordirghaḥ*)" (Pā. Sū. 7-4-25); by "सनाद्यंताधातवः (*sanādyaṃtādhātavaḥ*)" the form वृषाय (*vṛṣāya*) gets the name dhātu; the laṭ third-person plural form stands at the beginning of a pāda, so the nighāta accent does not arise; the root accent remains.

### Printed p. 41

*[Running head, p. 41: Aṣṭaka 3, Adhyāya 1, Varga 2, Ṛgvedasaṃhitā 41]*

Grammar page continued, noted briefly (the page is prose, no Ṛk). Words treated: महे (*mahe*) (from महते; the loss of the final letters is Vedic; the case-ending gets the udātta by the vārttika on बृहन्महतोः); पूर्वीः (*pūrvīḥ*) (root पृ, suffix कु by Uṇ. Sū. 1-23, guṇa blocked, उत्व by Pā. Sū. 7-1-102, feminine ङीष् by Pā. Sū. 4-1-44, lengthening by Pā. Sū. 8-2-77, final-udātta by the suffix accent); रश्मयः (*raśmayaḥ*) (root अशू व्याप्तौ, suffix मि with रश्-substitution, Uṇ. Sū. (4-486) [as supplied]); सुयामा: (*suyāmā:*) (यमनं यामः, suffix घञ्, vṛddhi by Pā. Sū. 7-2-116, bahuvrīhi accent by Pā. Sū. 6-2-172); देव (*deva*) (not at the beginning of a pāda, so the vocative nighāta does not arise; initial-udātta by Pā. Sū. 6-1-198); होतः (*hotaḥ*) (root ह्वेञ्, suffix तृन्, saṃprasāraṇa, guṇa; nighāta); मंद्रतरः (*maṃdrataraḥ*) (root मदि, suffix रक् by Uṇ. Sū. 2-186, नुम् by Pā. Sū. 7-1-58, तरप्); वक्षि (*vakṣi*) (root वह, laṭ 2nd sg. सिप्, loss of शप्, ढ-, क-, ष-changes). A stray "6" at the foot of the page is a sheet mark in the supplied text.

The grammar note of Ṛk 9 ends at the top of the page (ढ→क, ष-substitution; the form वक्षि (*vakṣi*) with the tiṅanta nighāta accent).

#### Ṛk 10

**॥ संहितापाठः ॥**

> पृ॒क्षप्र॑यजो द्रविणः सु॒वाचः॑ सुके॒तव॑ उ॒षसो॑ रे॒वदू॑षुः ।
> उ॒तो चि॑दग्ने महि॒ना पृ॑थि॒व्याः कृ॒तं चि॒देनः॒ सं म॒हे द॑शस्य ॥
> ॥ १० ॥

**॥ पदपाठः ॥**

> पृक्षप्रयुजः । द्रविणः । सुवाचः सुकेतवः । उषसः । रेवत् । ऊषुः ।
> उतो इति । चित् । अग्ने । महिना । पृथिव्याः । कृतं । चित् । एनः । सं । महे ।
> दशस्य ॥ १० ॥

> *pṛkṣaprayujaḥ | draviṇaḥ | suvācaḥ suketavaḥ | uṣasaḥ | revat | ūṣuḥ |*
> *uto iti | cit | agne | mahinā | pṛthivyāḥ | kṛtaṃ | cit | enaḥ | saṃ | mahe |*
> *daśasya ‖ 10 ‖*

(The Pada reads पृक्षप्रयुजः (*pṛkṣaprayujaḥ*) where the Saṃhitā and bhāṣya read पृक्षप्रयजः (*pṛkṣaprayajaḥ*); all as supplied.)

**॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥**

> द्रविणः । द्रवति सततं गच्छतीति द्रविणःशब्देनाग्निरभिधीयते ! द्रविणः सतत-
> गमनस्वभाव हे अग्ने पृक्षप्रयजः पृक्षाणि हविर्लक्षणान्यन्नानि प्रकर्षेण यष्टुं
> प्रक्रम्यते यासूषःसु ताः पृक्षप्रयजः सुवाचः प्रातरनुवाकादिशोभना वाचो
> वासां ताः । सुकेतवो वयसां मनुष्याणां च शब्दैः सुप्रज्ञानाः । एवंविधा
> उषसो रेवत् आस्माकं धनयुक्तं यथा भवति तथोषुः व्युच्छंति । उतो चित्
> अपि च हे अग्ने पृथिव्या विस्तीर्णाया ज्वालाया महिना महत्वेन कृतं संपादितं
> चित् यत्किंचिदेनः प्राण्यसघातादिलक्षणं पापं महे महते यजमानाय
> मह्यं मदर्थ० संदशस्य । संक्षपय ॥ पृक्षप्रयजः अन्येभ्योऽपि दृश्यंत
> इति कर्मणि विच् दृशिग्रहणादधिकृतो भवतीत्युक्तं । बहुप्रीहित्वात्तूर्व-
> पदप्रकृतिस्वरत्वं । द्रविणः । द्रु गतावित्यस्मात् द्रुदक्षिभ्यामिनन्नि तीनप्रत्ययः । गुणा-
> वादेशौ । संबुद्धौ सोर्लोपाभावश्चांदसः : आमंत्रितप्पानि घातः । सुवाचः ।
> नञ्सुभ्यामित्युत्तरपदांतोदात्तत्वं । रेवत् रयिर्धनमस्मिन्विद्यत इति मतुप् ।
> द्रस्वनुड्व्यामिति मतुबुदात्तः । छंदसीर इति वत्वं । रयेर्मत्बहुलं छंदसीति
> संप्रसारणं । परपूर्वत्वे गुणः । ऊषुः । उष दाहे । दाहार्थोऽप्ययं धातु-
> रत्र प्रकाशने वर्तते । छंदसि लुज्जति वर्तमाने लिट् । उसि रूपं । निघातः ।
> पृथिव्याः । प्रथ प्रख्यान इत्यस्मात्पथे: शिवन् संप्रसारणं चेति षिवन्प्रत्ययः । तत्स-

> *draviṇaḥ | dravati satataṃ gacchatīti draviṇaḥśabdenāgnirabhidhīyate ! draviṇaḥ satata-*
> *gamanasvabhāva he agne pṛkṣaprayajaḥ pṛkṣāṇi havirlakṣaṇānyannāni prakarṣeṇa yaṣṭuṃ*
> *prakramyate yāsūṣaḥsu tāḥ pṛkṣaprayajaḥ suvācaḥ prātaranuvākādiśobhanā vāco*
> *vāsāṃ tāḥ | suketavo vayasāṃ manuṣyāṇāṃ ca śabdaiḥ suprajñānāḥ | evaṃvidhā*
> *uṣaso revat āsmākaṃ dhanayuktaṃ yathā bhavati tathoṣuḥ vyucchaṃti | uto cit*
> *api ca he agne pṛthivyā vistīrṇāyā jvālāyā mahinā mahatvena kṛtaṃ saṃpāditaṃ*
> *cit yatkiṃcidenaḥ prāṇyasaghātādilakṣaṇaṃ pāpaṃ mahe mahate yajamānāya*
> *mahyaṃ madartha0 saṃdaśasya | saṃkṣapaya ‖ pṛkṣaprayajaḥ anyebhyo 'pi dṛśyaṃta*
> *iti karmaṇi vic dṛśigrahaṇādadhikṛto bhavatītyuktaṃ | bahuprīhitvāttūrva-*
> *padaprakṛtisvaratvaṃ | draviṇaḥ | dru gatāvityasmāt drudakṣibhyāminanni tīnapratyayaḥ | guṇā-*
> *vādeśau | saṃbuddhau sorlopābhāvaścāṃdasaḥ : āmaṃtritappāni ghātaḥ | suvācaḥ |*
> *nañsubhyāmityuttarapadāṃtodāttatvaṃ | revat rayirdhanamasminvidyata iti matup |*
> *drasvanuḍvyāmiti matubudāttaḥ | chaṃdasīra iti vatvaṃ | rayermatbahulaṃ chaṃdasīti*
> *saṃprasāraṇaṃ | parapūrvatve guṇaḥ | ūṣuḥ | uṣa dāhe | dāhārtho 'pyayaṃ dhātu-*
> *ratra prakāśane vartate | chaṃdasi lujjati vartamāne liṭ | usi rūpaṃ | nighātaḥ |*
> *pṛthivyāḥ | pratha prakhyāna ityasmātpathe: śivan saṃprasāraṇaṃ ceti ṣivanpratyayaḥ | tatsa-*

*Gist: Agni, ever-moving, is addressed; the dawns, with abundant oblation-foods, fair speech (morning recitations) and clear signs by the sounds of birds and men, are to dawn for us bringing wealth; and he is asked to destroy whatever sin, such as injury to living creatures, has been committed, by the greatness of his wide flame, for the worshipper. The grammatical tail (दविणः, सुवाचः, रेवत्, ऊषुः, पृथिव्याः) is transcribed, not explained; the bhāṣya continues on the next page.*

### Printed p. 43

*[Running head, p. 43: Aṣṭaka 3, Adhyāya 1, Varga 2, Ṛgvedasaṃhitā 43]*

**Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya (continued)**

> न्नियोगेन धातोः संप्रसारणं । षिद्गौरादिभ्यश्चति जीष् । प्रत्ययस्वरेणांतो-
> दात्तः । उदात्तयणो हल्पूर्वादिति विभक्तेरुदात्तत्वं । दशस्य । निघातः ॥

> *nniyogena dhātoḥ saṃprasāraṇaṃ | ṣidgaurādibhyaścati jīṣ | pratyayasvareṇāṃto-*
> *dāttaḥ | udāttayaṇo halpūrvāditi vibhakterudāttatvaṃ | daśasya | nighātaḥ ‖*

*Note: the grammatical tail ends with पृथिव्याः (*pṛthivyāḥ*) and दशस्य (*daśasya*); transcribed, not explained.*

**प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)**

(O) ever-moving one (द्रविणः (*draviṇaḥ*)), O Agni; the dawns (उषसः (*uṣasaḥ*)) that have much oblation-food and the like offered in the fire (प्रैक्षप्रयजः (*praikṣaprayajaḥ*) [sic, as supplied]); that are possessed of fair speech such as the morning recitation (सुवाचः (*suvācaḥ*)); that are indicative by words such as the "prakṣa" cries (सुकेतवः (*suketavaḥ*)); rise (ऊषुः (*ūṣuḥ*)) endowed with wealth (that is to be given to us) (रेवत् (*revat*)). And (उतो चित् (*uto cit*)), O Agni (अग्ने (*agne*)), for me, the respected (sacrificer) (महे (*mahe*)), whatever sin (चित् एनः (*cit enaḥ*)), such as injury to living creatures, that has been accumulated (कृतं (*kṛtaṃ*)), by the greatness (महिना (*mahinā*)) of your exceedingly wide flame (पृथिव्याः (*pṛthivyāḥ*)), destroy completely (सं दशस्य (*saṃ daśasya*)).

**भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)**

O ever-moving Agni, may the dawns, endowed with the morning recitations and other praises, with oblations and the like offered in the fire, and with the awakening "prakṣa" cries and the like, rise for us as givers of wealth and the rest; and, O Agni, whatever sin, such as injury to living creatures, has been committed by us, destroy it for me, the respected one, by your greatly extended flame.

**Printed English translation of the Ṛk**

Ever-moving (Agni) may the mornings rise for us, abounding with oblatio ns [sic], with pious prayers, and with auspicious signs, and conferring wealth ; and with the might of your (diffusive flame), consume all sin on behalf of your respectable (worshipper).

**विशेषविषयगळु (Viśeṣaviṣayagaḷu)**

**द्रविणः (*draviṇaḥ*)** — "द्रवति सततं गच्छति इति द्रविणशब्देन अग्निरभिधीयते (*dravati satataṃ gacchati iti draviṇaśabdena agnirabhidhīyate*)". The word द्रविण (*draviṇa*), formed from the root द्रु (*dru*) meaning "to go", means "one who is always moving"; so the meaning given here is that Agni has the nature of ever moving about. The Nirukta-writers read the word द्रविण (*draviṇa*) among the names of strength and also among the names of wealth (Ni. 3-9).

**पृक्षप्रयजः (*pṛkṣaprayajaḥ*)** — "पृक्षाणि हविर्लक्षणान्नानि प्रकर्षेण यष्टुं प्रक्रम्यते यासु उषस्सु ताः (*pṛkṣāṇi havirlakṣaṇānnāni prakarṣeṇa yaṣṭuṃ prakramyate yāsu uṣassu tāḥ*)". The Nirukta-writers read the word पृक्ष (*pṛkṣa*) among the names of food (Ni. 3-9). Sāyaṇa explains पृक्ष (*pṛkṣa*) as food in the form of oblation, and explains that the time at which this oblation-food is offered abundantly (excessively) in the fire is the time of dawn.

### Printed p. 44

*[Running head, p. 44: 44 Sāyaṇabhāṣyasahitā [Maṃ. 3. A. 1. Sū. 7.]]*

**Special Topics (continued)**

**सुवाचः (*suvācaḥ*)** — "प्रातरनुवाकादिशोभना वाचो यासां ताः (*prātaranuvākādiśobhanā vāco yāsāṃ tāḥ*)". The time at which the priests recite in the sacrifice the mantras belonging to the morning, and the (auspicious) words of good meaning recited to wake Agni, is full of such utterances; hence the word सुवाचः (*suvācaḥ*) is used as an epithet of the dawn.

**सुकेशवः (*sukeśavaḥ*)** [sic, as supplied; the Saṃhitā word is सुकेतवः (*suketavaḥ*)] — The word केतु (*ketu*) is read among the names of intelligence (Ni. 3-13). In अह्नां केतुः (*ahnāṃ ketuḥ*) the word केतु (*ketu*) means "mark, sign" (Ni. 11-6). केतुना कर्मणा प्रज्ञया वा (*ketunā karmaṇā prajñayā vā*) (Ni. 11-27): by केतु (*ketu*) is meant action or intelligence. In देवं वहंति केशवः (*devaṃ vahaṃti keśavaḥ*), केशवः रश्मयः (*keśavaḥ raśmayaḥ*) (Ni. 12-17) gives "rays". So the word has several meanings: intelligence, action, mark, ray. Sāyaṇa, taking the word as an epithet of the dawn, says वयसां मनुष्याणां च शब्दैः सुप्रज्ञानाः (*vayasāṃ manuṣyāṇāṃ ca śabdaiḥ suprajñānāḥ*): at dawn men and birds make sounds, the whole world wakes, and so it is the time that gives all the world the knowledge needed for its business.

**उषसः रेवत् ऊषुः (*uṣasaḥ revat ūṣuḥ*)** — "एवंविधाः उषसः अस्माकं धनयुक्तं यथा भवति तथा व्युच्छंति (*evaṃvidhāḥ uṣasaḥ asmākaṃ dhanayuktaṃ yathā bhavati tathā vyucchaṃti*)". The word उषाः (*uṣāḥ*) is explained (Ni. 2-19): उषाः कस्मादुच्छतीति, सत्या रात्रेरपरः कालः (*uṣāḥ kasmāducchatīti, satyā rātreraparaḥ kālaḥ*). Formed from the root उच्छ (*uccha*) meaning "to dispel" (vivāsana), उषाः (*uṣāḥ*) is the last part of the night, which destroys darkness. The dawns, according to the epithets given here, act so that the sacrificers become wealthy.

**पृथिव्या: (*pṛthivyā:*)** — "विस्तीर्णायाः ज्वालायाः (*vistīrṇāyāḥ jvālāyāḥ*)". The word पृथ्वि (*pṛthvi*) is read among the names of the earth (Ni. 2-5) and of the atmosphere (Ni. 2-10). Here the word पृथ्वी (*pṛthvī*), formed from the adjective पृथु (*pṛthu*), is used as पृथिव्याः (*pṛthivyāḥ*) by Vedic usage. The meaning can be explained as: Agni's flame is abundant, spread everywhere.

**एनः (*enaḥ*)** — "प्राण्युपघातलक्षणं पापं (*prāṇyupaghātalakṣaṇaṃ pāpaṃ*)". When explaining the words किल्पिषं (*kilpiṣaṃ*) and एनः (*enaḥ*) the Nirukta-writers say सुकृतकर्मणो भयं एनः (*sukṛtakarmaṇo bhayaṃ enaḥ*): it destroys meritorious acts. एनः एतेः (*enaḥ eteḥ*) (Ni. 11-25): the word एनः (*enaḥ*), from the root इण् (*iṇ*) "to go", is explained as what unfailingly comes and joins at the proper time. Accordingly Sāyaṇa explains that sin checks the force of meritorious action and brings the creature into trouble.

**अग्ने (*agne*)** — "पृथिव्याः ज्वालायाः महिना चित् एनः महे संदशस्य विस्तीर्णायाः ज्वालायाः महत्वेन यत्किंचित् पापं महते यजमानाय मह्यं संक्षपय (*pṛthivyāḥ jvālāyāḥ mahinā cit enaḥ mahe saṃdaśasya vistīrṇāyāḥ jvālāyāḥ mahatvena yatkiṃcit pāpaṃ mahate yajamānāya mahyaṃ saṃkṣapaya*)"

### Printed p. 45

*[Running head, p. 45: [A. 3. A. 1. V. 2.] Ṛgvedasaṃhitā 45]*

Addressing Agni, the prayer is that he destroy the sacrificer's sins by the greatness of his abundant flames.

The Nirukta-writers explain the word Agni thus: अग्निः कस्मात् अग्रणीर्भवति अग्रं यज्ञेषु प्रणेयते अंगं नयति सन्नममानः (*agniḥ kasmāt agraṇīrbhavati agraṃ yajñeṣu praṇeyate aṃgaṃ nayati sannamamānaḥ*). Durgācārya explains the analysis अग्रं नयति अग्रणीः भवति (*agraṃ nayati agraṇīḥ bhavati*): the word अग्र (*agra*) means "chief"; सर्वेष्टर्थेषु असावात्मानं अग्रं नयति सेनां वा अग्रे नयति अग्निर्वैदेवानां सेनानीः इति श्रुतेः (*sarveṣṭartheṣu asāvātmānaṃ agraṃ nayati senāṃ vā agre nayati agnirvaidevānāṃ senānīḥ iti śruteḥ*). He takes the first place in all necessary duties; or, being the commander of the gods' armies, Agni leads the gods' army. This is one kind of meaning. Secondly, in sacrifices the fire is prepared first, before all necessary duties; hence the meaning अग्रं प्रणीयते इति अग्निः (*agraṃ praṇīyate iti agniḥ*). Thirdly, wherever he enters he himself is chief and brings all else under his control; hence अंगं नयतीति अग्निः (*aṃgaṃ nayatīti agniḥ*). In all worldly and Vedic works, and even in grass and wood, when Agni has entered and is connected, he keeps the position of being chief; whatever is connected with Agni stays subordinate to him. This is the purport.

The teacher Sthaulāṣṭhivi [sic, as supplied] says अकोपनो भवति न स्नेहयति (*akopano bhavati na snehayati*), and describes Agni's quality: he makes the thing that joins him (that is connected with him) take another form. The teacher Śākapūṇi explains the formation of the word Agni: it is from इतात् (*itāt*), अक्ताद्वा दग्धाद्वा नीतात् (*aktādvā dagdhādvā nītāt*), that is, from the three roots इण् गत् (*iṇ gat*), अंजू व्यक्तिश्लक्ष्ययोः (*aṃjū vyaktiślakṣyayoḥ*), दह भस्मीकरणे (*daha bhasmīkaraṇe*), with the element नी (*nī*) from the root णीज् प्रापणे (*ṇīj prāpaṇe*) added, the form अग्नि (*agni*) results. The Nirukta-writers quote the Ṛk

> ⟦अग्निमीळे पुरोहितं यज्ञस्य देवमृत्वजं । होतारं रत्नधातमं ॥⟧ (Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-1-1)

> *⟦agnimīḷe purohitaṃ yajñasya devamṛtvajaṃ | hotāraṃ ratnadhātamaṃ ‖⟧ (Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-1-1)*

and say that this Agni is called the terrestrial fire, while the other forms of Agni, such as Jātavedas, are spoken of as middle and highest. In this terrestrial fire the sacrificers invoke the other fires according to the respective rites, to suit their sacrifice. Hence Agni has the identity of all the gods; the Brāhmaṇa sentence अग्निः सर्वा देवताः (*agniḥ sarvā devatāḥ*) and

### Printed p. 46

*[Running head, p. 46: 46 Sāyaṇabhāṣyasahitā [Maṃ. 3. A. 1. Sū. 7.]]*

> ⟦इंद्रं मित्रं वरुणमग्निमाहुरथो दिव्यः स सुपर्णो गरुत्मान् । एकं सद्विप्रा बहुधा वदंत्यग्निं यमं मातरिश्वानमाहुः ॥⟧ (Ma. [sic, as supplied] Saṃ. 1-164-46)

> *⟦iṃdraṃ mitraṃ varuṇamagnimāhuratho divyaḥ sa suparṇo garutmān | ekaṃ sadviprā bahudhā vadaṃtyagniṃ yamaṃ mātariśvānamāhuḥ ‖⟧ (Ma. [sic, as supplied] Saṃ. 1-164-46)*

this mantra too make clear that Agni has the nature of all the gods.

But: यस्तु सूक्तं भजते यस्मै हविर्निरुप्यते आयमेव सोऽग्निः। निपातमेवैते उत्तरे ज्योतिषी एतेन नामधेयेन भजेते (*yastu sūktaṃ bhajate yasmai havirnirupyate āyameva so 'gniḥ| nipātamevaite uttare jyotiṣī etena nāmadheyena bhajete*) (Ni. 2-7-18). The Agni who is chiefly praised in the various rites, and for whom oblations are prepared with Vedic mantras, is spoken of as the terrestrial fire. The other fires, though in the position of highest and middle (the lightning fire, Jātavedas), are not so spoken of in the world by sentences such as अग्निमानय (*agnimānaya*) ("bring fire"); and अग्नियेषु सूक्तेषु हविः संप्रदानेषु स्तुत्यत्वेन भोक्तृत्वेन चैतदेवोपासीत (*agniyeṣu sūkteṣu haviḥ saṃpradāneṣu stutyatvena bhoktṛtvena caitadevopāsīta*): this terrestrial fire, which is praised in the hymns that describe Agni and is the recipient in the giving of oblations, is to be worshipped, so the injunction says. तस्मादग्निशब्देन पार्थिवस्य ज्योतिषः मुख्यस्संबंधः गौण इतरयोर्मध्यमोत्तमयोः (*tasmādagniśabdena pārthivasya jyotiṣaḥ mukhyassaṃbaṃdhaḥ gauṇa itarayormadhyamottamayoḥ*). Hence the chief sense of the word Agni is that the terrestrial fire is worshipped; the middle and highest rank of the other deities, designated through Agni's all-selfhood, is secondary here, as Durgācārya explains.

**चित् (*cit*)** — The Nirukta-writers say अप्युपमार्थेऽपि कर्मोपसंग्रहार्थेऽपि पदपूरणाः (*apyupamārthe 'pi karmopasaṃgrahārthe 'pi padapūraṇāḥ*): particles may convey comparison, or some meaning, or merely fill out the sentence — three uses. Then चिदितैषो अनेककर्मा, आचार्यश्चिदिदं ब्रूयादिति पूजायां, दधिचिदित्युपमार्थे, कुल्माषांश्चिदाहरेति अवकुत्सिते (*ciditaiṣo anekakarmā, ācāryaścididaṃ brūyāditi pūjāyāṃ, dadhicidityupamārthe, kulmāṣāṃścidāhareti avakutsite*): the particle चित् (*cit*) has several functions: comparison in ददि चित् (*dadi cit*) [sic, as supplied], respect in आचार्यश्चित् (*ācāryaścit*), contempt (disparagement) in कुल्माषांश्चित् (*kulmāṣāṃścit*). In places other than these it conveys only the general filling-out of the sentence; the supplied text ends this passage with a sentence that it prints as "इल्ल पदपूरणफलकवागि अनर्थकवागुवुदु (*illa padapūraṇaphalakavāgi anarthakavāguvudu*)" (as supplied: it becomes meaningless as a mere filler).

**Vyākaraṇa-prakriyā (grammar)**

Grammar page begun, continuing on the next page. Headword पृक्षप्रयज: (*pṛkṣaprayaja:*): root यज (*yaja*) (worship, union, giving); the sūtra अन्येभ्योपिदृश्यंते (*anyebhyopidṛśyaṃte*) (Pā. 3-2-75) is cited, from whose word दृशि (*dṛśi*) the affix विच् (*vic*) in the passive sense is derived for this root; the text breaks off at the page end.

### Printed p. 47

*[Running head, p. 47: [A. 3. A. 1. P. 2.] Ṛgvedasaṃhitā 47]*

Grammar page (Vyākaraṇa-prakriyā) continued from the previous page, noted briefly (prose, no Ṛk). It begins with the affix and the bahuvrīhi compound of पृक्षप्रयजः (*pṛkṣaprayajaḥ*) (the sūtra cited is Pā. 6-2-1, giving the prakṛti accent of the first member). Words then treated: द्रविणः (*draviṇaḥ*) (root द्रु (*dru*), Uṇ. 2-208, affix इनन् (*inan*); the nighāta accent in the vocative by Pā. 8-1919 as supplied); सुवाचः (*suvācaḥ*) (Pā. 6-2-162, accent on the last syllable of the second member); सुकृतवः (*sukṛtavaḥ*) [sic, as supplied; the Saṃhitā word is सुकेतवः (*suketavaḥ*)]; रेवत् (*revat*) (Pā. 5-2-94, the affix मतुप् (*matup*), Pā. 8-1-15, Pā. 6-1-176); ऊषुः (*ūṣuḥ*) (root उष (*uṣa*), Pā. 3-4-6, lit; nighāta accent); पृथिव्याः (*pṛthivyāḥ*) (Uṇ. 1-148, Pā. 4-1-41, Pā. 6-1-174); महे (*mahe*) (root मह (*maha*), Pā. 6-1-168); and the beginning of दशस्य (*daśasya*) (Pā. 3-1-27, the affix यक् (*yak*)), which continues on the next page.
