The grammar note of दशस्य (*daśasya*) ends at the top of the page (the imperative middle-person singular, सिप् (*sip*) replaced by हि (*hi*), then loss of हि (*hi*) by Pā. 6-4-105; the tiṅanta nighāta accent).

#### Ṛk 11

**॥ संहितापाठः ॥**

> इळा॑मग्ने पुरु॒दंसं॑ स॒निं गोः श॑श्वत्त॒मं हव॑मानाय साध ।
> स्यान्नः॑ सू॒नुस्तन॑यो वि॒जावाग्ने॒ सा ते॑ सुम॒तिर्भू॑त्व॒स्मे ॥ ११ ॥

**॥ पदपाठः ॥**

(The Pada reads तश्वर्तमं (*taśvartamaṃ*) where the Saṃhitā and the bhāṣya read शश्वत्तमं (*śaśvattamaṃ*); noted as supplied.)

> इळां । अग्ने । पुरुदंसं । सनिं । गोः । तश्वर्तमं । हवमानाय । साध ।
> स्यात् । नः । सूनुः । तनयः । विजावा । अग्ने । सा । ते । सुमतिः । भूतु ।
> अस्मे इति ॥ ११ ॥

**॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥**

> हे अग्ने पुरुदंसं । अपोऽप्रो दंसो वेष इति कर्मनामसु पठितश्वा-
> द्वंसःशब्दः कर्मवाची । पुरूणि बहूनि दंसांसि कर्माणि यस्याः सा । तां बहुक-
> र्माणं गोः सनिं गवादिपशून्संपादयित्रीमिळामेतन्नामिकां गोरूपां देवतां
> शश्वत्तमं निरंतरं हवमानाय यजमानाय मह्यं साध : साधय । किंच नोऽस्माकु
> सूनुः पुत्रस्तनयः पौत्रः स्याद्भवत्त्विति ते तव या सुमतिः शोभना बुद्धिः सा विजा-
> वाबंध्या सत्यस्मे अस्माकं भूतु भवतु ॥ शश्वत्तमं । उच्चादिसु पाठादंतोदा-
> त्तत्वं । साध । राध साध संसिद् । अंतर्भावितण्यर्थोऽयं । लोट व्यत्ययेन
> शप् । सेर्ह्यपिच्छेति हिरादेशः । तस्यातो हेंति लुक् । निघातः । विजाना । जनी
> प्रादुर्भावे । आस्वादन्येभ्योऽपि दृश्यंत इति वनिष् । विड्वनोनुनासिकस्यादित्यात्वं ।
> कृदुत्तरपदप्रकृतिस्वरत्वं । लिंगव्यत्ययः । भूतु । भू सत्तायां । बहुलं छंदसीति
> शपो लुक् । भूसुवोस्तिजीति गुणप्रतिषेधः ॥

*Gist: Agni is asked to grant the sacrificer the cow-formed deity Iḷā, giver of cattle and doer of many works, perpetually; and that sons and grandsons be ours and his good will be fruitful for us. The grammatical tail treats śaśvattamam, sādha, vijāvā and bhūtu; transcribed, not explained.*

**प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)**

(O) Agni (अग्ने (*agne*)): endowed with many works (पुरुदंसं (*purudaṃsaṃ*)), and the giver of cattle such as cows (गोः सनिं (*goḥ saniṃ*)), the deity named Iḷā in the form of a cow (इळां (*iḷāṃ*)); perpetually (शश्वत्तमं (*śaśvattamaṃ*)), to me who am performing the sacrifice (हवमानाय (*havamānāya*)), accomplish (grant) (साध (*sādha*)). To us (नः (*naḥ*)), may there be a son (सूनुः (*sūnuḥ*)) and a grandson (तनयः (*tanayaḥ*)) (so) (स्यात् (*syāt*)). O Agni (अग्ने (*agne*)), whatever good will (सुमतिः (*sumatiḥ*)) you have (ते (*te*)), that (सा (*sā*)) for our sake (अस्मे (*asme*)) may be unbarren (विजावा (*vijāvā*)), may it become so (भूतु (*bhūtu*)).

**भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)**

O Agni, make the earth, named Iḷā and in the form of a cow, who gives cattle and the like that are helpful to many rites, favourable to us who are engaged in the continuous performance of sacrifice; and, O Agni, may that gracious intention of yours to bestow sons, grandsons and the like on the worshippers be ever unbarren (fruitful) for us.

**Printed English translation of the Ṛk**

Grant, Agni, to the offerer of the oblation, earth the bestower of cattle, the means of many (pious rites), such that it may be perpetual : may there be to us sons and grandsons, and may your good-will ever be productive of benefits to us.

### Printed p. 49

*[Running head, p. 49: [A. 3. A. 1. V. 2.] Ṛgvedasaṃhitā 49]*

**विशेषविषयगळु (Viśeṣaviṣayagaḷu)**

**ಪುರುದಂಸಂ** — पुरूणि बहूनि दंसांसि कर्माणि यस्याः सा (*purūṇi bahūni daṃsāṃsi karmāṇi yasyāḥ sā*). The word दंस (*daṃsa*) is stated among the names of action, listed beginning अप्पः दंसः (*appaḥ daṃsaḥ*) (as supplied; the text says "twenty-six", इप्पात्तारु (*ippāttāru*), names of action) (Ni. 3-2). The meaning is "possessed of many works".

**ಗೋಃ ಸನಿಂ** — गवादिपर्शू संपादयित्रीं (*gavādiparśū saṃpādayitrīṃ*) [sic, as supplied]. The word गो (*go*) has many meanings: earth, speech, animal, ray. Since the words गोः सनिं (*goḥ saniṃ*) are epithets of the word इळा (*iḷā*), the sense is described as: the deity Iḷā bestows everything, cows and the rest.

**ಇಳಾಂ** — एतन्नामिकां गोरूपां देवतां (*etannāmikāṃ gorūpāṃ devatāṃ*). The word इळा (*iḷā*) is stated among the names of the earth (Ni. 2-5), also among the names of speech (Ni. 2-23), of food (Ni. 3-9) and of the cow (Ni. 3-9). The word इळा (*iḷā*) is also formed from the root ईश (*īśa*) (as supplied). In the Ṛks

अभिन इळा यूथस्य मातास्मन्नदीभिरुर्वशी वा गृणातु । उर्वशी वा बृहद्दिवा गृणानाभ्यूर्वाना प्रथृथस्यायोः । सिषक्तु न ऊर्जव्यस्य पुष्टेः ॥ (*abhina iḷā yūthasya mātāsmannadībhirurvaśī vā gṛṇātu | urvaśī vā bṛhaddivā gṛṇānābhyūrvānā prathṛthasyāyoḥ | siṣaktu na ūrjavyasya puṣṭeḥ ‖*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 5-41-19, 20) [the supplied text has a stray fragment "(ಋ. ಸ" before the reference]

the goddess Iḷā or Iḍā is described as a deity of the middle region. Sāyaṇa explains the word as the deity named Iḷā who has taken the form of a cow.

### Printed p. 50

*[Running head, p. 50: 50 Sāyaṇabhāṣyasahitā [Maṃ. 3. A. 1. Sū. 7.]]*

*[Special Topics of Ṛk 11 continued from the previous page.]*

**ಶಶ್ವತ್ತಮಂ** — शश्वत्तमं हवमानाय साध (*śaśvattamaṃ havamānāya sādha*): निरंतरं हवमानाय यजमानाय मह्यं साधय (*niraṃtaraṃ havamānāya yajamānāya mahyaṃ sādhaya*). Agni is prayed that the favour of the deity Iḷā, in the form of a cow, who fulfils the desired ends of the sacrificer who continually gratifies Agni with oblations, and who is full of abundant greatness, may come about.

**ಸೂನುಃ** — सूनुः-पुत्रः, तनयः पौत्रः (*sūnuḥ-putraḥ, tanayaḥ pautraḥ*). Among the fifteen names of offspring (apatya-nāmas) beginning with tuk, toka (Ni. 3-2), both the word tanaya and the word sūnu are read. In the Ṛk

एता विश्वा सवना तूतुमा कृषे स्वयं सूनो सहसो यानि दधिषे । वराय ते पात्रं धर्मणे तना यज्ञो मंत्रो ब्रहोद्यतं वचः ॥ (*etā viśvā savanā tūtumā kṛṣe svayaṃ sūno sahaso yāni dadhiṣe | varāya te pātraṃ dharmaṇe tanā yajño maṃtro brahodyataṃ vacaḥ ‖*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 10-50-6)

the meaning "son" is given for the word सूनो (*sūno*), and Agni is called सहससूनो (*sahasasūno*), "son of strength". Sāyaṇa explains the word sūnu as "son" and the word tanaya as "grandson" (son's son).

**ಸುಮತಿಃ** — सुमतिः—शोभना बुद्धिः (*sumatiḥ—śobhanā buddhiḥ*). The author of the Nirukta, at देवसुमतिं (*devasumatiṃ*), explains it as देवनां कल्याणीं मतिं (*devanāṃ kalyāṇīṃ matiṃ*), the auspicious mind of the gods; at वैश्वानरस्य सुमत् स्याम (*vaiśvānarasya sumat syāma*) as कल्याण्यां मत् (*kalyāṇyāṃ mat*); and at वयं देवस्य धीमहि सुमतिं सत्यधर्मणः (*vayaṃ devasya dhīmahi sumatiṃ satyadharmaṇaḥ*) also कल्याणीं मतिं (*kalyāṇīṃ matiṃ*), the same sense. Sāyaṇa's remark concerns the sacrificers who have gratified Agni with oblations; the prayer is that there may arise Agni's auspicious mind, namely the birth and increase of the sons, grandsons and the like that Agni can bestow. (The sentence is printed as supplied; its construction is loose.)

**Vyākaraṇa-prakriyā (grammar)**

Grammar page, noted briefly. Words treated: पुरुदंसम् (*purudaṃsam*) (bahuvrīhi: she of whom there are many works; Pā. 6-2-119 gives the accent on the last syllable of the second member); शश्वत्तमम् (*śaśvattamam*) (read in the uṇ-group, so by Pā. 6-1-160 the final-syllable accent); साध (*sādha*) (root rādh/sādh, sense of accomplishing; causative sense included; imperative second person singular, sip; śap by Pā. 3-4-87; loss of hi by Pā. 6-4-105; nighāta accent); स्यात् (*syāt*) (root as, optative third person singular; no unaccented form since it begins the pāda; yāsuṭ is accented).

### Printed p. 51

*[Running head, p. 51: [A. 3. A. 1. V. 3.] Ṛgvedasaṃhitā 51]*

Grammar page continued, noted briefly. विजावा (*vijāvā*) (root janī, to be born; affix vanip by Pā. 3-2-57; root vowel ā by Pā. 6-4-41; compound with vi; nominative singular; gender change; accent of the second member of a kṛt compound); सुमतिः (*sumatiḥ*) (root man, affix ktin; Pā. 3-3-96 as printed; the sūtra reference is as supplied); भूतु (*bhūtu*) (root bhū, imperative third person singular, tip; ukāra substitution; luk of the vikaraṇa śap by the "bahulaṃ chandasi" rule; prohibition of guṇa; nighāta accent); अस्मे (*asme*) (by the "supāṃ suluk" rule, the substitution śe in the sixth(?) plural, as supplied).

## Sūkta 8 (Maṇḍala 3)

**The eighth sūkta**

**Pīṭhikā (Introduction)**

In this sūkta the sacrificial post (yūpa) is praised. Since the setting up of the yūpa and its consecrations have special importance in the sacrifice, they will be explained here in a special manner. The yūpa is the wooden pillar planted in the sacrifice for binding the animal. It is also called vanaspati; in the Ṛgveda the word vanaspati is the one more used. The word yūpa is used in only five Ṛks, namely यूपः (*yūpaḥ*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-51-14); यूपवाहाः (*yūpavāhāḥ*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-162-6); यूपव्रस्काः (*yūpavraskāḥ*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-162-6); यूपेव (*yūpeva*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 4-33-3); यूपात् (*yūpāt*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 5-2-7); nowhere else. In the Yajurveda and the like the use of the word yūpa is frequent. Yāska too gives no nirvacana of the word yūpa. Regarding the word vanaspati, when explaining the Ṛk मेद्यंतु ते वह्नयो येभिरीयसे (*medyaṃtu te vahnayo yebhirīyase*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 2-37-3), he says:

वनस्पत इत्येनमाहैष हि वनानां पाता वा पालयिका वा । वनं वनोशेः । (*vanaspata ityenamāhaiṣa hi vanānāṃ pātā vā pālayikā vā | vanaṃ vanośeḥ |*) (Ni. 8-3)

Durgācārya, commenting on this, says:

### Printed p. 52

*[Running head, p. 52: 52 Sāyaṇabhāṣyasahitā [Maṃ. 3. A. 1. Sū. 8.]]*

यस्मादेषः वनानां वृक्षादीनां अंतर्गतोऽपि समर्थोऽपि दग्गुं न तानि दहति तस्मादयं तेषां पाता रक्षिता पालयिता वेति । (*yasmādeṣaḥ vanānāṃ vṛkṣādīnāṃ aṃtargato 'pi samartho 'pi dagguṃ na tāni dahati tasmādayaṃ teṣāṃ pātā rakṣitā pālayitā veti |*)

That is, Agni, although he is inside trees and the like, does not burn them, and so is called their protector. Here vanaspati is explained as Agni. We have explained this matter at length in commenting on the Āprī sūkta सुसमिद्दो न आ वह (*susamiddo na ā vaha*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-13) (see Ṛgveda Saṃhitā Part 2, pp. 576 and 577). Or, following the derivation वनं वनोतेः (*vanaṃ vanoteḥ*) of the word vana, he says that the tree (vṛkṣa) is used for various purposes, the word being formed from the root vanati/sambhajati ("to resort to, to serve").

Regarding the yūpa there is an old story in the Aitareya Brāhmaṇa (Ai. Brā. 2-1). Its account is given here:

यज्ञेन वै देवा ऊर्ध्वाः स्वर्गं लोकमायंस्तेबिभयुरिमं नो दृष्ट्वा मनुष्याश्च ऋषयश्चानु प्रज्ञास्यंतीति तं वै यूपेनैवायोपयिंस्तं यद्यूपेन्वैवायेपयंस्तद्यूपस्य यूपत्वं तमवाचीनाग्रं निमित्योर्ध्वा उदायंस्ततो वै मनुष्याश्च ऋषयश्च देवानां यज्ञवास्त्वभ्यायन्यज्ञस्य किंचिदेषिष्यामः प्रज्ञात्या इति ते वै यूपमेवाविंदन्न वाचीनाग्रं निमितं ते विदुरनेन वै देवा यज्ञमयूयुपन्निति तमुत्थायोर्ध्वं न्यमिन्वंस्ततो वै ते प्रयज्ञमजानन्न स्वर्गं लोकं । तद्यद्यूप ऊर्ध्वो निमिषायते यज्ञस्य प्रज्ञात्यै स्वर्गस्यलोकस्यानुख्यात्यै ॥ (*yajñena vai devā ūrdhvāḥ svargaṃ lokamāyaṃstebibhayurimaṃ no dṛṣṭvā manuṣyāśca ṛṣayaścānu prajñāsyaṃtīti taṃ vai yūpenaivāyopayiṃstaṃ yadyūpenvaivāyepayaṃstadyūpasya yūpatvaṃ tamavācīnāgraṃ nimityordhvā udāyaṃstato vai manuṣyāśca ṛṣayaśca devānāṃ yajñavāstvabhyāyanyajñasya kiṃcideṣiṣyāmaḥ prajñātyā iti te vai yūpamevāviṃdanna vācīnāgraṃ nimitaṃ te viduranena vai devā yajñamayūyupanniti tamutthāyordhvaṃ nyaminvaṃstato vai te prayajñamajānanna svargaṃ lokaṃ | tadyadyūpa ūrdhvo nimiṣāyate yajñasya prajñātyai svargasyalokasyānukhyātyai ‖*)

(Explanation.) In former times the gods performed the sacrifice called Jyotiṣṭoma and by its power went to the world of heaven. Seeing this sacrificial procedure of the gods, the gods feared that men and seers too would perform sacrifice in the same way and come to heaven, and so they devised a means. To bewilder men and seers, the gods planted a sacrificial post in their sacrifice. Because this post was planted so that the sacrifice might seem fully performed, it got the name yūpa (yūpa meaning "obstruct, make it not come"). When the gods were going to heaven they planted the top (tip) of the yūpa upside down. Afterwards men and seers came there, looked, and found only the yūpa planted upside down. What is its use? Why was it planted? None of these details of the sacrifice were understood. Thinking that the gods must have planted the yūpa upside down in order to deceive them, that is, so that the details of the sacrifice might not be known to them, they pulled it out and planted it correctly, with the top upward. Thereupon [the printed page ends here; the sentence continues on the next page]. ("Mamaṣyarū", sic, for "men", is as supplied.)

### Printed p. 53

*[Running head, p. 53: [A. 3. A. 1. V. 3.] Ṛgvedasaṃhitā 53]*

*[Continuation of the Pīṭhikā of Sūkta 8 from the previous page.]*

Thereupon men and seers came to know the order of the sacrifice and the like, and they too performed the sacrifice duly and obtained the world of heaven. Therefore, so that the sacrificial rite may proceed correctly and heaven may be gained by it, there is the injunction that the yūpa must be planted with its top upward.

> वज्रो वा एष यद्यूपः सोऽष्टाश्रिः कर्तव्योऽष्टाश्रिर्वै वज्रस्तं तं
> प्रहरति द्विषते भ्रातृव्याय वधं योऽस्य स्तृत्यस्तस्मै स्तरै ।
> वज्रो वै यूपः स एष द्विषतो वध उद्यतस्तिष्ठति तस्माद्दाप्येतर्हि
> यो द्वेषि तस्याप्रियं भवत्यमुष्यायं यूपोऽमुष्यायं
> यूप इति दृष्ट्वा ।

> *vajro vā eṣa yadyūpaḥ so 'ṣṭāśriḥ kartavyo 'ṣṭāśrirvai vajrastaṃ taṃ*
> *praharati dviṣate bhrātṛvyāya vadhaṃ yo 'sya stṛtyastasmai starai |*
> *vajro vai yūpaḥ sa eṣa dviṣato vadha udyatastiṣṭhati tasmāddāpyetarhi*
> *yo dveṣi tasyāpriyaṃ bhavatyamuṣyāyaṃ yūpo 'muṣyāyaṃ*
> *yūpa iti dṛṣṭvā |*

The yūpa is said to be a part of the thunderbolt weapon (vajra). On this subject there is the śruti sentence of the Taittirīya of the Yajurveda: इंद्रो वृत्राय वज्रं प्राहरत्स श्रेधा व्यभवत्स्फुतीयं यूपस्तृतीयं (*iṃdro vṛtrāya vajraṃ prāharatsa średhā vyabhavatsphutīyaṃ yūpastṛtīyaṃ*). The thunderbolt has eight edges; therefore the yūpa must be made with eight edges (corners or angles). Just as one kills one's enemy with the thunderbolt, so the yūpa too is a cause of the destruction of the enemy. The yūpa stands upright, facing upward, for the slaying of the foe. Therefore, if an enemy of the sacrificer who performs the sacrifice sees the yūpa standing upright, evil will certainly befall him. (The supplied text reads "ಯೂದವನ್ನು" in the first sentence of this page and "ಸ್ಫುತೀಯಂ" for the quotation; printed as supplied.)

> खादिरं यूपं कुर्वीत स्वर्गकामः खादिरेण वै यूपेन देवाः
> स्वर्गं लोकमजयंस्तथैवैतद्यजमानः खादिरेण यूपेन स्वर्गं
> लोकं जयति ॥

> *khādiraṃ yūpaṃ kurvīta svargakāmaḥ khādireṇa vai yūpena devāḥ*
> *svargaṃ lokamajayaṃstathaivaitadyajamānaḥ khādireṇa yūpena svargaṃ*
> *lokaṃ jayati ‖*

It is the custom to make this yūpa from various kinds of tree; there is a rule that if it is made from such and such a tree, such and such a fruit is obtained. That is explained here. Sacrificers desiring heaven must make the yūpa from the particular tree called khādira. The gods made the yūpa of khādira wood in their sacrifice and so obtained the world of heaven. Therefore sacrificers who desire heaven, if they sacrifice with a khādira yūpa, will obtain the world of heaven.

> बैल्वं यूपं कुर्वीतान्नाद्यकामः पुष्टिकामः समां समां वै
> बिल्वो गृभीतस्तदन्नाद्यस्य रूपमामूलाच्छाखाभिरनुचितस्तत्पुष्टेः
> पुष्यति प्रजां च पशूंश्च य एवं विद्वान्पैल्वं यूपं कुरुते ॥

> *bailvaṃ yūpaṃ kurvītānnādyakāmaḥ puṣṭikāmaḥ samāṃ samāṃ vai*
> *bilvo gṛbhītastadannādyasya rūpamāmūlācchākhābhiranucitastatpuṣṭeḥ*
> *puṣyati prajāṃ ca paśūṃśca ya evaṃ vidvānpailvaṃ yūpaṃ kurute ‖*

One who desires food (nourishment) and prosperity must make the yūpa from the bilva tree, because the bilva tree bears fruit every year. The bilva tree is a sign of food and prosperity. From the root to the tip all its branches produce increase every year, that is, it keeps growing large; therefore this tree is a sign of prosperity.

### Printed p. 54

*[Running head, p. 54: 54 Sāyaṇabhāṣyasahitā [Maṃ. 3. A. 1. Sū. 8.]]*

Therefore the man who knows this matter well and performs the sacrifice after making the yūpa from the bilva tree will have his progeny (children and the like) and cattle endowed with prosperity.

> यदेव बैल्वां ३ बिल्वं ज्योतिरिति वा आचक्षते ज्योतिः स्वेषु भवति
> श्रेष्ठः स्वानां भवति य एवं वेद ॥

> *yadeva bailvāṃ 3 bilvaṃ jyotiriti vā ācakṣate jyotiḥ sveṣu bhavati*
> *śreṣṭhaḥ svānāṃ bhavati ya evaṃ veda ‖*

Among these bilva trees there is a special kind called the śrīvṛkṣa or jyotirvṛkṣa. If one makes the yūpa from such a tree and sacrifices, splendour arises. The sacrificer who knows this and sacrifices after making the yūpa from this particular kind of tree becomes full of splendour and the foremost (esteemed) among his own people.

> पालाशं यूपं कुर्वीत तेजस्कामो ब्रह्मवर्चसकामस्तेजो वै ब्रह्म-
> वर्चसं ऎनस्पतीनां पलाशस्तेजस्वी ब्रह्मवर्चसी भवति य एवं
> विद्वान्पालाशं यूपं कुरुते यदेव पालाशां ३ सर्वॆषां वा एव
> वनस्पतीनां योनिर्यत्पलाशस्तस्मात्पलाशस्यैव पलाशेनाचक्षतेऽमुष्य
> पलाशममुष्य पलाशमिति सर्वॆषां हास्य वनस्पतीनां काम उपापो
> भवति य एवं वेद ॥

> *pālāśaṃ yūpaṃ kurvīta tejaskāmo brahmavarcasakāmastejo vai brahma-*
> *varcasaṃ ènaspatīnāṃ palāśastejasvī brahmavarcasī bhavati ya evaṃ*
> *vidvānpālāśaṃ yūpaṃ kurute yadeva pālāśāṃ 3 sarvèṣāṃ vā eva*
> *vanaspatīnāṃ yoniryatpalāśastasmātpalāśasyaiva palāśenācakṣate 'muṣya*
> *palāśamamuṣya palāśamiti sarvèṣāṃ hāsya vanaspatīnāṃ kāma upāpo*
> *bhavati ya evaṃ veda ‖*

One who desires splendour (tejas) and spiritual radiance (brahmavarcasa) must make the yūpa from the palāśa (mutta) tree. The palāśa tree is the source of all the trees of the forest (vanaspati), and among them is the most full of splendour and of spiritual radiance. Therefore any man who knows this and uses in the sacrifice a yūpa made of palāśa wood becomes endowed with splendour and with spiritual radiance. (The supplied text writes "ಯೂಸವನ್ನು" for the word yūpa here and just below; as supplied.)

This yūpa is smeared with ghee (clarified butter) and certain consecrations are performed with mantras. This procedure is explained in the Aitareya Brāhmaṇa (Ai. Brā. 2-2) as follows:

> अंजो यूपमनु ब्रूहीत्याहाध्वर्युः। अंजंति त्वामध्वरे देवयंत
> इत्यन्वाहाद्वरे है नं देवयंतोऽज्ञांति । वनस्पते मधुना दैव्ये-
> नेत्येतद्वै मधु दैव्यं यदाज्यं यदूर्ध्वस्तिष्ठा द्रविणेह धत्ता-
> द्यद्वा क्षयो मातुरस्या उपस्थ इति यदि च तिष्ठासि यदि च शयास्यै
> द्रविणमेवास्मासु धत्तादित्येव तदाह ॥

> *aṃjo yūpamanu brūhītyāhādhvaryuḥ| aṃjaṃti tvāmadhvare devayaṃta*
> *ityanvāhādvare hai naṃ devayaṃto 'jñāṃti | vanaspate madhunā daivye-*
> *netyetadvai madhu daivyaṃ yadājyaṃ yadūrdhvastiṣṭhā draviṇeha dhattā-*
> *dyadvā kṣayo māturasyā upastha iti yadi ca tiṣṭhāsi yadi ca śayāsyai*
> *draviṇamevāsmāsu dhattādityeva tadāha ‖*

When the consecration of anointing the yūpa with ghee is being performed, the Adhvaryu, addressing the Hotṛ, utters the Praiṣa mantra: "O Hotṛ, we shall now anoint the yūpa with ghee; for that recite the mantra of anointing the yūpa." This matter is set out in the Āpastamba Śrauta Sūtra in the words यूपायाज्यमानायानुब्रूहीति संप्रेष्यते, आज्यमानायानुब्रूहीति, (*yūpāyājyamānāyānubrūhīti saṃpreṣyate, ājyamānāyānubrūhīti,*) [continued on the next page]

### Printed p. 55

*[Running head, p. 55: [A. 3. A. 1. V. 3.] Ṛgvedasaṃhitā 55]*

अंज्यो यूपमनुब्रूहीति वा (*aṃjyo yūpamanubrūhīti vā*) [completing the sūtra quotation begun on the previous page]. And the manner of anointing is also stated there, in the words अथैनमसंस्कृतेनाज्येन यजमानोग्रतः शकलेनानक्तैंद्रमसीति चषालमंक्त्वा सुपिप्पलाभ्यस्तौषधीभ्य इति प्रतिमुच्य देवस्त्या सविता मध्यानक्किति स्रुवेण संततमविच्छिंदन्नग्निष्ठामश्रिमॆनक्ति (*athainamasaṃskṛtenājyena yajamānogrataḥ śakalenānaktaiṃdramasīti caṣālamaṃktvā supippalābhyastauṣadhībhya iti pratimucya devastyā savitā madhyānakkiti sruveṇa saṃtatamavicchiṃdannagniṣṭhāmaśrimènakti*) (the text is given as supplied; some words look garbled). As soon as the Adhvaryu has uttered the Praiṣa in this way, the Hotṛ recites the Ṛk अंजंति त्वामध्वरे देवयंतः (*aṃjaṃti tvāmadhvare devayaṃtaḥ*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 3-8-1), which is suited to the smearing with ghee. The meaning of this Ṛk is: "O yūpa, the priests anoint you with ghee fit for the gods. Whether you now stand upright in this place of sacrifice or lie on the ground, procure for us abundant wealth."

> उच्छयस्व वनस्पत इत्युछ्प्रियमाणायाभिरूपा यद्यज्ञेऽभिरूपं
> तत्समृद्धं वष्मन्‌पृथिव्या अधीत्येतद्वै वर्ष्म पृथिव्यै यत्र यूष-
> मुन्मिन्वंति सुमिती मीयमानो वर्चोधा यज्ञवाहस इत्याशिष-
> माशास्ते ॥

> *ucchayasva vanaspata ityuchpriyamāṇāyābhirūpā yadyajñe 'bhirūpaṃ*
> *tatsamṛddhaṃ vaṣmanpṛthivyā adhītyetadvai varṣma pṛthivyai yatra yūṣa-*
> *munminvaṃti sumitī mīyamāno varcodhā yajñavāhasa ityāśiṣa-*
> *māśāste ‖*

Next the Hotṛ recites the Ṛk उच्छयस्व वनस्पते (*ucchayasva vanaspate*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 3-8-3). Since this Ṛk is to be recited when the yūpa is being planted, it is suited to that act. Its meaning is: "O yūpa, rise up on the earth. Until now you have been lying on the earth. Now rise and stand up, make the sacrifice go forward, and bestow splendour on us." Because the word उच्छयस्व (*ucchayasva*) ("stand up") occurs in this Ṛk, its recital is appropriate to the occasion. By the recital of a mantra whose meaning is so apt to the occasion the sacrifice is well accomplished. The words पृथिव्या अधि (*pṛthivyā adhi*) indicate the state of the yūpa, which until then had not been planted and lay across the ground. The sentence सुमती मायमानो वर्चोधा यज्ञवाहसे (*sumatī māyamāno varcodhā yajñavāhase*) (as supplied) indicates a prayer for welfare, that splendour may come to the sacrificer who performs the sacrifice.

> समिद्दस्य श्रयमाणः पुरस्तादिति समिद्दस्य ह्येष एतत्पुरस्ताच्छ्रयते
> ब्रह्मॆ वन्वानो अजरं सुवीरमित्याशिषमेवाशास्ते । आरे अस्मदमतिं
> बाधमान इत्यशनाया वै पाप्मामतिस्तामेव तदारान्नुदतेयज्ञाच्चि
> यजमानाच्च उच्छ्रयस्व महते सौभगायेत्याशिषमेवाशास्ते ॥

> *samiddasya śrayamāṇaḥ purastāditi samiddasya hyeṣa etatpurastācchrayate*
> *brahmè vanvāno ajaraṃ suvīramityāśiṣamevāśāste | āre asmadamatiṃ*
> *bādhamāna ityaśanāyā vai pāpmāmatistāmeva tadārānnudateyajñācci*
> *yajamānācca ucchrayasva mahate saubhagāyetyāśiṣamevāśāste ‖*

Then the Hotṛ recites the Ṛk समिद्धस्य श्रयमाणः पुरस्तात् (*samiddhasya śrayamāṇaḥ purastāt*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 3-8-2). Its meaning is: "You who shine in front of the blazing Agni here, bring this sacrificial work to completion, which produces excellent sons and the like and imperishable, immeasurable power. Standing erect and tall, drive away our enemies and bring us good fortune"; thus it is said [the sentence breaks off here; it continues on the next page].

### Printed p. 56

*[Running head, p. 56: 56 Sāyaṇabhāṣyasahitā [Maṃ. 3. A. 1. Sū. 8.]]*

[continuing from the previous page] The sentence समिद्धस्य श्रयमाणः पुरस्तात् (*samiddhasya śrayamāṇaḥ purastāt*) indicates that the yūpa post stands in front of the Agni that has just been kindled on the sacrificial altar and is blazing brightly. The sentence ब्रह्म वन्वानो अजरं सुवीरं (*brahma vanvāno ajaraṃ suvīraṃ*) is a prayer for the welfare of the sacrificer. The sentence अरे अस्मदमतिं बाधमानः (*are asmadamatiṃ bādhamānaḥ*) indicates the prayer that our enemies be driven far away; here the commentator gives the word अमतिं (*amatiṃ*) the meaning "hunger". The words यज्ञाच्च यजमानाच्च (*yajñācca yajamānācca*) indicate the prayer that there be no fear of enemies for the sacrificer and others at the place where the sacrifice is going on.

> ऊर्ध्व ऊ षु ण ऊतये तिष्ठा देवो न सवितेति यद्वै देवानां नेति
> तदेषामोमिति तिष्ठ देव इव सवितेत्येव तदाह ऊर्ध्व वाजस्य
> सनितेति वाजसनिमेवैनं तद्धनसां सनोति यदंजिभिर्वाघद्भिर्विह्वया-
> मह इति छंदांसि वा अंजयो वाघतस्मैरेतद्देवान्यजमाना विह्व-
> यंते मम यज्ञमागच्छत मम यज्ञमिति यदि ह वा अपि बहव इव
> यजंतेऽथ हास्य देवा यज्ञमेव गच्छंति यत्त्रैवं विद्वानेताम-
> न्वाह ॥

> *ūrdhva ū ṣu ṇa ūtaye tiṣṭhā devo na saviteti yadvai devānāṃ neti*
> *tadeṣāmomiti tiṣṭha deva iva savitetyeva tadāha ūrdhva vājasya*
> *saniteti vājasanimevainaṃ taddhanasāṃ sanoti yadaṃjibhirvāghadbhirvihvayā-*
> *maha iti chaṃdāṃsi vā aṃjayo vāghatasmairetaddevānyajamānā vihva-*
> *yaṃte mama yajñamāgacchata mama yajñamiti yadi ha vā api bahava iva*
> *yajaṃte 'tha hāsya devā yajñameva gacchaṃti yattraivaṃ vidvānetāma-*
> *nvāha ‖*

Next the Hotṛ recites the Ṛk ऊर्ध्व ऊ षु ण ऊतये (*ūrdhva ū ṣu ṇa ūtaye*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-36-13). Its meaning: "O yūpa, rise up like the god Sūrya and stand erect. When you have risen, while we invoke you with many kinds of metres, and the priests perform the sacrifice, anointing it with ghee, grant us food and other nourishment." In the sentence देवो न सविता (*devo na savitā*) the word न (*na*) is used in the sense of comparison (meaning "like"); देवो न सविता (*devo na savitā*) means "like the god Savitā, the Sun". The word ओं (*oṃ*) in the sentence तदेषामोमिति (*tadeṣāmomiti*) is to be understood likewise. The sentence ऊर्ध्व वाजस्य सनिता (*ūrdhva vājasya sanitā*) indicates "rise and be the giver of food to us", for the yūpa is prayed to bestow food. The sentence यदंजिभिर्वाघद्भिविह्वयामहे (*yadaṃjibhirvāghadbhivihvayāmahe*) suggests that the priests who smear the post with ghee call upon it with many kinds of metres. Here अंजयः (*aṃjayaḥ*) = छंदांसि (*chaṃdāṃsi*), i.e. Ṛks of various metres; and वाघद्धिः (*vāghaddhiḥ*) (as supplied) refers to the priests who, by the acts of the sacrifice, repeatedly invite the gods to "my sacrifice, to my sacrifice". For there are many sacrificers who perform sacrifices; the gods go only to the sacrifice of the one who invites them saying "come to my sacrifice, come to mine". The meaning is that the gods go to those Hotṛs who know this matter well and who invite them with mantras and the like.

### Printed p. 57

*[Running head, p. 57: [A. 3. A. 1. V. 3.] Ṛgvedasaṃhitā 57]*

> उर्ध्वो नः पाह्यंहसो नि केतुना विश्वं समत्रिणं दहेति रक्षांसि वै
> पाप्मात्रिणो रक्षांसि पाप्मानं दहेत्येव तवाह कृधी न ऊर्ध्वां
> चरथाय जीवस इति यदाह कृधी न ऊर्ध्वां चरणाय जीवस इत्येव
> तदाह यदि हवा अपि नीत इव यजमानो भवति परि हैवैनं तत्सं-
> वत्सराय ददाति विदा देवेषु नो दुव इत्याशिषमेवाशास्ते ॥

> *urdhvo naḥ pāhyaṃhaso ni ketunā viśvaṃ samatriṇaṃ daheti rakṣāṃsi vai*
> *pāpmātriṇo rakṣāṃsi pāpmānaṃ dahetyeva tavāha kṛdhī na ūrdhvāṃ*
> *carathāya jīvasa iti yadāha kṛdhī na ūrdhvāṃ caraṇāya jīvasa ityeva*
> *tadāha yadi havā api nīta iva yajamāno bhavati pari haivainaṃ tatsaṃ-*
> *vatsarāya dadāti vidā deveṣu no duva ityāśiṣamevāśāste ‖*

Then the Hotṛ recites the Ṛk ऊर्ध्व नः पाह्यंहसो नि केतुना (*ūrdhva naḥ pāhyaṃhaso ni ketunā*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-36-14). Its meaning: "O yūpa, rise and stand up, and protect us from distress. With your rays (your power) burn up and reduce to ashes the rākṣasas and the like. Make us too rise and move about. As messenger of the gods make known to the gods our oblations and the services we offer." Here रक्षांसि (*rakṣāṃsi*) means rākṣasas, or sins and evil deeds; burn them to ashes so that they do not trouble us. चरथाय जीवसे (*carathāya jīvase*) means "for moving about and for living". If the sacrificer is already afflicted by rākṣasas and the like, the prayer is that the affliction be removed and he be protected by the gift of life, so that he lives for a full year, that is, for a long time.

> जातो जायते सुदिनत्वे अह्नामिति जातो ह्येष एतज्जायते समर्य
> आ विदथे वर्धमान इति वर्धयंत्येवैनं तत्पुनंति धीरा अपसो
> मनीषेति पुनंत्येवैनं तद्देवया विप्र उदियर्ति वाचमिति देवेभ्य
> एवैनं तॆन्निवेदयति ।

> *jāto jāyate sudinatve ahnāmiti jāto hyeṣa etajjāyate samarya*
> *ā vidathe vardhamāna iti vardhayaṃtyevainaṃ tatpunaṃti dhīrā apaso*
> *manīṣeti punaṃtyevainaṃ taddevayā vipra udiyarti vācamiti devebhya*
> *evainaṃ tènnivedayati |*

Then the Hotṛ recites the Ṛk जातो जायते सुदिनत्वे अह्नां (*jāto jāyate sudinatve ahnāṃ*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 3-8-5). Its meaning: "This yūpa, once born, grows well and helps the sacrifices performed by men. The wise (the word is printed एवेकिगळादवरु (*evekigaḷādavaru*) in the supplied text) adorn this yūpa skilfully. Like a messenger of the gods, it raises its voice so that the gods may hear." In the sentence जातो जायते सुदिनत्वे अह्नां (*jāto jāyate sudinatve ahnāṃ*) the yūpa is first addressed, so the meaning is that it has now come into being. The word वर्धमान (*vardhamāna*) in समर्य आ विदथे वर्धमानः (*samarya ā vidathe vardhamānaḥ*) indicates that the yūpa has been raised up and made to stand. The sentence पुनंति धीरा अपसो मनीषा (*punaṃti dhīrā apaso manīṣā*) indicates that the intelligent priests purify this yūpa and consecrate it with the smearing of ghee and the like. The sentence देवया विप्र उदियर्ति वाचं (*devayā vipra udiyarti vācaṃ*), by the words वाचमुदियर्ति (*vācamudiyarti*), indicates that the yūpa is made ready here for the work of the gods.

> युवा सुवासाः परिवीत आगादित्युत्तमया परिदधाति स उ श्रेयान्भवति
> जायमान इति श्रेयांच्छ्रीयान् ह्येष एतद्भवति जायमानस्तं

*[At the foot of the page the supplied text has the stray numeral "8", apparently a sheet signature; the Brāhmaṇa passage continues on the next page.]*

### Printed p. 58

*[Running head, p. 58: 58 Sāyaṇabhāṣyasahitā [Maṃ. 3. A. 1. Sū. 8]]*

> धीरासः कवय उन्नयंति स्वाध्यो मनसा देवयंत इति ये वा अनू-
> जानास्ते कवयस्त एवैनं तदुन्नयंति ॥

> *dhīrāsaḥ kavaya unnayaṃti svādhyo manasā devayaṃta iti ye vā anū-*
> *jānāste kavayasta evainaṃ tadunnayaṃti ‖*

Then the Hotṛ recites, as the last, the seventh Ṛk युवा सुवासाः परिवीत आगात् (*yuvā suvāsāḥ parivīta āgāt*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 3-8-4). With the recital of this Ṛk the rite of anointing the yūpa is completed. Its meaning: "This young man (the yūpa) has come here adorned with garments and the like. Of all the trees that are born and grow, this yūpa is the best. The wise priests raise the yūpa and make it stand erect by the utterance of the mantras that they recite carefully and with a will." Here युवा सुवासाः (*yuvā suvāsāḥ*), the young man shining with garments and ornaments, means the vital breath (life), which is covered by the body and its limbs. The sentence स उश्रेयान्भवति (*sa uśreyānbhavati*) indicates that the yūpa, purified by the mantras, shines forth well. कवयः (*kavayaḥ*) are the wise priests who recite the mantras; the meaning is that by reciting these mantras they raise the yūpa and make it stand.

> ता एताः सप्तान्नाह रूपसमृद्धा एतद्वै यज्ञस्य समृद्धं यद्रूपस-
> मृद्धं यत्कर्म क्रियमाणमृगभिवदति शासां त्रिः प्रथमामन्वाह
> त्रिरुत्तमां ता एकादश संपद्यंत एकादशाक्षरा वै त्रिष्टुप्‌त्रिष्टु-
> बिंद्रस्य वज्र इंद्रायतनाभिरेवाभी राध्योति य एवं वेद त्रिः
> प्रथमां त्रिरुत्तमामन्वाह यज्ञस्यैव तद्बर्स् नह्यति स्थेन्ने बला-
> याविस्रंसाय ॥

> *tā etāḥ saptānnāha rūpasamṛddhā etadvai yajñasya samṛddhaṃ yadrūpasa-*
> *mṛddhaṃ yatkarma kriyamāṇamṛgabhivadati śāsāṃ triḥ prathamāmanvāha*
> *triruttamāṃ tā ekādaśa saṃpadyaṃta ekādaśākṣarā vai triṣṭuptriṣṭu-*
> *biṃdrasya vajra iṃdrāyatanābhirevābhī rādhyoti ya evaṃ veda triḥ*
> *prathamāṃ triruttamāmanvāha yajñasyaiva tadbars nahyati sthenne balā-*
> *yāvisraṃsāya ‖*

The Hotṛ recites these seven mantras, and so the anointing of the yūpa is duly accomplished. By reciting each Ṛk at its proper time, mantras suited to the occasion are recited and the sacrificial work is successfully carried out. Of these seven Ṛks the Hotṛ recites the first and the last three times each, so that eleven Ṛks in all are recited; for the pāda of the triṣṭubh metre has eleven syllables, and the triṣṭubh is the thunderbolt of Indra; these mantras are established in Indra. Therefore the man who knows this well and recites the first and seventh (last) Ṛks three times each binds the sacrificial work, with his own hand, so that it neither slips away nor loosens but stands firm; the meaning is that the sacrificial work proceeds without obstacle.

After the sacrifice is finished, as to whether the yūpa should be left standing as it is or should be put into the fire, some say that sacrificers who desire cattle should not put it into the fire but leave it standing. On this matter there is an old story. [The sentence ends here at the foot of p. 58; the story continues on the next page.]

### Printed p. 59

*[Running head, p. 59: [A. 3. A. 1. V. 3.] Ṛgvedasaṃhitā 59]*

Since there is a doubt as to how the yūpa is to be used (the supplied text prints "yūda" for yūpa), this is clarified in the Aitareya Brāhmaṇa (Ai. Brā. 2-3):

> देवेभ्यो वै पशवोऽन्नाद्यायालंभाय नातिष्ठंत तेऽपक्रम्य प्रति-
> वावदतोऽतिष्ठन्नास्मानालप्स्यध्वे नास्मानिति ततो वै देवा एतं
> यूपं वज्रवःपश्यंस्तमेभ्य उदश्रयंस्तस्माद्विभ्यत उपावर्तंत
> तमेवाद्याप्युपावृत्तास्ततो वै देवेभ्यः पशवोऽन्नाद्यायालं-
> भायातिष्ठंत तिष्ठंतेऽस्मै पशवोऽन्नाद्यायालंभाय य एवं वेद
> यस्य चैवं विदुषो यूपस्तिष्टति अनु प्रहरेत्स्वर्गकामस्य तमु ह
> स्मैतं पूर्वेऽन्वेव प्रहरंति ।

> *devebhyo vai paśavo 'nnādyāyālaṃbhāya nātiṣṭhaṃta te 'pakramya prati-*
> *vāvadato 'tiṣṭhannāsmānālapsyadhve nāsmāniti tato vai devā etaṃ*
> *yūpaṃ vajravaḥpaśyaṃstamebhya udaśrayaṃstasmādvibhyata upāvartaṃta*
> *tamevādyāpyupāvṛttāstato vai devebhyaḥ paśavo 'nnādyāyālaṃ-*
> *bhāyātiṣṭhaṃta tiṣṭhaṃte 'smai paśavo 'nnādyāyālaṃbhāya ya evaṃ veda*
> *yasya caivaṃ viduṣo yūpastiṣṭati anu praharetsvargakāmasya tamu ha*
> *smaitaṃ pūrve 'nveva praharaṃti |*

Once upon a time the animals ran away from the gods, so that the gods could not take them as they pleased. Having gone a little way, they stood there and said to the gods: "O gods, you cannot get us; we are not for you." Hearing this, the gods remembered the sacrificial post (yūpa) they had set up at the sacrifice, and looked at it. The animals, frightened by this, came and stood facing the gods. For this very reason it is still the custom today to tie the animals at a sacrifice so that they stand facing the yūpa. The animals that thus stood quietly, without running away from them, the gods accepted for their food. The animals of the sacrificer who knows this well stand quietly beside the yūpa set up high, without trouble, to be accepted by the gods. Therefore the sacrificer who desires cattle should not pull out the sacrificial post but leave it standing as it is. But on behalf of a sacrificer who desires heaven the Adhvaryu pulls out the post and puts it into the fire. For in former times many sacrificers, after the rite of binding the animal was complete, pulled out the posts and put them into the fire. For it is said: the sacrificer himself is the yūpa, the sacrificer himself is the darbha grass called prastara, and the fire is the womb (place of origin) of the gods. By offering the oblation in this way the sacrificer, with a golden body, goes to heaven, the abode of the gods.

> अथ ये तेभ्योऽवर आसंस्त एतं सृरुमपश्यन्यूपशकलं तं तस्मिन्कालेs-
> नुप्रहरेत्तत्र स काम उपापो योऽनुप्रहरणे तत्र स काम उपा-
> पो यः स्थाने ॥

> *atha ye tebhyo 'vara āsaṃsta etaṃ sṛrumapaśyanyūpaśakalaṃ taṃ tasminkāles-*
> *nupraharettatra sa kāma upāpo yo 'nupraharaṇe tatra sa kāma upā-*
> *po yaḥ sthāne ‖*

Those sacrificers of former times, that is, sacrificers other than the famous ṛṣis, understood that a small chip or piece of the yūpa stands in place of the yūpa, and that, therefore, if one offers into the fire a small chip of the yūpa instead of the large yūpa, it is as if the whole yūpa had been offered.

### Printed p. 60

*[Running head, p. 60: 60 Sāyaṇabhāṣyasahitā [Maṃ. 3. A. 1. Sū. 8]]*

All the desired ends that are attained by putting the yūpa into the fire are attained by offering a small piece of the yūpa. Hence, instead of putting the yūpa into the fire, it became the custom to offer the sruva (spoon) or the yūpa-chip (a small piece of the yūpa). On this matter the Taittirīya Saṃhitā of the Yajurveda says:

> देवा वै सग्गस्थिते सोमे प्रसुचोऽहरन्न, यूपं तेऽमन्यंत यज्ञ
> वेशसं वा इदं कुर्म इति ते प्रस्तरग्ग सुचां निष्क्रियणमपश्यन्
> स्वरुं यूपस्य सग्गस्थिते सोमे प्र प्रस्तरग्ं हरति जुहोति स्वरुम-
> यज्ञवेशसाय. (तै. सं. ६-३-४-९).

> *devā vai saggasthite some prasuco 'haranna, yūpaṃ te 'manyaṃta yajña*
> *veśasaṃ vā idaṃ kurma iti te prastaragga sucāṃ niṣkriyaṇamapaśyan*
> *svaruṃ yūpasya saggasthite some pra prastaragṃ harati juhoti svaruma-*
> *yajñaveśasāya. (tai. saṃ. 6-3-4-9).*

Concerning the putting of the yūpa into the fire, the Āpastamba Śrauta Sūtra says जुह्वां स्वरुमवदायानूयाजांते जुहोति द्यां ते धूमो गच्छतु (*juhvāṃ svarumavadāyānūyājāṃte juhoti dyāṃ te dhūmo gacchatu*), that is, after the recital of the anūyāja mantra, the sruva or yūpa-chip is placed in the juhū (the wooden offering ladle) and the offering is made while reciting द्यां ते धूमो गच्छतु (*dyāṃ te dhūmo gacchatu*) ("may your smoke go to heaven"). The meaning is that by offering the sruva or yūpa-chip one obtains the fruit of offering the whole yūpa, and also the fruit, such as the gaining of cattle, that comes when the yūpa is left as it is without being offered.

All that has been said so far is the order of setting up the yūpa to be carried out by the Hotṛ or the priests of the Hotṛ's group. Besides this there are some further rites to be performed by the Adhvaryu and others in setting up the yūpa. They are stated in the Anuvākas Tai. Saṃ. 1-3-5 and 1-3-6; 6-3-3; 6-3-4. Of these, the first (1-3-5) describes the cutting of the yūpa, 1-3-6 the setting up of the yūpa, 6-3-3 the yūpa rite, and 6-3-4 the rites beginning with the sprinkling of the yūpa. In the first two Anuvākas there are only the mantras of the yūpa rite. The last two Anuvākas are the Brāhmaṇa of the first ones, that is, they state the application of those mantras and their meaning. Therefore we shall give only the text of the first two Anuvākas and explain the last two.

Mantras of the cutting and setting up of the yūpa (the supplied text prints "yūha" for yūpa).

> अत्यन्यानगान्नान्यानुपागामर्वाक्त्वा परैरविदं परोवरैस्तं त्वा जुषे
> वैष्णवं देवयज्याय्यै देवस्त्व सविता मध्वानक्कोषधे त्राय
> स्वैनग्स् स्वधिते मैनग्ं हिग्ंसीर्दिवमग्रेण मा लेखीरंतरिक्षं

> *atyanyānagānnānyānupāgāmarvāktvā parairavidaṃ parovaraistaṃ tvā juṣe*
> *vaiṣṇavaṃ devayajyāyyai devastva savitā madhvānakkoṣadhe trāya*
> *svainags svadhite mainagṃ higṃsīrdivamagreṇa mā lekhīraṃtarikṣaṃ*

### Printed p. 61

*[Running head, p. 61: [A. 3. A. 1. V. 3] Ṛgvedasaṃhitā 61]*

> मध्येन मा हिग्ंसीः पृथिव्या संभव वनस्पते शतवल्‌शो वि रोह
> सहस्रवल्शा वि वयग् रुहेम यं त्वा यग्ग स्वधितिस्तेतिजानः प्रणि-
> नाय महते सौभगायाच्चिन्नो रायस्सुवीरः ॥

> *madhyena mā higṃsīḥ pṛthivyā saṃbhava vanaspate śatavalśo vi roha*
> *sahasravalśā vi vayag ruhema yaṃ tvā yagga svadhitistetijānaḥ praṇi-*
> *nāya mahate saubhagāyāccinno rāyassuvīraḥ ‖*

(Tai. Saṃ. 1-3-5; the supplied text prints "Kṛ. Saṃ.")

> पृथिव्यै त्वांतरिक्षाय त्वा दिवे त्वा शुंधतां लोकः पितृषदनो
> यवोऽसि यवयास्मद्वेषो यवयारातीः पितृणाग्ं सदनमसि
> स्वावेशोऽस्यग्रेगा नेत्रणां वनस्पतिरधि त्वा स्थास्यति तस्य वित्ताद्दे-
> वस्त्वा सविता मध्वानक्त सुपिप्पलाभ्यसौषधीभ्य उद्दिवग्ग स्तभानांत-
> रिक्षं पृण प्रथिवीमुपरेण दृग्ंह ते ते धामान्युत्मसि गमध्यॆ
> गावो यत्र भूरिशृंगा अयासः । अत्राह तदुरुगायस्य विष्णोः
> परमं पदमव भाति भूरेः । विष्णोः कर्माणि पश्यत यतो व्रतानि
> पस्पशे । इंद्रस्य युज्यस्सखा । तद्विष्णोः परमं पदग्ं सदा पश्यंति
> सूरयः । दिवीव चक्षुराततं । ब्रह्मवनिं त्वा क्षत्रवनिग्ं सुप्रजाव-
> निग्ं रायस्पोषवनिं पर्यूहामि ब्रह्म दृग्ंह क्षत्रं दृग्ंह
> प्रजां दृग्ंह रायस्पोषं दृग्ंह परिवीरसि परि त्वा दैवीर्विशो
> व्ययंतां परीमग्‌ रायस्पोषो यजमानं मनुष्या अंतरिक्षस्य
> त्वा सानावव गूहामि ॥

> *pṛthivyai tvāṃtarikṣāya tvā dive tvā śuṃdhatāṃ lokaḥ pitṛṣadano*
> *yavo 'si yavayāsmadveṣo yavayārātīḥ pitṛṇāgṃ sadanamasi*
> *svāveśo 'syagregā netraṇāṃ vanaspatiradhi tvā sthāsyati tasya vittādde-*
> *vastvā savitā madhvānakta supippalābhyasauṣadhībhya uddivagga stabhānāṃta-*
> *rikṣaṃ pṛṇa prathivīmupareṇa dṛgṃha te te dhāmānyutmasi gamadhyè*
> *gāvo yatra bhūriśṛṃgā ayāsaḥ | atrāha tadurugāyasya viṣṇoḥ*
> *paramaṃ padamava bhāti bhūreḥ | viṣṇoḥ karmāṇi paśyata yato vratāni*
> *paspaśe | iṃdrasya yujyassakhā | tadviṣṇoḥ paramaṃ padagṃ sadā paśyaṃti*
> *sūrayaḥ | divīva cakṣurātataṃ | brahmavaniṃ tvā kṣatravanigṃ suprajāva-*
> *nigṃ rāyaspoṣavaniṃ paryūhāmi brahma dṛgṃha kṣatraṃ dṛgṃha*
> *prajāṃ dṛgṃha rāyaspoṣaṃ dṛgṃha parivīrasi pari tvā daivīrviśo*
> *vyayaṃtāṃ parīmag rāyaspoṣo yajamānaṃ manuṣyā aṃtarikṣasya*
> *tvā sānāvava gūhāmi ‖*

(Tai. Saṃ. 1-3-6; the supplied text prints "Kṛ. Saṃ.")

The two Anuvākas that are in the form of a Brāhmaṇa for the above mantras, that is, that explain their meaning and application, are in the sixth Kāṇḍa, third Prapāṭhaka of the Taittirīya Saṃhitā; they are explained here with their meaning:

> वैष्णव्यर्चा हुत्वा यूपमच्छैति वैष्णवो वै देवतया यूपः स्वयै-
> वैनं देवतयोच्छैत्यत्यन्यानगां नान्यानुपागामित्याहाति ह्यस्यानेति
> नान्यानुपैत्यर्वाक्त्वा परैरविदं परोवरॆरित्याहार्वाग्न्येनं परैर्विं-
> दति परोवरै स्तं त्वा जुषे वैष्णवं देवयज्याया इत्याह देवय-
> ज्यायै हैनं जुषते देवस्त्वा सविता मध्वानक्तित्याह तेजसैवैनम-
> नक्कोषधे त्रायस्वैनग् स्वधिते मैनग् हिंग्‌सीरित्याह वज्रो वै
> स्वधितिः शांतै स्वधितेर्वक्षस्य बिभ्यतः प्रथमेन शकलेन सह तेजः परा
> पतति यःप्रथमः शकलः इरापतेत्तमप्या हरेश्वतेजसमेवैनमा हरती
> मे वैलोका यूपात्पयतो बिभ्यति दिवमग्रेण मा लेखीरंतरिक्षं
> मध्यॆन मा हिंग्‌सीरिष्याहैभ्य एवैनं लोकेभ्यः शमयति वनस्पते
> शतवल्‌शो वि रोहेत्याव्रश्चने जुहोति तस्मदाव्रश्चनाद्वृक्षाणां

*[The passage continues on the next page.]*

### Printed p. 62

*[Running head, p. 62: 62 Sāyaṇabhāṣyasahitā [Maṃ. 3. A. 1. Sū. 8]]*

*[The Brāhmaṇa passage of Tai. Saṃ. 6-3-3 continues from the previous page.]*

> भूयाग्ंस उत्तिष्ठंति सहस्रवल् शा वि वयग्ं रुहेमेत्याहाशिषमे-
> वैतामा शास्त्रेनक्षसंगं वृश्चेद्यदक्षसंगं वृश्चेदध ईषं यजमानस्य
> प्रमायुकग् स्याद्यं कामयेताप्रतिष्ठितः स्यादित्यारोहं तस्मै
> वृश्चेदेष वै वनस्पतीनामप्रतिष्ठितोऽप्रतिष्ठित एव भवति यं काम-
> येतापशुः स्यादित्यपर्ण० तस्मै तुष्काग्रं वृश्चेदेष वै वनस्पतीनाम
> पशव्योऽपशुरेव भवॆति यं कामयेत पशुमान्त्यादिति बहुपर्णं
> तस्मै बहुशाखं वृश्चेदेष वै वनस्पतीनां पशव्यः पशुमानेव भवति
> प्रतिष्ठितं वृश्चेत्पतिष्ठाकामस्येष वै वनस्पतीनां प्रतिष्ठितो यः
> समे भूम्यॆ स्वाद्योनेरूढः प्रत्येव तिष्ठति यः प्रश्यज्जुपनतस्तं
> वृश्चेत्स हि मेधमभ्युपनतः पंचारत्निं तस्मै वृश्चेद्यं कामये-
> तोपैनमुत्तरो यज्ञो नमेदिति पंचाक्षरा पंक्तिः पांक्तो यज्ञ
> उपैनमुत्तरो यज्ञो नमति षडरत्निं प्रतिष्ठाकामस्य षड्वा ऋतव
> ऋतुस्वेव प्रति तिष्ठति सप्तारत्निं पशुकामस्य सप्तपदा शक्वरी पशवः
> शक्वरी पशूनेवाव रुंधे नवारत्निं तेजस्कामस्य तिवृता स्तोमेन
> संमितं तेजस्त्रिवृत्तेजस्वेव भवत्येकादशारत्निमिंद्रियकामस्यैकाद-
> शाक्षरा त्रिष्टगिंद्रियं त्रिष्टुगिंद्रियाव्येव भवति पंचडशारत्निं भ्रातृ-
> व्यवतः पंचदशो वज्रो भ्रातृव्याभिभूत्यै सप्तदशारत्निं प्रजाकामस्य
> सप्तदशः प्रजापतिः प्रजापतेराप्ता एकविग्ंशत्यरत्निं प्रतिष्ठाकाम-
> स्यैकविग्०शः सोमानां प्रतिष्ठा प्रतिष्ठित्या अष्टाश्रिर्भवत्यष्टाक्षरा
> गायत्री तेजो गायत्री गायत्री यज्ञमुखं तेजसैव गायत्रिया
> यज्ञमुखेन संमितः ॥ (तै. सं. ६-३-३).

> *bhūyāgṃsa uttiṣṭhaṃti sahasraval śā vi vayagṃ ruhemetyāhāśiṣame-*
> *vaitāmā śāstrenakṣasaṃgaṃ vṛścedyadakṣasaṃgaṃ vṛścedadha īṣaṃ yajamānasya*
> *pramāyukag syādyaṃ kāmayetāpratiṣṭhitaḥ syādityārohaṃ tasmai*
> *vṛścedeṣa vai vanaspatīnāmapratiṣṭhito 'pratiṣṭhita eva bhavati yaṃ kāma-*
> *yetāpaśuḥ syādityaparṇa0 tasmai tuṣkāgraṃ vṛścedeṣa vai vanaspatīnāma*
> *paśavyo 'paśureva bhavèti yaṃ kāmayeta paśumāntyāditi bahuparṇaṃ*
> *tasmai bahuśākhaṃ vṛścedeṣa vai vanaspatīnāṃ paśavyaḥ paśumāneva bhavati*
> *pratiṣṭhitaṃ vṛścetpatiṣṭhākāmasyeṣa vai vanaspatīnāṃ pratiṣṭhito yaḥ*
> *same bhūmyè svādyonerūḍhaḥ pratyeva tiṣṭhati yaḥ praśyajjupanatastaṃ*
> *vṛścetsa hi medhamabhyupanataḥ paṃcāratniṃ tasmai vṛścedyaṃ kāmaye-*
> *topainamuttaro yajño namediti paṃcākṣarā paṃktiḥ pāṃkto yajña*
> *upainamuttaro yajño namati ṣaḍaratniṃ pratiṣṭhākāmasya ṣaḍvā ṛtava*
> *ṛtusveva prati tiṣṭhati saptāratniṃ paśukāmasya saptapadā śakvarī paśavaḥ*
> *śakvarī paśūnevāva ruṃdhe navāratniṃ tejaskāmasya tivṛtā stomena*
> *saṃmitaṃ tejastrivṛttejasveva bhavatyekādaśāratnimiṃdriyakāmasyaikāda-*
> *śākṣarā triṣṭagiṃdriyaṃ triṣṭugiṃdriyāvyeva bhavati paṃcaḍaśāratniṃ bhrātṛ-*
> *vyavataḥ paṃcadaśo vajro bhrātṛvyābhibhūtyai saptadaśāratniṃ prajākāmasya*
> *saptadaśaḥ prajāpatiḥ prajāpaterāptā ekavigṃśatyaratniṃ pratiṣṭhākāma-*
> *syaikavig0śaḥ somānāṃ pratiṣṭhā pratiṣṭhityā aṣṭāśrirbhavatyaṣṭākṣarā*
> *gāyatrī tejo gāyatrī gāyatrī yajñamukhaṃ tejasaiva gāyatriyā*
> *yajñamukhena saṃmitaḥ ‖ (tai. saṃ. 6-3-3).*

The setting up of the yūpa. At the beginning the priest called Adhvaryu makes an offering in the fire with the Viṣṇu-deity mantra उरु विष्णो विक्रमस्वोरु क्षयाय (*uru viṣṇo vikramasvoru kṣayāya*) (Tai. Saṃ. 1-3-4-1) and then comes to the yūpa. For the yūpa belongs to Viṣṇu as its deity; therefore, having made the oblation with a mantra addressed to Viṣṇu, he must approach the yūpa.

Then, standing by the tree for the yūpa, he says अत्यन्यानगां नान्यानुपागामित्याह (*atyanyānagāṃ nānyānupāgāmityāha*): although there are many trees, leaving them all he addresses (prays to) the tree, "I have come to you alone." In the same way he goes up to the tree that he has chosen for the yūpa, leaving the other trees. अर्वाक्त्वा परैरविदं परोवरैरित्याह (*arvāktvā parairavidaṃ parovarairityāha*): he says, "You, endowed with excellent qualities, are far away, and yet you are not very far but quite near."

### Printed p. 63

*[Running head, p. 63: [Maṃ. 3. A. 1. Va. 3] Ṛgvedasaṃhitā 63]*

तं त्वा जुषे वैष्णवं देवयज्याया इत्याह (*taṃ tvā juṣe vaiṣṇavaṃ devayajyāyā ityāha*): "You are a part of Viṣṇu; therefore I welcome you for the performance of the sacrifice to the gods", so saying he asks the yūpa to be welcome.

देवस्त्वा सविता मध्वानक्तित्याह (*devastvā savitā madhvānaktityāha*): "May the god Savitṛ anoint you with ghee", so saying he praises the power and capacity of the yūpa.

ओषधे त्रायस्वैनग् स्वधिते मैनग्ं हिग्ंसीरित्याह (*oṣadhe trāyasvainag svadhite mainagṃ higṃsīrityāha*): "O lord of the forest, protect (the sacrificer); O axe, do not harm this tree", so saying he addresses the weapon with praise.

वज्रो वै स्वधितिः शांतिः (*vajro vai svadhitiḥ śāṃtiḥ*): this axe has the nature of the thunderbolt. For the sake of peace he prays, "Do not harm this tree."

The tree is afraid of the axe. With the first chip cut off by the blow of the axe the splendour (tejas) of the tree falls down. Therefore the first chip must be taken along (with the yūpa); so saying, he brings the first chip, which is endowed with splendour, together with the yūpa.

इमै वै लोका यूपात्पयतो बिभ्यति (*imai vai lokā yūpātpayato bibhyati*) and the following: these worlds (earth, atmosphere, sky) are afraid that the tree being cut will fall on them. Therefore he prays to the tree, "O tree, when you fall do not scratch the sky with your tip, do not harm the atmosphere with your middle part (trunk)", so that no trouble may be done to the earth, atmosphere and sky.

वनस्पते शतवल्‌शो वि रोहेत्याव्रश्चने जुहोति (*vanaspate śatavalśo vi rohetyāvraścane juhoti*) and the following: "O best of trees, grow with hundreds of shoots and flourish", so saying, with the mantra वनस्पते शतवल्‌शो वि रोह सहस्रवल्‌शा वि वयग् रुहेम (*vanaspate śatavalśo vi roha sahasravalśā vi vayag ruhema*) (Tai. Saṃ. 1-3-5-1) he places gold at the foot of the tree, spreads the strewing (paristaraṇa) round it and makes the oblation. By doing so the cut tree grows large with hundreds of shoots.

सहस्रवल्शा वि वयग्ं रुहेमेत्याह (*sahasravalśā vi vayagṃ ruhemetyāha*): "May we grow, flourishing with thousands of shoots", so saying he makes a prayer for well-being.

Then he cuts the tree: अनक्षसंगं वृश्चेदध ईषं यजमानस्य प्रमायुकग् स्यात् (*anakṣasaṃgaṃ vṛścedadha īṣaṃ yajamānasya pramāyukag syāt*): in cutting the tree its size should be such that it does not touch the axle of the cart (the passage continues on the next page).

### Printed p. 64

*[Running head, p. 64: 64 Sāyaṇabhāṣyasahitā [Maṃ. 3. A. 1. Sū. 8]]*

(Continued.) If the size is too great and touches the axle of the cart, the sacrificer's cattle and the like will perish. Therefore the tree must be cut to the proper measure.

यं कामयेताप्रतिष्ठितः स्यादित्यारोहं तस्मै वृश्चेदेश वै वनस्पतीनाम- (*yaṃ kāmayetāpratiṣṭhitaḥ syādityārohaṃ tasmai vṛścedeśa vai vanaspatīnāma-*) and the following: if one wishes that the sacrificer should be without support (should not become a person of consequence), the one who cuts the tree should cut for the yūpa a branch that stands high on the tree (not touching the ground). Among plants the root or main part of the plant is the best, not its branches, which are not so important. Therefore by making the yūpa from an unimportant branch the sacrificer's standing is not increased but diminished.

यं कामयेत पशुमास्तादिति बहुपर्णं तस्मै बहुशाखं वृश्चेदेश (*yaṃ kāmayeta paśumāstāditi bahuparṇaṃ tasmai bahuśākhaṃ vṛścedeśa*) and the following: one who wishes that the sacrificer should be endowed with cattle such as cows should cut for the yūpa a tree that has grown luxuriantly with many leaves and branches. For animals are very fond of a tree that is fresh and full of leaves. Therefore one desiring animals should cut a tree with many leaves and branches. If such a tree is cut the sacrificer becomes rich in cattle.

प्रतिष्ठितं वृश्चेत्पतिष्ठाकामस्मैष वै वनस्पतीनां प्रतिष्ठितो यः समे (*pratiṣṭhitaṃ vṛścetpatiṣṭhākāmasmaiṣa vai vanaspatīnāṃ pratiṣṭhito yaḥ same*) and the following: one who desires standing or honour (should cut a tree that is firmly grown on level ground, without pits or mounds); such a tree is held to be the best among the trees of the forest. And this tree must have sprouted and grown from its own source (yoni), that is, from the seed; it must not be a tree that has sprouted from a cut branch planted in the ground. One who cuts for the yūpa such an independent tree, sprouted from seed and grown large, obtains honour and consideration among men.

यः प्रत्यज्जुपनतस्तं वृश्चेत्स हि मेधमभ्युपनतः (*yaḥ pratyajjupanatastaṃ vṛścetsa hi medhamabhyupanataḥ*): the tree to be cut for the yūpa should be one that leans towards the east. For if it is so, it is helpful to the sacrifice and fit for it.

पंचारत्निं तस्मै वृश्चेद्यं कामयेतोपैनमुत्तरो यज्ञो नमेदिति (*paṃcāratniṃ tasmai vṛścedyaṃ kāmayetopainamuttaro yajño namediti*) and the following: from here on the measure (length) of the yūpa [the supplied text prints "ಯೂಹ 5", i.e. "yūha 5"; doubtful] is described. In the sacrifices that everyone performs the yūpa may be of any measure, while in the optional rites (kāmyeṣṭi, in which the attainment of a particular fruit is desired) ... (the passage continues on the next page).

### Printed p. 65

*[Running head, p. 65: [Maṃ. 3. A. 1. Va. 3] Ṛgvedasaṃhitā 65]*

(Continued from the previous page.) ...the sacrifices performed with the aim that a given fruit may result: according to some, the yūpa should be of the measure that suits each fruit. Here the measure of the yūpa is indicated by the word अरत्नि (*aratni*) [the supplied text first prints अशत्नि (*aśatni*), evidently a misreading, [sic]]. अरत्नि (*aratni*) means चतुर्विंशत्यंगलयोऽरत्निः (*caturviṃśatyaṃgalayo 'ratniḥ*), a measure of twenty-four finger-breadths; that is, in common usage, one cubit in length.

पंचारत्नि (*paṃcāratni*): one who wishes that the sacrifice may be completed in an excellent manner, in all its parts, and that the sacrificer may attain the desired fruit, should cut the tree so that the yūpa is five cubits long. For in the metre called Paṅkti there are five syllables in each pāda, and the sacrifice also consists of five kinds of rites. Therefore the tree must be cut to a length of five cubits. Hence the sacrifice of the sacrificer who has cut a yūpa five cubits long is completed successfully and the intended fruit is obtained.

षडरत्निं प्रतिष्ठाकामस्य षड्वा ऋतव ऋतुष्टेव प्रति तिष्ठति (*ṣaḍaratniṃ pratiṣṭhākāmasya ṣaḍvā ṛtava ṛtuṣṭeva prati tiṣṭhati*): one who desires standing (honour) should cut the yūpa six cubits long. For there are six seasons; the sacrifice is established in these seasons. Thereby he obtains standing.

सप्तारत्निं पशुकामस्य सप्तपदा शक्वरी पशवः शक्वरी पशूनेवावरुंधे (*saptāratniṃ paśukāmasya saptapadā śakvarī paśavaḥ śakvarī paśūnevāvaruṃdhe*): one who desires abundance of cattle should cut a yūpa seven cubits long. For the Śakvarī metre has seven pādas, and there is a mutual relation between the Śakvarī metre and cattle; Śakvarī is said to be cattle. Therefore the sacrificer who has made a yūpa seven cubits long obtains abundance of cattle. (The supplied text prints निर्मिसिदु (*nirmisidu*) for the expected निर्मिसिद (*nirmisida*), [sic].)

नवारत्निं तेजस्कामस्य त्रिवृता स्तोमेन संमितं तेजस्त्वत्तेजस्वेव भवति (*navāratniṃ tejaskāmasya trivṛtā stomena saṃmitaṃ tejastvattejasveva bhavati*): one who desires splendour should cut a yūpa nine cubits long and praise it with the Trivṛt stoma. For splendour has the form of the Trivṛt stoma. If he does so he becomes splendid.

एकादशारत्निमिंद्रियकामस्यैकादशाक्षरा त्रिष्टुगिंद्रियं त्रिष्टुगिंद्रियाव्येव भवति (*ekādaśāratnimiṃdriyakāmasyaikādaśākṣarā triṣṭugiṃdriyaṃ triṣṭugiṃdriyāvyeva bhavati*): one who desires power and capacity should cut a yūpa eleven cubits long. For in the pāda of the Triṣṭubh metre there are eleven syllables, and the Triṣṭubh metre signifies indriya, that is, strength. Therefore one who does so becomes strong.

पंचदशारत्निं भ्रातृव्यवतः पंचदशो वज्रो भ्रातृव्याभिभूत्यै (*paṃcadaśāratniṃ bhrātṛvyavataḥ paṃcadaśo vajro bhrātṛvyābhibhūtyai*): one afflicted by enemies should cut and set up a yūpa fifteen cubits long. For the thunderbolt has a measure of fifteen, and the thunderbolt is able to destroy enemies (the sentence continues on the next page).

### Printed p. 66

*[Running head, p. 66: 66 Sāyaṇabhāṣyasahitā [Maṃ. 3. A. 1. Sū. 8]]*

(Continued.) Therefore the sacrificer who cuts and sets up a yūpa of fifteen aratni, the number that symbolises the thunderbolt, becomes free from harm by enemies.

सप्तदशारत्निं प्रजाकामस्य सप्तदशः प्रजापतिः प्रजापतेराप्त्यै (*saptadaśāratniṃ prajākāmasya saptadaśaḥ prajāpatiḥ prajāpaterāptyai*): one who desires progeny, sons, grandsons and so on, should make a yūpa seventeen cubits long. For Prajāpati is called the seventeenfold; by propitiating that Prajāpati the sacrificer becomes possessed of progeny.

एकविग्‌ंशत्यरत्नं प्रतिष्ठाकामस्यैकविग्‌०शः स्तोमानां प्रतिष्ठा प्रतिष्ठितै (*ekavigṃśatyaratnaṃ pratiṣṭhākāmasyaikavig0śaḥ stomānāṃ pratiṣṭhā pratiṣṭhitai*) (the supplied text prints एकविग्‌०शः (*ekavig0śaḥ*) the second time; as supplied): one who desires standing and honour should cut a yūpa twenty-one cubits long. For the standing or excellence of the stomas is indicated by twenty-one (one may also say that twenty-one is the number of the stomas). Therefore the sacrificer who desires standing should cut and set up a yūpa twenty-one cubits long.

अष्टाश्रिर्भवत्यष्टाक्षरागायत्रि तेजो गायत्रि गायत्रि यज्ञमुखं तेजसैव गायत्रिया यज्ञमुखेन संमितः (*aṣṭāśrirbhavatyaṣṭākṣarāgāyatri tejo gāyatri gāyatri yajñamukhaṃ tejasaiva gāyatriyā yajñamukhena saṃmitaḥ*): this yūpa has eight corners (angles or edges). For in the pāda of the Gāyatrī metre there are eight syllables. The Gāyatrī has the nature of splendour; the sacrifice begins with mantras in the Gāyatrī metre; or the Gāyatrī is like the head of the sacrifice, the chief part. The principal or foremost part of the sacrifice is joined to the havirdhāna of the Gāyatrī metre. There is the Vedic sentence शिरो वा एतद्यज्ञस्य यद्धविर्धानं (*śiro vā etadyajñasya yaddhavirdhānaṃ*) (तृ. सं. ६-२-११-१ (*tṛ. saṃ. 6-2-11-1*) as printed). As the sacrifice that begins with the splendid Gāyatrī is endowed with splendour, so the yūpa post with eight corners is fit for the sacrifice.

॥ Yūpaprokṣaṇādividhiḥ ॥ (heading as printed: the rite of sprinkling the yūpa and the rest.)

(Kṛ. Saṃ. 63-4) [as printed in the supplied text, "Kṛ." for "Tai."; the numeral is given as supplied, with no hyphen between the first two figures].

*[The Sanskrit passage of these two pages is given without an English rendering here; its explanation by the commentary follows on the next pages.]*

### Printed p. 68

*[Running head, p. 68: Sāyaṇabhāṣyasahitā [Maṃ. 3. A. 1. Sū. 8.]]*

(Pīṭhikā on the yūpa, continued; the Kannada explanation of Brāhmaṇa passages, with the Sanskrit passages quoted in the Kannada text. The Sanskrit quotations are given here only by their opening words, in the three-layer form.)

Having so far stated the rules for cutting the yūpa, the commentary now states the order of the yūpa's consecration. First the manner of sprinkling the yūpa is described: पृथिव्यै त्वांतरिक्षाय त्वा दिवे त्वेत्याहैभ्य एवैनं लोकेभ्यः प्रोक्षति (*pṛthivyai tvāṃtarikṣāya tvā dive tvetyāhaibhya evainaṃ lokebhyaḥ prokṣati*) (the text prints प्रथिव्यै (*prathivyai*) in the explanation that follows [sic?]). Having uttered the formula "For the earth thee, for the atmosphere thee, for the heaven thee", the priest says, "O yūpa, I sprinkle you for the stability of the earth, the atmosphere and the heaven", and sprinkles the root part, the middle part and the upper part of the yūpa in that order. Why must he sprinkle in order from the root up to the tip? Because heaven is in the upper part; to obtain the heavenly world he must sprinkle from below up to the top. (The Sanskrit passage also contains परांचं प्रोक्षति पराजुव हि सुवर्गो लोकः (*parāṃcaṃ prokṣati parājuva hi suvargo lokaḥ*), as printed.)

क्रूरमिव वा एतत्करोति यत्ननत्यपोऽव नयति शांतै यवमतीरव नय-त्पूर्ग्वै यवः (*krūramiva vā etatkaroti yatnanatyapo 'va nayati śāṃtai yavamatīrava naya-tpūrgvai yavaḥ*) (as printed in the supplied text, with the line-break hyphen) — When the pit is being dug to plant the yūpa-post, the earth is addressed thus: "O earth, when you are dug with a spade and similar tools, though such digging appears cruel, to remedy that fault I pour water into this dug pit", and for the pacification of the fault he pours water mixed with barley into the pit. Barley stands for food (nourishment); to obtain it he pours barley-mixed water into the pit.

यजमानेन यूपः संमितो यावानेव यजमानस्तावतीरेवास्मिन्नूर्जं दधाति (*yajamānena yūpaḥ saṃmito yāvāneva yajamānastāvatīrevāsminnūrjaṃ dadhāti*) — This yūpa is as tall as the sacrificer. Since the sacrificer desires food and the like, he puts into it the means to that, namely food (barley). Or: as long as the sacrificer lives, he puts barley in it so that he obtains food.

पितृणाग्ं सदनमसीति बर्हिरव स्तृणाति (*pitṛṇāgṃ sadanamasīti barhirava stṛṇāti*) (the text continues पितृ देवत्या २ ग्ग् हैतदन्निखातं (*pitṛ devatyā 2 gg haitadannikhātaṃ*), as printed, with a stray numeral "೨" [sic?] and यद्बर्हिरनवस्तीर्य मिनुयात्पितृदेवत्यो निखातः स्याद्बर्हिरवस्तीर्य मिनोत्यस्या-मेवैनं मिनोति (*yadbarhiranavastīrya minuyātpitṛdevatyo nikhātaḥ syādbarhiravastīrya minotyasyā-mevainaṃ minoti*)) — "O pit, you are the seat of the Fathers", saying this he strews the cut pieces of darbha called barhis. If the yūpa were planted in the pit without strewing the darbha so, it would belong to the Fathers and the gods of the Fathers. Therefore he does not strew the darbha in the pit but spreads it on the ground at its side. Afterwards he plants the yūpa in the pit.

यूपशकलमवास्यति सतेजसमेवैनं मिनोति (*yūpaśakalamavāsyati satejasamevainaṃ minoti*) — He throws into that pit the chip of the yūpa-tree cut first. That chip is full of brilliance (tejas). Since all the brilliance of the tree is in that first chip (the first piece that splits off), it must necessarily be put in to obtain brilliance. That the first chip is endowed with brilliance has already been stated earlier (प्रथमेन शकलेन सह तेजः परा पतति (*prathamena śakalena saha tejaḥ parā patati*), Tṛ. Saṃ. 6-3-3, "see the page"; the reference is printed "ತೃ." in the supplied text).

देवस्त्वा सविता मध्वानक्त्याह तेजस्यॆवैनमनक्ति (*devastvā savitā madhvānaktyāha tejasyèvainamanakti*) — "May the god Savitṛ anoint you with honey" (that is, with ghee fit for the gods) — having uttered this formula he

### Printed p. 69

*[Running head, p. 69: [Aṃ. 3. A. 1. Va. 3.] Ṛgvedasaṃhitā 69] (printed "ಅ. ೩." for "ಮಂ. ೩." as supplied [sic?])*

smears the top part of the yūpa with ghee (clarified butter). Since it has been said तेजो वा आज्यं (*tejo vā ājyaṃ*), "ghee is brilliance", smearing with ghee amounts to smearing with brilliance.

सुपिप्पलाभ्यसौषधीभ्य इति चषालं प्रति मुंचति तस्माच्छीर्षत ओषधयः फलं गृह्णंति (*supippalābhyasauṣadhībhya iti caṣālaṃ prati muṃcati tasmācchīrṣata oṣadhayaḥ phalaṃ gṛhṇaṃti*) — सुपिप्पलाभ्यसौषधीभ्यः (*supippalābhyasauṣadhībhyaḥ*) means "for the attainment of trees that bear excellent fruits". Having said this formula he attaches the first chip of the yūpa at the top of the yūpa (the chaṣāla is the ring at the top). Why? Because trees bear fruit at their tip (the upper part); therefore, to obtain the fruit of the sacrifice, it must be attached at the very top of the yūpa.

अनक्ति तेजो वा आज्यं यजमानेनाग्निष्ठामश्रिस्संमिता यदग्निष्ठामत्रि-मनक्ति यजमानमेव तेजसानक्ति (*anakti tejo vā ājyaṃ yajamānenāgniṣṭhāmaśrissaṃmitā yadagniṣṭhāmatri-manakti yajamānameva tejasānakti*) (as printed; the word अश्रिस्संमिता (*aśrissaṃmitā*) and the form अत्रिमनक्ति (*atrimanakti*) are as in the supplied text) — He smears it with ghee; ghee is brilliance. The yūpa is as tall as the sacrificer who performs the sacrifice. He smears the part (the edge) of the yūpa that faces the fire (is near it); by this the sacrificer himself is smeared with brilliance.

अंतमनक्त्यांतमेव यजमानं तेजसानक्ति सर्वतः परिमृशत्यपरिवर्गमेवास्मिन् तेजो दधाति (*aṃtamanaktyāṃtameva yajamānaṃ tejasānakti sarvataḥ parimṛśatyaparivargamevāsmin tejo dadhāti*) — He smears the yūpa with ghee as far as the end. By this the sacrificer is smeared with brilliance up to his end. Afterwards he rubs the whole yūpa all around thoroughly. By this brilliance is established in the sacrificer completely.

उद्दिवग्स्तभानांतरिक्षं पृणेत्याहैषां लोकानां विधृत्यै (*uddivagstabhānāṃtarikṣaṃ pṛṇetyāhaiṣāṃ lokānāṃ vidhṛtyai*) — Then, when raising and setting up the yūpa, he utters उद्दिवग्स्तभानांतरिक्षं पृण (*uddivagstabhānāṃtarikṣaṃ pṛṇa*) ("O yūpa, support the heaven or sky; fill the atmosphere with your form; make the earth firm"), and says that this is to be done for the firm establishment of these worlds, earth, atmosphere and heaven.

वैष्णव्यर्चा कल्पयति वैष्णवो वै देवतया यूपः स्वयैवैनं देवतया कल्पयति द्वाभ्यां कल्पयति द्विपाद्यजमानः प्रतिष्ठितै (*vaiṣṇavyarcā kalpayati vaiṣṇavo vai devatayā yūpaḥ svayaivainaṃ devatayā kalpayati dvābhyāṃ kalpayati dvipādyajamānaḥ pratiṣṭhitai*) — Then, reciting a ṛk addressed to Viṣṇu, he sets the yūpa up. For the yūpa's deity is Viṣṇu; therefore he establishes it with the mantra of its own deity. In establishing it so he recites the mantra addressed to Viṣṇu, ते ते धामान्युष्मसि गमध्यॆ गावो यत्र भूरिशृंगा अयासः । अश्राह तदुरुगायस्य विष्णोः परमं पदमव भाति भूरेः ॥ (*te te dhāmānyuṣmasi gamadhyè gāvo yatra bhūriśṛṃgā ayāsaḥ | aśrāha tadurugāyasya viṣṇoḥ paramaṃ padamava bhāti bhūreḥ ‖*) (Tai. Saṃ. 1-3-6-2), and the mantra विष्णूः कर्माणि पश्यत यतो व्रतानि पस्पशे ॥ इंद्रस्य युज्यः सखा॥ (*viṣṇūḥ karmāṇi paśyata yato vratāni paspaśe ‖ iṃdrasya yujyaḥ sakhā‖*) (Tai. Saṃ. 1-3-6-2), that is, two mantras. For the sacrificer has two feet; for the welfare of such a sacrificer he recites two mantras. (The Kannada text prints यजमावन (*yajamāvana*) "yajamāvana" for "yajamānana" in this sentence [sic?].)

### Printed p. 70

*[Running head, p. 70: Sāyaṇabhāṣyasahitā [Maṃ. 3. A. 1. Sū. 8.]]*

यं कामयेत तेजसैनं देवताभिरिंद्रियेण व्यर्धयेयमित्यग्निष्ठां तस्यात्रिमाहवनीयादित्थं वेत्थं वाति नावयेत्तेजसैनं देवताभिरिंद्रियेण व्यर्ध-यति (*yaṃ kāmayeta tejasainaṃ devatābhiriṃdriyeṇa vyardhayeyamityagniṣṭhāṃ tasyātrimāhavanīyāditthaṃ vetthaṃ vāti nāvayettejasainaṃ devatābhiriṃdriyeṇa vyardha-yati*) (as printed) — If one desires, with regard to some man, "Let me deprive him of the brilliance and the power of the senses that belong to the gods", then the edge of the yūpa that faces the fire (of the eight corners, the corner nearest the fire) should be turned away from the Āhavanīya fire, to the left or to the right, so that it comes to another side. If this is done, the brilliance connected with the gods does not arise in him. (The yūpa is planted facing east towards the Āhavanīya fire on the northern altar. Here अग्निष्ठां (*agniṣṭhāṃ*) means the corner near the fire, that is, the edge facing the Āhavanīya fire is to be made to come to another side: such is the meaning.) The text reads पार्शक्कॆ (*pārśakkè*) for "pārśvakke" [sic?].

यं कामयेत तेजसैनं देवताभिरिंद्रियेण समर्धयेयमित्नग्निष्ठां तस्याश्रिमाहवनीयेन सं मिनुयात्तेजसैवैनं देवताभिरिंद्रियेण समर्धयति (*yaṃ kāmayeta tejasainaṃ devatābhiriṃdriyeṇa samardhayeyamitnagniṣṭhāṃ tasyāśrimāhavanīyena saṃ minuyāttejasaivainaṃ devatābhiriṃdriyeṇa samardhayati*) (as printed; इत्नग्निष्ठां (*itnagniṣṭhāṃ*) and तस्याश्रिमाहवनीयेन (*tasyāśrimāhavanīyena*) are as in the supplied text [sic?]) — If one wishes that some man may obtain the brilliance connected with the gods, then the corner of the yūpa that is near the fire should be made to face the Āhavanīya fire. By doing so that sacrificer obtains the brilliance connected with the gods.

ब्रह्मवनिं त्वा क्षत्रवनिमित्याह यथायजुरेवैतत् (*brahmavaniṃ tvā kṣatravanimityāha yathāyajurevaitat*) — "O yūpa, you are the one who gains priestly splendour and the power of the warrior class" — saying this he strews the dung (purīṣa) around, clockwise. This is done in accordance with the yajus mantra ब्रह्मवनिं त्वा क्षत्रवनिग्ं सुप्रजावनिग्ं रायस्पोषवनिं पर्यूहामि (*brahmavaniṃ tvā kṣatravanigṃ suprajāvanigṃ rāyaspoṣavaniṃ paryūhāmi*) (the reference is printed शै. सं. १-३-६-२ (*śai. saṃ. 1-3-6-2*), Śai. Saṃ. 1-3-6-2, "Śai." in the supplied text).

परि व्ययत्यूर्गै रशना यजमानेन यूपः संमितो यजमानमेवोर्जा समर्धयति नाभिदघ्ने परि व्ययति नाभिदघ्न एवास्मा ऊर्जं दधाति तस्मान्नाभिदघ्न ऊर्जा भुंजते (*pari vyayatyūrgai raśanā yajamānena yūpaḥ saṃmito yajamānamevorjā samardhayati nābhidaghne pari vyayati nābhidaghna evāsmā ūrjaṃ dadhāti tasmānnābhidaghna ūrjā bhuṃjate*) — With the darbha called raśana he winds a new rope around the yūpa. The raśanā is strength; the yūpa is as tall as the sacrificer; by winding it so strength is added to the sacrificer. In winding it, he must wind at a height corresponding to the navel region in the middle part of the yūpa. By doing so power arises in the sacrificer, because men have their power at the navel region. Since the yūpa is as tall as the sacrificer, winding the darbha at the level of the sacrificer's navel increases the sacrificer's strength: such is the meaning. (The text prints अभिवृद्धिगॊळ्ळुवुदॆंभिप्रायवु (*abhivṛddhigòḷḷuvudèṃbhiprāyavu*) "…eṃbhiprāyavu" [sic?] and हॊसद हग्गवन्नु (*hòsada haggavannu*) as supplied.)

यं कामयेतोर्जैनं व्यर्धयेयमित्युद्ध्वां वा तस्यावाचीं वावोहेदूर्जैवैनं व्यर्धयति (*yaṃ kāmayetorjainaṃ vyardhayeyamityuddhvāṃ vā tasyāvācīṃ vāvohedūrjaivainaṃ vyardhayati*) — If one wishes with regard to anyone to make him without strength, then the darbha rope should be wound round the yūpa either above or below the level of the navel region. By this that man becomes without strength.

*[Pages 68–70 contain no Ṛk and no grammar page; the passage continues on the next page.]*

### Printed p. 71

*[Running head, p. 71: [A. 3. A. 1. Va. 3.] Ṛgvedasaṃhitā 71] (printed "ಆ ೩-" for the Maṇḍala numeral, as supplied [sic?])*

(Pīṭhikā on the yūpa, continued; Brāhmaṇa passages are given only by their opening words, in the three-layer form.)

यदि कामयेत वर्षुकः पर्जन्यः स्यादित्यवाजीमवो हेद्वृष्टिमेव (*yadi kāmayeta varṣukaḥ parjanyaḥ syādityavājīmavo hedvṛṣṭimeva*) — If he desires that Parjanya should rain, the darbha rope should be wound round the lower part of the yūpa; by that Parjanya is made to rain. If he does not wish it to rain (the text prints "ಕುಯ್ಯಬಾರದೆಂದು", evidently "ಹುಯ್ಯ-" [sic?]), the darbha should be wound round the upper part; then rain does not come.

पितृणां निखातं मनुष्याणामूर्ध्वं निखातादा रशनाया ओषधीनाग्ं रशना (*pitṛṇāṃ nikhātaṃ manuṣyāṇāmūrdhvaṃ nikhātādā raśanāyā oṣadhīnāgṃ raśanā*) — The author supports the view that the yūpa belongs to all the deities. The part of the yūpa planted (sunk) in the pit dug in the earth, that is, below ground level, belongs to the Pitṛs; the part from ground level up to where the rope (raśanā) is wound belongs to men. The part where the rope is wound belongs to the herbs (oṣadhi); from the rope up to the top, that is, up to the caṣāla (the ring at the top), belongs to the Viśvedevas; the caṣāla-place, that is, the upper end (tip), belongs to Indra; the remainder belongs to the Sādhyas. Since the parts of the yūpa thus belong to various deities, by planting the yūpa all the deities are satisfied.

यज्ञेन वै देवाः सुवर्गं लोकमायन्तेऽमन्यंत मनुष्या (*yajñena vai devāḥ suvargaṃ lokamāyante 'manyaṃta manuṣyā*) — The gods went to the heavenly world by sacrifice. While going they thought that men too would perform sacrifice, follow them to heaven and become their equals there; so, to prevent their coming, they planted the yūpa. Since men did not know its secret, thinking "it is not possible for them to go to heaven by sacrifice", the gods thus planted the yūpa and went to heaven; the yūpa was planted only to keep men from heaven. For this reason the word derived from a root meaning "to hold back" denotes the yūpa, and this became the cause of the name. But the seers found the means of going to the heavenly world by that yūpa-post. Since it is possible to go to heaven by sacrificing with this yūpa, the yūpa is planted in the sacrifice for the attainment of the heavenly world. (The text prints "ಯೂಪವವನ್ನು" once for "ಯೂಪವನ್ನು" [sic].)

पुरस्तान्मिनोति पुरस्ताद्भि यज्ञस्य प्रज्ञायतेऽप्रज्ञातग्ं हि तद्यदतिपन्न (*purastānminoti purastādbhi yajñasya prajñāyate 'prajñātagṃ hi tadyadatipanna*) — The yūpa is to be planted in front of the Āhavanīya fire. For if it is in front, the manner of performing the sacrifice is known; if it is not in front, it is not known. In the world, when people act in a matter they do not understand, it is customary to say "this should not have been done thus"; (continued on the next page).

### Printed p. 72

*[Running head, p. 72: 72 Sāyaṇabhāṣyasahitā [Maṃ. 3. A. 1. Sū. 8.]]*

Therefore, so that the manner of performing the sacrifice may not be lost to knowledge and the rite spoiled, the yūpa should be planted just in front of the Āhavanīya fire (facing east). Done thus, the matter of the sacrifice is rightly understood and goes through completely. (The text prints "ಕಾರ್ಯವುಕೆಟ್ಟು" run together [sic].)

साध्या वै देवा यज्ञमत्यमन्यंत (*sādhyā vai devā yajñamatyamanyaṃta*) — The deities called Sādhyas misunderstood the sacrifice. That is: not understanding what was said earlier, that in the yūpa the parts of the Pitṛs, men, herbs, Viśvedevas and Indra are excluded and the remaining part belongs to the Sādhyas, they wrongly took everything to be theirs; hence they did not obtain the sacrifice (the share). For them, apart from the parts assigned in the yūpa to the other deities, only the surplus part, that is, the portion above the caṣāla, was obtained. That is their share. With that the sacrificer satisfies them.

देवा वै सग्स् स्थिते सोमे (*devā vai sags sthite some*) — After finishing the Soma sacrifice they were performing, the gods threw into the fire the sruks (the wooden ladles used for offering) and the yūpa ("ಸ್ವಹಿ" in the text, apparently a misprint [sic?]). Afterwards they realised that what they had done was wrong and that the sacrifice was spoiled. Then they saw the prastara (the bundle of darbha) laid down for placing the sruks, and the svaru (the first chip or splinter that falls when the yūpa is cut), at the top of the yūpa. They understood that if the prastara is offered in place of the sruks and the svaru or first chip in place of the yūpa, the sacrifice is not broken. Therefore men, when performing a sacrifice, so that the sacrifice may not be lost, put the prastara in the fire as the substitute for the sruks, and offer the svaru in place of the yūpa (the text prints "ಯೂಷಕ್ಕೆ" and "ಸ್ವರನ್ನು" [sic]). Thus the sacrifice is completed without obstruction.

The rite of preparing the yūpa (printed "ಯೂಹಸಂಸ್ಕರಣವಿಧಿಯು", evidently "ಯೂಪ-" [sic]) is concluded.

### Printed p. 73

*[The supplied text has no running head for p. 73; its text is merged into p. 72. The marker was inserted before the heading of the eighth sūkta (structure only; see repair_log.txt).]*

**The eighth sūkta** (ಎಂಟನೆಯ ಸೂಕ್ತವು)

**Sāyaṇa's introduction to the sūkta** (Sanskrit, transcribed; see the Anuvāda below for the sense):

**॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥**

> अंजंतीत्यष्टमं वैश्वामित्रमेकादशर्चं सूक्तं । अश्रानुक्रमणिका ।
> अंजंति यूपस्तुतिः षष्ठ्या द्याभिर्बहवोऽन्या व्रश्चिन्यष्टमी वैश्वदेवी वा तृतीया-
> सप्तम्यावनुष्टुभाविति । उच्छयस्व वनस्पते ये वृक्षास इत्येते तृतीयासप्तम्या-
> वनुष्टुभौ शिष्टास्त्रिष्टुभः । कृत्स्नस्य सूक्तस्य यूपो देवता । वनस्पते शतवल्य इत्य-
> स्याश्विन्नयूपस्य मूलभूतः स्थाणुर्देवता । आदित्या रुद्रा वसव इत्यष्टमी वैश्व-
> देवी यौपी ना : अत्र षष्ठ्यादीनां बहवो यूपा देवता बहूनां स्तूय-
> मानत्वात् । शिष्टा एकयूपदेवत्याः ॥ यूपांजनेऽञ्छंति त्वामित्येषा । प्रेषितो
> यूपायाज्यमानायांजंति त्वामध्वरे देवयंतः । आ. ३-१ । इति ॥ यूपो-
> च्छयणे समिद्धस्यॆति द्वे ऋचौ ! उच्छयस्व वनस्पते समिद्धस्य श्रयमाणः पुर-
> स्तात् । आ. ३-१ । इति सूत्रितत्वात् ॥ यूपपरिव्ययणे युवा सुवासाः परिवीत
> आगादिति परिदध्यात् । आ. ३-१ । इति सूत्रितत्वात् ॥ यूपोच्छयणे जातो
> जायते सुदिनत्ते आह्नामित्यर्धर्च आरमेदिति सूत्रितं । यूपैकादशिन्यामन्यत्रापि
> बहुयूपयुक्ते कर्मणि यान्वो नर इत्यादिभिः षड्भिः पंचभिर्वा संस्तुयात् ।
> संस्तुयादनभिहिंकृत्य यान्वो नरो देवयंतो नि मिम्युरिति षड्भिः पंच-
> भिर्वा । आ. ३-१ । इति ॥

> *aṃjaṃtītyaṣṭamaṃ vaiśvāmitramekādaśarcaṃ sūktaṃ | aśrānukramaṇikā |*
> *aṃjaṃti yūpastutiḥ ṣaṣṭhyā dyābhirbahavo 'nyā vraścinyaṣṭamī vaiśvadevī vā tṛtīyā-*
> *saptamyāvanuṣṭubhāviti | ucchayasva vanaspate ye vṛkṣāsa ityete tṛtīyāsaptamyā-*
> *vanuṣṭubhau śiṣṭāstriṣṭubhaḥ | kṛtsnasya sūktasya yūpo devatā | vanaspate śatavalya itya-*
> *syāśvinnayūpasya mūlabhūtaḥ sthāṇurdevatā | ādityā rudrā vasava ityaṣṭamī vaiśva-*
> *devī yaupī nā : atra ṣaṣṭhyādīnāṃ bahavo yūpā devatā bahūnāṃ stūya-*
> *mānatvāt | śiṣṭā ekayūpadevatyāḥ ‖ yūpāṃjane 'ñchaṃti tvāmityeṣā | preṣito*
> *yūpāyājyamānāyāṃjaṃti tvāmadhvare devayaṃtaḥ | ā. 3-1 | iti ‖ yūpo-*
> *cchayaṇe samiddhasyèti dve ṛcau ! ucchayasva vanaspate samiddhasya śrayamāṇaḥ pura-*
> *stāt | ā. 3-1 | iti sūtritatvāt ‖ yūpaparivyayaṇe yuvā suvāsāḥ parivīta*
> *āgāditi paridadhyāt | ā. 3-1 | iti sūtritatvāt ‖ yūpocchayaṇe jāto*
> *jāyate sudinatte āhnāmityardharca āramediti sūtritaṃ | yūpaikādaśinyāmanyatrāpi*
> *bahuyūpayukte karmaṇi yānvo nara ityādibhiḥ ṣaḍbhiḥ paṃcabhirvā saṃstuyāt |*
> *saṃstuyādanabhihiṃkṛtya yānvo naro devayaṃto ni mimyuriti ṣaḍbhiḥ paṃca-*
> *bhirvā | ā. 3-1 | iti ‖*

**Anuvāda (Kannada).** This sūkta, beginning अंजंति (*aṃjaṃti*), is the eighth sūkta in the third maṇḍala. It has eleven ṛks. Viśvāmitra is the seer of this sūkta. In the Anukramaṇikā it is said: अंजंति यूपस्तुतिः षष्ठ्या द्याभिर्बहवोंत्या वश्चिन्यष्टमी वैश्वदेवी वा तृतीया (*aṃjaṃti yūpastutiḥ ṣaṣṭhyā dyābhirbahavoṃtyā vaścinyaṣṭamī vaiśvadevī vā tṛtīyā*) (continued सप्तम्यावनुष्टुभाविति (*saptamyāvanuṣṭubhāviti*)). The ṛk उच्छयस्व वनस्पते (*ucchayasva vanaspate*) and the third and seventh ṛks, the one beginning ये वृक्षासः (*ye vṛkṣāsaḥ*) (the text prints "ಯೇ ವೃ-ಕ್ಷಾಸಃ ಎಂಬ ಮೂರು ಮತ್ತು ಏಳನೆಯ"), are in the Anuṣṭubh metre; the remaining ṛks are in Triṣṭubh. For the whole sūkta the yūpa is the deity. Because the ṛk beginning वनस्पते शतवल्क (*vanaspate śatavalka*) praises, after the yūpa has been cut, the base of the tree standing in the earth, that ṛk has as deity the mūlabhūta sthāṇu (the stump). The eighth ṛk has the Ādityas, Rudras and Vasus as deities, or else the yūpa. For, as the statement षष्ठ्यादीनां बहवो (*ṣaṣṭhyādīnāṃ bahavo*) ... यूपा देवता बहूनां (*yūpā devatā bahūnāṃ*) says, the sixth, seventh, eighth, ninth and tenth ṛks (printed ೬, ೭, ೮, ೯, ೧೦) have many yūpas as deities. The remaining ṛks are addressed to a single yūpa. While the yūpa is being anointed with ghee ("ಧೃತ" in the text for "ಘೃತ" [sic?]) the first ṛk beginning अंजंति त्वां (*aṃjaṃti tvāṃ*) is to be recited, as directed by the sūtra of the Āśvalāyana Śrauta-sūtra प्रेषितो यूपायाज्यमानायांजंति (*preṣito yūpāyājyamānāyāṃjaṃti*) (Ā. 3-1, as printed). When the yūpa is raised and set up, the ṛk समिद्धस्य (*samiddhasya*) (the text prints "ಸಮಿದ್ಧಸ್ಯ ತ್ರಯಮಾಣಃ" [sic?]) and the ṛk उच्छयस्व वनस्पते (*ucchayasva vanaspate*) are also to be recited, as the Āśvalāyana Śrauta-sūtra (continued on the next page).

(A stray "10" stands at the end of this page in the supplied text; left as supplied.)

### Printed p. 74

*[Running head, p. 74: 74 Sāyaṇabhāṣyasahitā [Maṃ. 3. A. 1. Sū. 8.]]*

explains with the sūtra उच्छयस्व वनस्पते समिद्धस्य श्रयमाणः पुरस्तात् (*ucchayasva vanaspate samiddhasya śrayamāṇaḥ purastāt*) (Ā. 3-1, as printed). At the time of clothing the yūpa the ṛk युवा सुवासाः परिवीत आगात् (*yuvā suvāsāḥ parivīta āgāt*) is to be recited, as stated in the same sūtra (Ā. 3-1). When the yūpa is raised and planted, only half of the ṛk जातो जायते (*jāto jāyate*) (the text: "ಸುದಿನತ್ವೇ ಅಹ್ನಾಂ") is to be recited and then stopped, so the sūtra says. In the rite called Yūpaikādaśinī and in other rites in which many yūpas are to be planted, the yūpas are to be praised with five or six ṛks beginning यान्नो नरो देवयंतो नि मिम्युः (*yānno naro devayaṃto ni mimyuḥ*) (the Sanskrit of the bhāṣya above has "ಯಾನ್ವೋ"; the Anuvāda prints "ಯಾನ್ನೋ" [sic?]), as directed by the Āśvalāyana Śrauta-sūtra संस्तु यादनभिहिंकृत्य (*saṃstu yādanabhihiṃkṛtya*) ... षड्भिः पंचभिर्वा (*ṣaḍbhiḥ paṃcabhirvā*) (Ā. 3-1).

**Sūkta data (as printed).** Sūkta 8. Maṇḍala 3; Anuvāka 1; Sūkta 8. Aṣṭaka 3; Adhyāya 1; Varga 3, 4. Number of ṛks in the sūkta: 11. Ṛṣi: Viśvāmitra. Devatā: ṛks 1–5 Yūpas; 6, 7, 9, 10 Yūpas; 8 Viśvedevas or Yūpas; 11 Sthāṇu, the base of the broken yūpa (bhinnayūpasya mūlabhūtataḥ sthāṇuḥ). Chandas: 1, 2, 4–6, 8–11 Triṣṭubh; 3, 7 Anuṣṭubh.

**॥ संहितापाठः ॥**

> अं॒जंति॒ त्वा म॑ध्व॒रे दे॑व॒यंतो॒ वन॑स्पते॒ मधु॑ना॒ दैव्ये॑न ।
> यदू॒र्ध्वस्तिष्ठा॒ द्रवि॑णे॒ह ध॑त्ता॒द्यद्वा॒ क्षयो॑ मा॒तुर॒स्या उ॒-॑
> पस्थे ॥ १ ॥

**॥ पदपाठः ॥**

> अंजंति । त्वां । अध्वरे । देवयंतः । वनस्पते । मधुना । दैव्येन ।
> यत् । ऊर्ध्वः । तिष्ठाः । द्रविणा । इह । धत्तात् । यत् । वा । क्षयः । मातुः ।
> अस्याः । उपस्थे ॥ १ ॥
