### Printed p. 103

*[Running head, p. 103: [A. 3. A. 1. V. 4.] Ṛgvedasaṃhitā 103]*

**॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥**

> सुनीथा यज्ञस्य सुष्टु नेतार आदित्या द्वादशादित्या रुद्रा एकादशरुद्रा
> वसवोऽषौ वसवः । यद्वा सुष्टु नीथाः सुष्टुतयः । स्तुतशस्त्राणि नीथानि चोत्करा-
> मदानि च । ऐ. ब्रा. २-३८ । इति हि श्रूयते । द्यावाक्षामा द्यावापृथिव्यौ । पृथिवी ।
> सुब्बत्ययः । पृथु विस्तीर्णमंतरिक्षं च । सजोषस एते सर्वे देवा ऐकमत्येन
> परस्परं संगता भूत्वेममस्मदीयं यज्ञमवंतु । रक्षंतु । किंच । अध्वरस्य
> यज्ञस्य केतुं ध्वजभूतं प्रज्ञापकं वेमं यूपमूर्ध्व० कृण्वंतु । उच्चृतं
> कुर्वंतु ॥ आदित्याः । अदितेरपत्यानि । दित्यदित्यादित्यपत्युत्तरपदादिति ण्यः । णित्त्वादा-
> दिवृद्धिः । प्रत्ययस्वरः । रुद्राः । रोदयंति सर्वानंतकाले । रोदेर्णिलुक् चेति
> रक्षृत्ययः । तत्संनियोगे णिलोपः । प्रत्ययस्वरः । जसः सुप्त्वादनुदात्तत्वे एका-
> देश उदात्तेनोदात्त इत्येकादेशस्याप्युदात्तत्वं । वसवः । वस निवासे । निदित्यनुवृत्तौ
> श्यस्वस्तिहित्रप्यसिवसीत्यादिनोप्रत्ययः । नित्त्वादाद्युदात्तः । सुनीथाः । णीञ्
> प्रापणे । हनिकुषिनीरमिकाशिभ्य इति क्धन् । कित्त्वादगुणः । थाथघइत्तेत्युत्तरपदां-
> तोदात्तत्वं । द्यावाक्षामा । द्यौश्च क्षामा च । दिवो द्यावेति द्यावादेशः । देवता-
> द्वंद्वे चेत्युभयपदप्रकृतिस्वरत्वं । पृथिवी । प्रथ प्रख्याने । प्रथेः शिवन् संप्र-
> सारणं चेति षिवन् संप्रसारणं च । षिद्धौरादिभ्यश्चेति जीष् । प्रत्ययस्वरेणांतो-
> दात्तः । अवंतु । अव रक्षणे । निघातः । कृण्वंतु । कृवि हिंसाकरणयोरित्यस्मा-
> ल्लोटि धिन्विकृष्णोरच्छेत्युप्रत्ययः । अकारश्चांतादेशः । निघातः ॥

> *sunīthā yajñasya suṣṭu netāra ādityā dvādaśādityā rudrā ekādaśarudrā*
> *vasavo 'ṣau vasavaḥ | yadvā suṣṭu nīthāḥ suṣṭutayaḥ | stutaśastrāṇi nīthāni cotkarā-*
> *madāni ca | ai. brā. 2-38 | iti hi śrūyate | dyāvākṣāmā dyāvāpṛthivyau | pṛthivī |*
> *subbatyayaḥ | pṛthu vistīrṇamaṃtarikṣaṃ ca | sajoṣasa ete sarve devā aikamatyena*
> *parasparaṃ saṃgatā bhūtvemamasmadīyaṃ yajñamavaṃtu | rakṣaṃtu | kiṃca | adhvarasya*
> *yajñasya ketuṃ dhvajabhūtaṃ prajñāpakaṃ vemaṃ yūpamūrdhva0 kṛṇvaṃtu | uccṛtaṃ*
> *kurvaṃtu ‖ ādityāḥ | aditerapatyāni | dityadityādityapatyuttarapadāditi ṇyaḥ | ṇittvādā-*
> *divṛddhiḥ | pratyayasvaraḥ | rudrāḥ | rodayaṃti sarvānaṃtakāle | roderṇiluk ceti*
> *rakṣṛtyayaḥ | tatsaṃniyoge ṇilopaḥ | pratyayasvaraḥ | jasaḥ suptvādanudāttatve ekā-*
> *deśa udāttenodātta ityekādeśasyāpyudāttatvaṃ | vasavaḥ | vasa nivāse | nidityanuvṛttau*
> *śyasvastihitrapyasivasītyādinopratyayaḥ | nittvādādyudāttaḥ | sunīthāḥ | ṇīñ*
> *prāpaṇe | hanikuṣinīramikāśibhya iti kdhan | kittvādaguṇaḥ | thāthaghaittetyuttarapadāṃ-*
> *todāttatvaṃ | dyāvākṣāmā | dyauśca kṣāmā ca | divo dyāveti dyāvādeśaḥ | devatā-*
> *dvaṃdve cetyubhayapadaprakṛtisvaratvaṃ | pṛthivī | pratha prakhyāne | pratheḥ śivan saṃpra-*
> *sāraṇaṃ ceti ṣivan saṃprasāraṇaṃ ca | ṣiddhaurādibhyaśceti jīṣ | pratyayasvareṇāṃto-*
> *dāttaḥ | avaṃtu | ava rakṣaṇe | nighātaḥ | kṛṇvaṃtu | kṛvi hiṃsākaraṇayorityasmā-*
> *lloṭi dhinvikṛṣṇoracchetyupratyayaḥ | akāraścāṃtādeśaḥ | nighātaḥ ‖*

Gist: The well-guiding twelve Ādityas, eleven Rudras, eight Vasus, Heaven and Earth, Earth and the firmament, united in mind, are to protect our sacrifice and raise the yūpa, the banner of the sacrifice, aloft. The bhāṣya quotes Ai. Brā. 2-38 on नीथानि (*nīthāni*). The grammatical tail covers आदित्याः (*ādityāḥ*), रुद्राः (*rudrāḥ*), वसवः (*vasavaḥ*), सुनीथाः (*sunīthāḥ*), द्यावाक्षामा (*dyāvākṣāmā*), पृथिवी (*pṛthivī*), अवंतु (*avaṃtu*) and कृण्वंतु (*kṛṇvaṃtu*) (not transcribed further here).

**प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)**

सुनीथा (*sunīthā*): (the sacrifices) auspiciously conducting; आदित्याः (*ādityāḥ*): the twelve Ādityas; रुद्राः (*rudrāḥ*): the eleven Rudras; वसवति (*vasavati*) [sic, as supplied; वसवः (*vasavaḥ*) expected]: the eight Vasus; (सुनीथाः (*sunīthāḥ*): good praises); द्यावाक्षामा (*dyāvākṣāmā*): Heaven and Earth; पृथिवी (*pṛthivī*): the extensive; अंतरिक्षं (*aṃtarikṣaṃ*): the firmament (and the others); देवाः (*devāḥ*): the gods; सजोषसः (*sajoṣasaḥ*): of equal affection (united with one another); यज्ञं (*yajñaṃ*): this our sacrifice; अवंतु (*avaṃtu*): may they protect; (and) अध्वरस्य (*adhvarasya*): of the sacrifice; केतुं (*ketuṃ*): banner-like (indicating) this yūpa; ऊर्ध्वं कृण्वंतु (*ūrdhvaṃ kṛṇvaṃtu*): may they lift up (make it raised).

**भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)**

May all the gods, the leaders of the sacrifice: the twelve Ādityas, eleven Rudras, eight Vasus, Heaven and Earth, the wide firmament and the rest, united in agreement with one another, protect our sacrifice, and lift up this yūpa-post, the banner of the sacrifice.

*(page break)*

### Printed p. 104

*[Running head, p. 104: 104 Sāyaṇabhāṣyasahitā [Maṃ. 3. A. 1. Sū. 8]]*

**Printed English translation**

May the leaders of the rite, the divine Adityas, Rudras, Vasus, Heaven and Earth, the Earth, the firmament, well pleased, protect our sacrifice: let them raise aloft the standard of the ceremony.

**विशेषविषयगळु (Viśeṣaviṣayagaḷu)**

सुनीथा (*sunīthā*) — सुष्टु यज्ञस्य नेतारः (*suṣṭu yajñasya netāraḥ*). Sāyaṇa explains the meaning as: those who conduct the sacrifice, without any obstacle and in the prescribed order, until it is complete.

In the Ṛk श्वामिंद्र मतिभिस्सुते सुनीथासो वसूयवः । गोभिः क्राणा अनूषत ॥ (*śvāmiṃdra matibhissute sunīthāso vasūyavaḥ | gobhiḥ krāṇā anūṣata ‖*) (as supplied; the first word looks garbled, so no gloss is given) Durgācārya (Ni. 4-19) explains सुष्ठ स्तोतुं ये शक्नुवंते सुनीथाः (*suṣṭha stotuṃ ye śaknuvaṃte sunīthāḥ*) as: those who are skilled in praising well. The Nirukta also says that the word सुनीथः (*sunīthaḥ*) is stated among the ten words for praise beginning with अस्रेवाः (*asrevāḥ*) अनेमाः (*anemāḥ*) (Ni. 3-13, printed with a trailing hyphen) and denotes the excellent.

आदित्याः (*ādityāḥ*) — द्वादशादित्याः (*dvādaśādityāḥ*). In explaining the word *āditya* the Nirukta-author says आदिश्यः कस्मात् आदत्तॆ रसान्, आदत्ते भासं, ज्योतिषामादीपो भासेति वा आदितेः पुत्र इति वा । अल्पप्रयोगं तस्यैतवार्जाभ्याम्नाये सूक्तभाक् सूर्यमादितेयमदितेः पुत्रं (*ādiśyaḥ kasmāt ādattè rasān, ādatte bhāsaṃ, jyotiṣāmādīpo bhāseti vā āditeḥ putra iti vā | alpaprayogaṃ tasyaitavārjābhyāmnāye sūktabhāk sūryamāditeyamaditeḥ putraṃ*) (as supplied; the first word reads आदिश्यः (*ādiśyaḥ*)). He explains that the meaning of *āditya* is: that which draws up the waters on the earth, takes to itself all the other lights, gives lustre to the other radiances, surpasses everything in brilliance, or is the son of the goddess Aditi; and he adds that the Ṛks praising this Āditya are few, while the hymns to the Āditya-varieties Savitṛ, Bhaga and Pūṣan are many; and he continues:

एवमन्यासामपि देवतानामादिक्यप्रवादाः स्तुतयो भवंति । तद्यथैतन्नित्रस्य वरुणस्यार्यमोण्ण दक्षस्य भगस्यांशस्येति ॥ (*evamanyāsāmapi devatānāmādikyapravādāḥ stutayo bhavaṃti | tadyathaitannitrasya varuṇasyāryamoṇṇa dakṣasya bhagasyāṃśasyeti ‖*) (Ni. 2-13) (as supplied, with doubtful letters: आदिक्यप्रवादाः (*ādikyapravādāḥ*), अर्यमोण्ण (*aryamoṇṇa*)).

That is: in the same way, in the case of the other deities too, namely Mitra, Varuṇa, Aryaman, Dakṣa, Bhaga and Aṃśa, the usage and praise as "Āditya" occurs; and he gives an example of the use of the word *āditya* in Ṛks addressed to these deities:

ता सम्राजा धृतासुती आदित्या दानुनस्पती । सचेते अनवह्वरं ॥ (*tā samrājā dhṛtāsutī ādityā dānunaspatī | sacete anavahvaraṃ ‖*) (Pu. Saṃ. 2-41-6, as printed). (No gloss given.)

In these children [sic, as supplied; probably "in this Ṛk"] Mitra and Varuṇa are praised by the word *āditya*, and … (the sentence continues on p. 105).

### Printed p. 105

*[Running head, p. 105: [A. 3. A. 1. V. 4.] Ṛgvedasaṃhitā 105]*

**Special Topics** (continued)

प्र स मित्र मर्तो आस्तु प्रयस्वान्यस्त आदित्य शिक्षति व्रतेन । न हन्यते न जीयते त्वोतो नैनमंहो अश्नोत्यंतितो न धूरात् (*pra sa mitra marto āstu prayasvānyasta āditya śikṣati vratena | na hanyate na jīyate tvoto nainamaṃho aśnotyaṃtito na dhūrāt*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 3-59-2; the line is printed twice, the second time as "Ma. Saṃ. 3-59-2"). In this Ṛk the deity Mitra is praised by the word आदित्य (*āditya*) (*āditya*), and likewise,

उदुत्तमं वरुण पाशमस्मदवाधमं वि मध्यमं श्रथाय । अथा वयमादित्य व्रते तवानागगो अदितये स्याम ॥ (*uduttamaṃ varuṇa pāśamasmadavādhamaṃ vi madhyamaṃ śrathāya | athā vayamāditya vrate tavānāgago aditaye syāma ‖*) (Ma. Saṃ. 1-24-15). In this Ṛk Varuṇa is addressed by the word आदित्य (*āditya*) (*āditya*). After giving the meaning of the word आदित्य (*āditya*) (*āditya*), the Nirukta-author cites,

स्वरादित्योभवति । वृश्निरादित्यो भवति । नाक आदित्योभवति । गौरा-दित्योभवति । विष्टबादित्यो भवति । नभ आदित्यो भवति ॥ (*svarādityobhavati | vṛśnirādityo bhavati | nāka ādityobhavati | gaurā-dityobhavati | viṣṭabādityo bhavati | nabha ādityo bhavati ‖*) (Ni. 9-15),

and so illustrates the secondary (figurative) grounds of application in the matters denoted by the word आदित्य (*āditya*) (*āditya*). The meaning is that by the words स्वः (*svaḥ*) (*svaḥ*), पृश्निः (*pṛśniḥ*) (*pṛśniḥ*) [as printed in the Sāyaṇa quotation: वृश्नि (*vṛśni*)], नाकः (*nākaḥ*) (*nākaḥ*), गौः (*gauḥ*) (*gauḥ*), विष्टप् (*viṣṭap*) (*viṣṭap*), heaven (the firmament) is denoted by the word आदित्य (*āditya*) (*āditya*).

यदेदेनमदधुर्यज्ञॆयासो दिवि देवा: सूर्यमादितेयं । यदा चरिष्णू मिथुनावभूतामादित्सा पश्यन् भुवनानि विश्वा ॥ (*yadedenamadadhuryajñèyāso divi devā: sūryamāditeyaṃ | yadā cariṣṇū mithunāvabhūtāmāditsā paśyan bhuvanāni viśvā ‖*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 10-88-11). Since in this Ṛk it is stated in the Brāhmaṇa sentence यदस्य दिवि त्कृतीयं तवसावादित्य इति हि ब्राह्मणं (*yadasya divi tkṛtīyaṃ tavasāvāditya iti hi brāhmaṇaṃ*) (as printed, doubtful letters), the use of the name Āditya for Agni also occurs.

इमा गिर आदित्येभ्यो धृतस्नूः सनाद्राजभ्यो जुह्वा जुहोमि । शृणोतु मित्रो आर्यमा भगो नस्तुविजातो वरुणो दक्षो अंशः ॥ (*imā gira ādityebhyo dhṛtasnūḥ sanādrājabhyo juhvā juhomi | śṛṇotu mitro āryamā bhago nastuvijāto varuṇo dakṣo aṃśaḥ ‖*) (Ma. Saṃ. 2-27-1). In this Ṛk, from the sentence आदित्येभ्यः जुह्वा जुहोमि (*ādityebhyaḥ juhvā juhomi*) (*ādityebhyaḥ juhvā juhomi*), together with

अथातो द्युस्थाना देवगणाः तेषामादिश्याः प्रथमागामिनो भवंति । (*athāto dyusthānā devagaṇāḥ teṣāmādiśyāḥ prathamāgāmino bhavaṃti |*) (Ni. 12-35),

it is said that among the groups of gods dwelling in the heavenly region the Ādityas are the chief; hence the name Āditya is applied figuratively to all the deities. Sāyaṇa here explains that the Ādityas are twelve deities beginning with Mitra, and that they are all varieties of the Āditya.

*(page break)*

### Printed p. 106

*[Running head, p. 106: 106 Sāyaṇabhāṣyasahitā [Maṃ. 3. A. 1. Sū. 8.]]*

रुद्रा: (*rudrā:*) (*rudrāḥ*) — एकादशरुद्राः (*ekādaśarudrāḥ*) (*ekādaśarudrāḥ*), the eleven Rudras. The word रुद्र (*rudra*) (*rudra*) is stated among the names of praisers (Ni. 3-19). The Nirukta-authors explain: रुद्रो रौतीति सतः, रोरूयमाणो द्रवतीति वा “यदरुदत् तद्रुद्रस्य रुद्रत्वम्” इति हारिद्रविकम् (*rudro rautīti sataḥ, rorūyamāṇo dravatīti vā “yadarudat tadrudrasya rudratvam” iti hāridravikam*) (*rudro rautīti sataḥ, rorūyamāṇo dravatīti vā "yadarudat tadrudrasya rudratvam" iti hāridravikam*) [as supplied; सतः (*sataḥ*) doubtful]; and in the Kāṭhaka-brāhmaṇa, “ स किल पितरं प्रजापतिमिषुणा निध्यंतमनुशोचन्नरुदत् तद्रुद्रस्य रुद्रत्वं ” (*“ sa kila pitaraṃ prajāpatimiṣuṇā nidhyaṃtamanuśocannarudat tadrudrasya rudratvaṃ ”*) — "he saw his father Prajāpati pierced by an arrow and wept; therefore he is named Rudra" is said; and the Maitrāyaṇa branch has the name Hāridrava, in which also the derivation of the word रुद्र (*rudra*) (*rudra*) is stated in the same way, say the Nirukta-authors. They go on to cite

इमा रुद्राय स्थिरधन्वने गिरः क्षिप्रेषवे देवाय स्वधान्ने । आषाळ्दाय सहमानाय वेधसे तिग्मायुधाय भरता शृणोतु नः ॥ (*imā rudrāya sthiradhanvane giraḥ kṣipreṣave devāya svadhānne | āṣāḷdāya sahamānāya vedhase tigmāyudhāya bharatā śṛṇotu naḥ ‖*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 7-46-1),

and describe the quality and greatness of Rudra with this Ṛk. Sāyaṇa explains that these Rudras are eleven deities; and he gives the meaning that, since रोदयंति सर्वमंतकाले (*rodayaṃti sarvamaṃtakāle*) (*rodayaṃti sarvamaṃtakāle*) [sic, as supplied] — at the time of dissolution they make everyone weep — they are Rudras.

वसव: (*vasava:*) (*vasavaḥ*) — अष्टौ वसवः (*aṣṭau vasavaḥ*) (*aṣṭau vasavaḥ*), the eight Vasus. The Nirukta-author says: वसवो यद्विवसते सर्वमग्निः वसुभिर्वास, वइति समाख्या तस्मात् सृधिवीस्थानाः । इंद्रोर्वसुभिर्वासव इति समाख्या तस्मात् मध्यमस्थानाः । वसव आदित्यरश्मयः विवासनात्तस्मात् द्युस्थानाः । (*vasavo yadvivasate sarvamagniḥ vasubhirvāsa, vaiti samākhyā tasmāt sṛdhivīsthānāḥ | iṃdrorvasubhirvāsava iti samākhyā tasmāt madhyamasthānāḥ | vasava ādityaraśmayaḥ vivāsanāttasmāt dyusthānāḥ |*) (as supplied; सृधिवीस्थानाः (*sṛdhivīsthānāḥ*) and the split वसुभिर्वास, वइति (*vasubhirvāsa, vaiti*) look garbled).

The word वसु (*vasu*) (*vasu*), formed from the root वस निवासे (*vasa nivāse*) (*vasa nivāse*), has two kinds of meaning: "dwell", and "make to dwell happily". Hence one meaning: because terrestrial fire nourishes the whole world so that it lives happily, it is the sense of the word *vasu*, and because this fire pervades only the earth it has the earth as its region. Because Indra, by his wealth (riches), makes the whole universe dwell happily, he is a *vasu* and a god of the middle region: the second meaning. Because the rays of the Sun protect the whole world happily, they are gods of the heavenly region: the third meaning. Thus the meaning of the word वसवः (*vasavaḥ*) (*vasavaḥ*) is stated in three ways, as Agni, Indra and the rays of the Sun [the Kannada has "the wives of the Sun" सूर्यपत्निगळु (*sūryapatnigaḷu*) (*sūryapatnigaḷu*), as supplied; the context suggests "rays"]. Then,

ज्मया अत्र वसवो रंत देवा उरावंतरिक्षे मर्जयंत शुभ्राः । अर्वाक्षथ उरुज्रयः कृणुध्वं श्रोता दूतस्य जग्मुषो नो अस्य ॥ (*jmayā atra vasavo raṃta devā urāvaṃtarikṣe marjayaṃta śubhrāḥ | arvākṣatha urujrayaḥ kṛṇudhvaṃ śrotā dūtasya jagmuṣo no asya ‖*) (Ma. Saṃ. 7-39-3).

Citing this Ṛk, he explains: Agni, being on the earth the chief god for the increase of sacrificial rites, is the one pervading the earth-region; likewise Indra, because he protects everything, is the god of the middle region; and the rays of the Sun, dwelling in the firmament and producing light, are gods of the heavenly region. And,

*(page break)*

### Printed p. 107

*[Running head, p. 107: [A. 3. A. 1. V. 4.] Ṛgvedasaṃhitā 107]*

सुगावो देवाः सुपथा अकर्म य आजग्मुः सवनमिदं जुषाणः । जक्षिवांसः पपिवांसश्च विश्वेऽस्मॆ धत्त वसवो वसूनि ॥ (*sugāvo devāḥ supathā akarma ya ājagmuḥ savanamidaṃ juṣāṇaḥ | jakṣivāṃsaḥ papivāṃsaśca viśve 'smè dhatta vasavo vasūni ‖*) (Pā. Saṃ. 8-18, as printed).

In this Ṛk he gives the meaning that these deities, denoted by the word *vasu*, may bestow वसु (*vasu*) (*vasu*), that is wealth, and describes their greatness. Hence the meaning "wealth" for the word वसु (*vasu*) (*vasu*) is stated in the Nirukta (Ni. 3-9); and वसवः (*vasavaḥ*) (*vasavaḥ*) as the rays of the Sun (Ni. 2-15) is also stated.

सुगुरसत्सुहिरण्यः स्वश्वो बृहदस्मै वय इंद्रो दधाति । यस्त्वायंतं वसुना प्रातरित्वो मुक्षीजयेव पदिमुत्सिनाति ॥ (*sugurasatsuhiraṇyaḥ svaśvo bṛhadasmai vaya iṃdro dadhāti | yastvāyaṃtaṃ vasunā prātaritvo mukṣījayeva padimutsināti ‖*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-125; and again "Pu. Saṃ. 1-125-2", as printed).

Here Durgācārya has commented वसुना अन्नेन (*vasunā annena*) (*vasunā annena*), "by food"; hence the word *vasu* also means food. A meaning not stated by the Nirukta-authors in the Nirukta is stated in other Vedas. Following that, Sāyaṇa explains that the Ādityas are twelve persons, the Rudras (eleven, एकादश (*ekādaśa*) (*ekādaśa*)) eleven persons, and the Vasus eight persons. This matter is expounded in the first part (Vol. 1) of this Ṛgveda. He says that they are सुनीथाः (*sunīthāḥ*) (*sunīthāḥ*), सुष्टुतयॆः (*suṣṭutayèḥ*) (*suṣṭutayeḥ*) [sic, as supplied], those having excellent praises, or those much praised, and cites the Aitareya-brāhmaṇa sentence स्तुतशस्त्राणि नीथानि चोक्षामदानि च (*stutaśastrāṇi nīthāni cokṣāmadāni ca*) (Ai. Brā. 2-8), describing their greatness.

द्यावाक्षामा (*dyāvākṣāmā*) (*dyāvākṣāmā*) — द्यौश्च क्षामा च (*dyauśca kṣāmā ca*) (*dyauśca kṣāmā ca*): the firmament and the earth. Although this word is not explained in the Nirukta among the words for Heaven-and-Earth, Sāyaṇa has explained the meaning "heaven and earth".

पृथिवी (*pṛthivī*) (*pṛthivī*) — for this word, saying that it means पृथु विस्तीर्णं (*pṛthu vistīrṇaṃ*) (*pṛthu vistīrṇaṃ*), extensive or large, Sāyaṇa has explained it as an epithet of the word अंतरिक्षं (*aṃtarikṣaṃ*) (*aṃtarikṣaṃ*).

सजोषसः (*sajoṣasaḥ*) (*sajoṣasaḥ*) — संगताः (*saṃgatāḥ*) (*saṃgatāḥ*), united, assembled together.

आ रुद्रास इंद्रवंतः सजोषसो हिरण्यरथाः सुविताय गंशन । इयं वो अस्मत्पति दर्यते मतिस्तृष्णजे न दिव उत्सा उपन्यवे ॥ (*ā rudrāsa iṃdravaṃtaḥ sajoṣaso hiraṇyarathāḥ suvitāya gaṃśana | iyaṃ vo asmatpati daryate matistṛṣṇaje na diva utsā upanyave ‖*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 5-57-1).

Here the Nirukta-authors' explanation is सजोषसः सह जोषणाः (*sajoṣasaḥ saha joṣaṇāḥ*) (*sajoṣasaḥ saha joṣaṇāḥ*). Durgācārya describes the meaning as परमैश्वर्येण नित्यमासेविताः (*paramaiśvaryeṇa nityamāsevitāḥ*) (*paramaiśvaryeṇa nityamāsevitāḥ*): those constantly served by supreme lordship.

अजुक्तान ईड्यो वंद्यश्च याद्यग्ने वसुभिः सजोषाः ॥ (*ajuktāna īḍyo vaṃdyaśca yādyagne vasubhiḥ sajoṣāḥ ‖*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 10-110-3).

Here it is interpreted: सजोषाः सहजोषणः समानप्रीतिः (*sajoṣāḥ sahajoṣaṇaḥ samānaprītiḥ*) (*sajoṣāḥ sahajoṣaṇaḥ samānaprītiḥ*), one of one and the same affection. The Ādityas, the Rudras, the Vasus, the firmament, and all these deities, in agreement (ऐकमत्येन (*aikamatyena*) (*aikamatyena*)) ... (the sentence continues on the next page).

---

### Printed p. 108

*[Running head, p. 108: 108 Sāyaṇabhāṣyasahitā [Maṃ. 3. A. 1. Sū. 8.]]*

**Special Topics** (continued)

… "mutually united" (परस्परं संगताः (*parasparaṃ saṃgatāḥ*)): that is, assembled together, may they protect the sacrifice that the sacrificer and the priests perform in co-operation; so it is described.

अध्वरस्य केतुं (*adhvarasya ketuṃ*) — यज्ञस्य ध्वजभूतं प्रज्ञापकं वा इमं यूपं (*yajñasya dhvajabhūtaṃ prajñāpakaṃ vā imaṃ yūpaṃ*): this post (yūpa), which is the banner of the sacrifice or its indicator. The word केतु (*ketu*) (*ketu*) is stated (Ni. 3-13) among the names of knowledge [the supplied text has पद्धज्ञानामगळल्लि (*paddhajñānāmagaḷalli*), "padd-jñāna-names"; probably *prajñā*; as supplied, [sic]].

नवो नवो भवति जायमानोऽह्नां केतुरुषसामेत्यग्रं । भागं देवेभ्यो विदधात्यायन् प्र चंद्रमास्तिरते दीर्घमायुः ॥ (*navo navo bhavati jāyamāno 'hnāṃ keturuṣasāmetyagraṃ | bhāgaṃ devebhyo vidadhātyāyan pra caṃdramāstirate dīrghamāyuḥ ‖*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 10-85-19). Here the word केतुः (*ketuḥ*) (*ketuḥ*) is used in the sense of लक्षणं (*lakṣaṇaṃ*) (*lakṣaṇaṃ*), "mark, sign".

महो अर्णस्सरस्वती प्र चेतयति केतुना । धियो विश्वा वि राजति ॥ (*maho arṇassarasvatī pra cetayati ketunā | dhiyo viśvā vi rājati ‖*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-3-12). Here केतुना कर्मणा प्रज्ञया वा (*ketunā karmaṇā prajñayā vā*) (*ketunā karmaṇā prajñayā vā*) explains the sense as action (karma) or knowledge (prajñā).

उदुत्यं जातवेदसं देवं वहंति केतवः । दृशे विश्वाय सूर्यं ॥ (*udutyaṃ jātavedasaṃ devaṃ vahaṃti ketavaḥ | dṛśe viśvāya sūryaṃ ‖*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 1-50-1). Here केतवः रश्मयः (*ketavaḥ raśmayaḥ*) (*ketavaḥ raśmayaḥ*) gives the sense "rays". Although the Nirukta states for the word *ketu* the senses knowledge, ray, mark and action, Sāyaṇa here explains the sense "banner" and "indicator", and describes the prayer that the gods make the post, the indicator of the sacrifice, shine out nobly, with loftiness.

**Vyākaraṇa-prakriyā**

Grammar page, noted briefly. आदित्याः (*ādityāḥ*) (*ādityāḥ*): the male descendants of Aditi; the suffix ṇya in the sense of descendant by दित्यदित्यादित्यपत्युत्तरपदात् ण्यः (*dityadityādityapatyuttarapadāt ṇyaḥ*) (Uṇ. Sū. 4-1-85, as supplied); because the suffix is ṇit, vṛddhi of the first vowel by तद्धितेष्वचामदेः (*taddhiteṣvacāmadeḥ*) (Pā. Sū. 7-2-1117, as supplied [sic]); final udātta by the suffix accent. रुद्राः (*rudrāḥ*) (*rudrāḥ*): "those who make all weep at the end of time"; root रुदिर् अश्रुविमोचने (*rudir aśruvimocane*) (*rudir aśruvimocane*); after the causative, the suffix rak by रोदेर्णिलुक्च (*roderṇilukca*) (Uṇ. Sū. 2-179); loss of ṇic; because the suffix is k-marked there is no laghūpadha guṇa; the suffix accent remains; when the ending jas becomes anudātta, the single replacement is udātta by एकादेशउदात्तेनोदात्तः (*ekādeśaudāttenodāttaḥ*). वसवः (*vasavaḥ*) (*vasavaḥ*): root वस निवासे (*vasa nivāse*) (*vasa nivāse*); suffix u by शृ स्वृ स्निहि त्रप्यसि (*śṛ svṛ snihi trapyasi*) (Uṇ. Sū. 1-10); ṇit carried on from the preceding sūtra, hence initial udātta.

### Printed p. 109

*[Running head, p. 109: [A. 3. A. 1. V. 4.] Ṛgvedasaṃhitā 109]*

(Grammar notes, continued.) सुनीथाः (*sunīthāḥ*) (*sunīthāḥ*): root णीञ् प्रापणी (*ṇīñ prāpaṇī*) (*ṇīñ prāpaṇī*, as supplied); suffix kvan [the supplied text has क्सन् (*ksan*), *ksan*, [sic]] by हनिकुषिनीरमिकाशिभ्यः (*hanikuṣinīramikāśibhyaḥ*) (Uṇ. Sū. 2-159); because it is k-marked there is no guṇa of the root ī; final-syllable-of-the-latter-member udātta by थाथघञ् क्ता (*thāthaghañ ktā*) (Pā. Sū. 6-2-144); nominative plural form. द्यावाक्षामा (*dyāvākṣāmā*) (*dyāvākṣāmā*): द्यौश्च क्षामा च (*dyauśca kṣāmā ca*); by दिवोद्यावा (*divodyāvā*) the word *div* becomes *dyāvā*; by देवताद्वंद्वेच (*devatādvaṃdveca*) (Pā. Sū. 6-2-141) both members keep their natural accent; by सुपांसुलुक् (*supāṃsuluk*) the case-ending is replaced by ā. पृथिवी (*pṛthivī*) (*pṛthivī*): root प्रथ प्रख्याने (*pratha prakhyāne*) (*pratha prakhyāne*); suffix ṣivan with samprasāraṇa by प्रथेः शिवन् संप्रसारणंच (*pratheḥ śivan saṃprasāraṇaṃca*) (Uṇ. Sū. 1-148); samprasāraṇa of the r of the root; because it is ṣit, the feminine suffix jīṣ by षिड्गौरादिभ्यश्च (*ṣiḍgaurādibhyaśca*) (Pā. Sū. 4-1-41); final udātta by the suffix accent. आवंतु (*āvaṃtu*) (*avantu*): root अव रक्षणे (*ava rakṣaṇe*) (*ava rakṣaṇe*); loṭ, third person plural; finite-verb nighāta accent. कृण्वंतु (*kṛṇvaṃtu*) (*kṛṇvantu*): root कृवि हिंसाकारणयोश्च (*kṛvi hiṃsākāraṇayośca*) (*kṛvi hiṃsākaraṇayoś ca*); loṭ, third person plural, with replacement of the ending (as supplied: अंदेश उत्व (*aṃdeśa utva*)); loss of a by धिन्विकृण्वोरच (*dhinvikṛṇvoraca*) (Pā. Sū. 3-1-80); because the root is i-marked the augment num; ṇatva after ṛ; yaṇ substitution; nighāta accent because it stands after a word that is not a finite verb (the supplied text has निघासस्वर (*nighāsasvara*) [sic]).

#### Ṛk 9

**॥ संहितापाठः ॥**

> [figure: vol16/html/figures/fig_line5604.jpg]
> हं॒सा इ॑व श्रेणि॒शो यता॑नाः शु॒क्रा वसा॑नाः॒ स्वर॑वो न॒॑
> आगुः॑ ।
> उ॒न्नी॒यमा॑नाः क॒विभिः॑ पु॒रस्ता॑द्दे॒वा दे॒वाना॒मपि॑ यंति॒॑
> पाथः ॥ ९

**॥ पदपाठः ॥**

> हंसाः ऽइव । श्रेणशः । यतानाः । शुक्रा । वसानाः । स्वरवः । नः । आ । अगुः ।
> उत्‌नीयमानाः । कविभिः । पुरस्तात् । देवाः । देवानां । आपि । यंति । पाथः ॥
> ॥ ౮ ॥

*[The Pada block ends with the Ṛk number printed in a different numeral form (a Telugu-style 8) from the Saṃhitā's 9; both as supplied.]*

### Printed p. 110

*[Running head, p. 110: 110 Sāyaṇabhāṣyasahitā [Maṃ. 3. A. 1. Sū. 8]]*

**॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥**

> शुक्रा शुक्राणि दीप्तानि वासांसि वसाना आच्छादयंतः स्वरवः सवरुमंतः
> श्रेणिशः पंक्तिशो यतानाः प्रवर्तमानाः । अत्र दृष्टांतः । हंसा इवेति । यथा
> नभसि पंक्तिशः पतंतो हंसाः शोभंते तद्वत् शोभमानास्ते यूपा नोऽस्मा
> नागुः । प्रापुः । अपि च कविभिर्मेधाविभिरध्वर्या दिभिः पुरस्तादाहवनीयस्य पूर्व-
> भाग उन्नीयमाना उच्छ्रियमाणा देवा दीप्यमानास्ते यूपा देवानां
> पाथो देवानां स्थानमंतरिक्षं । पाथोऽन्तरीक्षं पथा व्याख्यातमिति यास्कः ।
> पाथोनदीभ्यां ड्यण् । पा. ४-४-१११ । इति सूत्रे पाथोऽन्तरीक्षमिति वृत्तिकारोs-
> प्यवादीत् । तादृशमंतरिक्षमपियंति । प्राप्नु वंति । अध्वर्यादिभिरुच्छ्रियमाणा
> यूपा अतिदैर्घ्यादंतरिक्षं व्याप्य वर्तंत इत्यर्थः ॥ हंसा इव । इवेन विभक्त्य-
> लोप इति वचनात्तूर्वपदस्वरः । श्रेणितः । बह्वल्पार्थादिति शस् । प्रत्ययस्वरः ।
> श्रेणिशः । बहुल्पार्थादिति शस् । प्रत्ययस्वरः । शुक्रा । शुच दीप्तावित्यस्मादृजेंद्रा
> ग्रेत्यादिना रन्प्रत्ययांतत्वेन निपातनादंतोदात्तः । अगुः । इण् गतौ । आस्माल्लुजि
> रूपं । निघातः ॥

> *śukrā śukrāṇi dīptāni vāsāṃsi vasānā ācchādayaṃtaḥ svaravaḥ savarumaṃtaḥ*
> *śreṇiśaḥ paṃktiśo yatānāḥ pravartamānāḥ | atra dṛṣṭāṃtaḥ | haṃsā iveti | yathā*
> *nabhasi paṃktiśaḥ pataṃto haṃsāḥ śobhaṃte tadvat śobhamānāste yūpā no 'smā*
> *nāguḥ | prāpuḥ | api ca kavibhirmedhāvibhiradhvaryā dibhiḥ purastādāhavanīyasya pūrva-*
> *bhāga unnīyamānā ucchriyamāṇā devā dīpyamānāste yūpā devānāṃ*
> *pātho devānāṃ sthānamaṃtarikṣaṃ | pātho 'ntarīkṣaṃ pathā vyākhyātamiti yāskaḥ |*
> *pāthonadībhyāṃ ḍyaṇ | pā. 4-4-111 | iti sūtre pātho 'ntarīkṣamiti vṛttikāros-*
> *pyavādīt | tādṛśamaṃtarikṣamapiyaṃti | prāpnu vaṃti | adhvaryādibhirucchriyamāṇā*
> *yūpā atidairghyādaṃtarikṣaṃ vyāpya vartaṃta ityarthaḥ ‖ haṃsā iva | ivena vibhaktya-*
> *lopa iti vacanāttūrvapadasvaraḥ | śreṇitaḥ | bahvalpārthāditi śas | pratyayasvaraḥ |*
> *śreṇiśaḥ | bahulpārthāditi śas | pratyayasvaraḥ | śukrā | śuca dīptāvityasmādṛjeṃdrā*
> *gretyādinā ranpratyayāṃtatvena nipātanādaṃtodāttaḥ | aguḥ | iṇ gatau | āsmālluji*
> *rūpaṃ | nighātaḥ ‖*

*Gist: The shining pillars, clad in bright garments and arrayed in rows like flying swans, have come to us; set up by wise priests in front of the Āhavanīya fire, they reach the gods' region, the atmosphere. (The grammatical tail is transcribed, not explained: हंसा इव (*haṃsā iva*), श्रेणिशः (*śreṇiśaḥ*), शुक्रा (*śukrā*), अगुः (*aguḥ*), root इण् गतौ (*iṇ gatau*). The supplied text has बहुल्पार्थादिति (*bahulpārthāditi*) the second time for बह्वल्पार्थादिति (*bahvalpārthāditi*) and the clause श्रेणिशः । बहुल्पार्थादिति शस् । प्रत्ययस्वरः (*śreṇiśaḥ | bahulpārthāditi śas | pratyayasvaraḥ*) twice; as supplied, [sic].)*

**प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)**

शुक्रा (*śukrā*) — shining (white, bright) [garments called *raśanā*]; वसानाः (*vasānāḥ*) — wearing; स्वरवः (*svaravaḥ*) — together with their own parts; श्रेणिशः (*śreṇiśaḥ*) — in rows; यतानाः (*yatānāḥ*) — moving; हंसा इव (*haṃsā iva*) — like swans (shining as they shine); श्रेणिशः (*śreṇiśaḥ*) — in row upon row (the posts that proceed); नः (*naḥ*) — to us; आगुः (*āguḥ*) — have arrived; (and) कविभिः (*kavibhiḥ*) — by the wise priests such as the Adhvaryu [the supplied text has आध्वय्यादि (*ādhvayyādi*), [sic]]; पुरस्तात् (*purastāt*) — on the east of the Āhavanīya fire; उत् नीयमानाः (*ut nīyamānāḥ*) — erected; देवाः (*devāḥ*) — those shining posts; देवानां (*devānāṃ*) — of the gods; पाथः (*pāthaḥ*) — the region, the atmosphere; आपि यंति (*āpi yaṃti*) — pervade [the supplied text has आपि (*āpi*) for अपि (*api*), as supplied].

**भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)**

The posts, clad in bright garments called *raśanā*, endowed with all their parts, shining in row upon row like rows of swans, have arrived (are shining); set up by the wise priests, the Adhvaryu and the others, in front of the Āhavanīya fire, they grow tall and touch even the atmosphere, the abode of the gods.

**Printed English translation**

Arrayed in bright (garments), entire (in their parts), these pillars ranging in rows like swans, have come to us erected by pious sages on the east (of the fire); they proceed resplendent on the path of the gods.

### Printed p. 111

*[Running head, p. 111: [A. 3. A. 1. V. 4.] Ṛgvedasaṃhitā 111]*

**विशेषविषयगळु (Viśeṣaviṣayagaḷu)**

हंसा इव श्रेणिशो यतानाः (*haṃsā iva śreṇiśo yatānāḥ*) — पङ्क्तः प्रवर्तमानाः हंसा इव (*paṅktaḥ pravartamānāḥ haṃsā iva*): moving in rows, like swans. यथा नभसि पङ्क्तः पतंतो हंसाः शोभंते तद्वत् शोभमानास्ते यूपाः (*yathā nabhasi paṅktaḥ pataṃto haṃsāḥ śobhaṃte tadvat śobhamānāste yūpāḥ*): as swans flying in rows in the sky shine, so those posts shine. The Nirukta writers have stated the word हंसासः (*haṃsāsaḥ*) among the names of the horse (Ni. 2-27). And:

ईर्मांतासः सिलिकमध्यमासः सं शूरणासो दिव्यासो अत्याः । हंसा इव श्रेणिशो यतंते यदाक्षिषुर्दिव्यमज्ममश्वाः ॥ (*īrmāṃtāsaḥ silikamadhyamāsaḥ saṃ śūraṇāso divyāso atyāḥ | haṃsā iva śreṇiśo yataṃte yadākṣiṣurdivyamajmamaśvāḥ ‖*) (the supplied text has पु. सं. (*pu. saṃ.*) [sic] 1-163-10).

Having cited this Ṛk, they explain हंसाः हंतेर्घ्नंत्यध्वानं (*haṃsāḥ haṃterghnaṃtyadhvānaṃ*): the word हंस (*haṃsa*) (*haṃsa*), derived from the root हन (*hana*) (*hana*) [as supplied] in the sense of going, means "one that travels the road". They also describe the word श्रेणि (*śreṇi*) (*śreṇi*) in श्रेणितः (*śreṇitaḥ*) as derived from the root श्रिज् (*śrij*) (*śrij*) [as supplied], with the sense of "being in a row". Sāyaṇa has explained it by comparing the posts to rows of swan-birds flying in the sky, which shine.

शुक्रा वसानाः (*śukrā vasānāḥ*) — शुक्राणि दीप्तानि रशनाख्यानि वासांसि आच्छादयंतः (*śukrāṇi dīptāni raśanākhyāni vāsāṃsi ācchādayaṃtaḥ*): covering themselves with shining garments called *raśanā*. The word शुक्र (*śukra*) (*śukra*) is stated among the names of water (Ni. 2-24).

आ सुष्वयंती यजते उपाके उषासानक्ता सदतां नि योन् । दिव्ये योषणे बृहती सुरुक्मे अधि श्रियं शुक्रपितं दधाने ॥ (*ā suṣvayaṃtī yajate upāke uṣāsānaktā sadatāṃ ni yon | divye yoṣaṇe bṛhatī surukme adhi śriyaṃ śukrapitaṃ dadhāne ‖*) (the supplied text has पु. सं. (*pu. saṃ.*) [sic] 10-110-6).

Here शुक्रं शोचतेर्ज्वलतिकर्मणः (*śukraṃ śocaterjvalatikarmaṇaḥ*) (the supplied text has दीप्तर्थवन्नु (*dīptarthavannu*) for the sense "shining" [sic]): the word शुक्र (*śukra*) is derived from the root शुच (*śuca*) (*śuca*), which conveys the sense of shining, and the meaning "lustrous" is given. Durgācārya explains the meaning as शुक्लरूपां श्रियं (*śuklarūpāṃ śriyaṃ*) (white-formed splendour).

शुक्रं ते अन्यद्यजतं ते अन्यद्विषुरूपे अहनी द्यौरिवासि । विश्वा हि माया अवसि स्वधावो भद्रा ते पूषन्निह रातिरस्तु ॥ (*śukraṃ te anyadyajataṃ te anyadviṣurūpe ahanī dyaurivāsi | viśvā hi māyā avasi svadhāvo bhadrā te pūṣanniha rātirastu ‖*) (the supplied text has म. सं. (*ma. saṃ.*) [sic] 6-58-1).

Here the word शुक्रम् (*śukram*) is given the sense "white" (शुक्ल (*śukla*)). The meaning is that the posts, clad in garments called *raśanā*, white (of a white colour) and lustrous, shine.

कविभि: पुरस्तादुन्नियमाना: (*kavibhi: purastādunniyamānā:*) [colons as supplied] — the word कवि (*kavi*) (*kavi*) is stated among the names of the intelligent (Ni. 3-19). The meaning is that the priests such as the Adhvaryu have set the posts up, shining, in the sacrificial hall on the eastern side of the Āhavanīya fire.

देवानां पाथः अपियंति (*devānāṃ pāthaḥ apiyaṃti*) — देवानां स्थानमंतरक्षं प्राप्नुवंति (*devānāṃ sthānamaṃtarakṣaṃ prāpnuvaṃti*) (the supplied text has अंतरक्षं (*aṃtarakṣaṃ*) [sic] for अंतरिक्षं (*aṃtarikṣaṃ*)): they reach the atmosphere, the abode of the gods. The Nirukta writer, (continued on the next page)—

### Printed p. 112

*[Running head, p. 112: 112 Sāyaṇabhāṣyasahitā [Maṃ. 3. A. 1. Sū. 8.]]*

यत्रा चक्रुरमृता गातुमस्मै श्येनो न दीयन्नन्वेति पाथः । प्रति वां सूर उदिते विधेम नमोभिर्मित्रावरुणोत हव्यैः ॥ (*yatrā cakruramṛtā gātumasmai śyeno na dīyannanveti pāthaḥ | prati vāṃ sūra udite vidhema namobhirmitrāvaruṇota havyaiḥ ‖*) (the supplied text has पु. सं. (*pu. saṃ.*) [sic] 7-63-5).

Having cited this Ṛk, he explains पाथः अंतरिक्षं पथा व्याख्यानतं (*pāthaḥ aṃtarikṣaṃ pathā vyākhyānataṃ*) [as supplied; the sense is "pāthaḥ is the atmosphere, explained by 'path'"], giving the meaning "way". Then:

वनस्पते रशनया नियूया देवानां पाथ उप वक्षि विद्वान् । स्वदाति देवः कृणवद्धवींष्यवतां द्यावापृथिवी हवं मे ॥ (*vanaspate raśanayā niyūyā devānāṃ pātha upa vakṣi vidvān | svadāti devaḥ kṛṇavaddhavīṃṣyavatāṃ dyāvāpṛthivī havaṃ me ‖*) (10-70-10, as supplied).

For the word पाथः (*pāthaḥ*) in this Ṛk he explains अन्नमपि पाथ उच्यते पानादेव (*annamapi pātha ucyate pānādeva*) ("food too is called pātha, from drinking"); the supplied text has वाख्यानमाडि (*vākhyānamāḍi*) [sic] for "explained". Durgācārya has explained the meaning as हवनसंबंधादन्नं पाथ उच्यते पानादेव (*havanasaṃbaṃdhādannaṃ pātha ucyate pānādeva*) ("food connected with the oblation is called pātha, from drinking").

उपावसृज त्मन्या समंजन् देवानां पाथ यतुथा हवींषि । हनस्पतिश्यमिता देवो अग्निः स्वदंतु हव्यं मधुना धृतेन ॥ (*upāvasṛja tmanyā samaṃjan devānāṃ pātha yatuthā havīṃṣi | hanaspatiśyamitā devo agniḥ svadaṃtu havyaṃ madhunā dhṛtena ‖*) (the supplied text has म. सं. (*ma. saṃ.*) [sic] 10-110-10; it also has हनस्पति (*hanaspati*) [sic]).

In this Ṛk too the word पाथ (*pātha*) is explained as "food". Sāyaṇa explains (अध्वय्यादिभिः उच्छ्रीयमाणा यूपा अतिदैर्घ्यादंतरिक्षं व्याप्य वर्तंते (*adhvayyādibhiḥ ucchrīyamāṇā yūpā atidairghyādaṃtarikṣaṃ vyāpya vartaṃte*)): the posts, consecrated by the Adhvaryu and other priests, shine in the atmosphere, the abode of the gods, by their height.

**Vyākaraṇa-prakriyā**

Grammar page, noted briefly. हंसा इव (*haṃsā iva*): root हसे हसने (*hase hasane*) (as supplied), suffix ac; being in the Pṛṣodarādi group it takes the anusvāra augment; by the statement इवेनसमासो विभक्त्यलोपश्च (*ivenasamāso vibhaktyalopaśca*) the case-ending is not elided in the compound; the accent of the first member remains. श्रेणितः (*śreṇitaḥ*): suffix śas by बह्वल्पार्थात् शस् का (*bahvalpārthāt śas kā*) (Pā. Sū. 5-4-42); final udātta by the suffix accent. यताना: (*yatānā:*) [colon as supplied]: root यती प्रयत्ने (*yatī prayatne*); replacement of liṭ by śānac; luk for the vikaraṇa by बहुलंछंदसि (*bahulaṃchaṃdasi*); तास्यनुदात्तेत् (*tāsyanudāttet*) (Pā. Sū. 6-1-186) keeps the root accent. शुक्रा (*śukrā*): root शुच दीप्तौ (*śuca dīptau*); suffix rat by ऋज्रेंद्राग्र (*ṛjreṃdrāgra*) (Uṇ. Sū. 2-180), final udātta. स्वरमः (*svaramaḥ*) [sic, as supplied]: said to be explained in the sixth mantra of this same sūkta.

### Printed p. 113

*[Running head, p. 113: [A. 3. A. 1. V. 4.] Ṛgvedasaṃhitā 113]*

(Grammar notes, continued.) अगुः (*aguḥ*): root इण् गत् (*iṇ gat*) [as supplied], luṅ; the root becomes gā by इणोगालुजि (*iṇogāluji*) (Pā. Sū. 2-4-45); by गातिस्थाघुपा (*gātisthāghupā*) (Vā. Sū. 2-4-77, as supplied) sic is dropped; the ending jhi becomes us by आतः (*ātaḥ*) (Pā. Sū. 3-4-110); the augment aṭ; nighāta accent. उन्नीयमानाः (*unnīyamānāḥ*): root णीञ् प्रापणे (*ṇīñ prāpaṇe*); śānac in the passive; yak by सार्वधातुके यक् (*sārvadhātuke yak*); augment muk by आनेमुक् (*ānemuk*); the yak accent remains; in the prādi compound the accent of the latter member of a kṛt compound. यंति (*yaṃti*): root इण् गतौ (*iṇ gatau*), laṭ third person plural; nighāta accent. पाथः (*pāthaḥ*): root पा रक्षणे (*pā rakṣaṇe*); suffix asun and the augment thuṭ are taught as irregular by अन्नेच (*anneca*) (Uṇ. Sū. 4-644, as supplied); here it conveys the meaning "atmosphere" (the supplied text has इर्थ (*irtha*) [sic] for "meaning").

#### Ṛk 10

**॥ संहितापाठः ॥**

> [figure: vol16/html/figures/fig_line5779.jpg]
> शृंगा॑णी॒वेच्छृं॒गिणां॒ सं द॑दृश्रे च॒षाल॑वंतः॒ स्वर॑वः॒
> पृथिव्यां ।
> वा॒घद्भ॑र्वा विह॒वे शोष॑माणा अ॒स्मा अ॑वंतु पृत॒नाज्ये॑षु
> ॥ १० ॥

**॥ पदपाठः ॥**

> [figure: vol16/html/figures/fig_line5788.jpg]
> शृंगाणिइव । इत् । शृंगिणां । सं । ददृश्रे । चषालवंतः । स्वरवः । पृथिव्यां ।
> वाघत्भिः । वा । विहवे । शोषमाणाः । आस्मान् । आवंतु । पृतनाज्येषु ॥ १० ॥

**॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥**

> स्वरवः स्वरमंतश्च षालवंतः प्रतिमुक्तकंटकाः । एवंविधा यूपाः पृथिव्यां
> शृंगिणां महिषादीनां शृंणाणीवेत् विषाणानीव संददृश्रे । सम्यक् दृश्यंत एव ।
> वा अपि च विदवे । विविधं हूयंते हवींष्यत्रेति विहवो यज्ञः । तस्मिन्यज्ञे वाघ-

> *svaravaḥ svaramaṃtaśca ṣālavaṃtaḥ pratimuktakaṃṭakāḥ | evaṃvidhā yūpāḥ pṛthivyāṃ*
> *śṛṃgiṇāṃ mahiṣādīnāṃ śṛṃṇāṇīvet viṣāṇānīva saṃdadṛśre | samyak dṛśyaṃta eva |*
> *vā api ca vidave | vividhaṃ hūyaṃte havīṃṣyatreti vihavo yajñaḥ | tasminyajñe vāgha-*

### Printed p. 114

*[Running head, p. 114: 114 Sāyaṇabhāṣyasahitā [Maṃ. 3. A. 1. Sū. 8.]]*

> द्विऋत्विग्निः । वाघतो व्यक्तबर्हिष इत्यृत्विज्ञामसु पाठात् । शोषमाणास्मैः
> क्रियमाणं स्तुतिलक्षणं शब्दं शृण्वंतः । तादृशा यूपाः पृतनाज्येषु । पृतनाज्य-
> शब्दः संग्रामवाची । आजौ पृतनाज्यमिति संग्रामनामसु पठितत्वात् । पृतनाज्येष्व
> संग्रामेषु । वधस्थलेष्टिति यावत् । अस्मान्यजमानानवंतु । रक्षंतु ॥ शृंगाणि ।
> श्य हिंसायामित्यस्माच्छृणातेर्ह्रस्वश्च । उ. १-१२५ । इति गन् नुडागमो धातो-
> र्ह्रस्वत्व । नित्स्वरः । ददृश्रे । छंदसि लुङ्लज्जिट इति लजर्थे लिट् । इरयो र इती-
> रेचो रे इत्यादेशः । निघातः ॥

> *dviṛtvigniḥ | vāghato vyaktabarhiṣa ityṛtvijñāmasu pāṭhāt | śoṣamāṇāsmaiḥ*
> *kriyamāṇaṃ stutilakṣaṇaṃ śabdaṃ śṛṇvaṃtaḥ | tādṛśā yūpāḥ pṛtanājyeṣu | pṛtanājya-*
> *śabdaḥ saṃgrāmavācī | ājau pṛtanājyamiti saṃgrāmanāmasu paṭhitatvāt | pṛtanājyeṣva*
> *saṃgrāmeṣu | vadhasthaleṣṭiti yāvat | asmānyajamānānavaṃtu | rakṣaṃtu ‖ śṛṃgāṇi |*
> *śya hiṃsāyāmityasmācchṛṇāterhrasvaśca | u. 1-125 | iti gan nuḍāgamo dhāto-*
> *rhrasvatva | nitsvaraḥ | dadṛśre | chaṃdasi luṅlajjiṭa iti lajarthe liṭ | irayo ra itī-*
> *reco re ityādeśaḥ | nighātaḥ ‖*

*Gist: The posts, eight-cornered and smooth, are seen on the earth like the horns of horned animals, and, hearing the praise uttered by the priests at the sacrifice, may protect us in battles. (The grammatical tail is transcribed, not explained: शृंगाणि (*śṛṃgāṇi*), ददृश्रे (*dadṛśre*).)*

**प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)**

स्वरवः (*svaravaḥ*) (*svaravaḥ*) — possessing their parts; चषालवंतः (*caṣālavaṃtaḥ*) (*caṣālavantaḥ*) — free of thorns, the posts; पृथिव्यां (*pṛthivyāṃ*) (*pṛthivyāṃ*) — on the earth; शृंगिणां (*śṛṃgiṇāṃ*) (*śṛṅgiṇāṃ*) — of horned ones such as buffaloes; शृंगाणि इव इत् (*śṛṃgāṇi iva it*) (*śṛṅgāṇi iva it*) — just like horns; सं ददृश्रे (*saṃ dadṛśre*) (*saṃ dadṛśre*) — are well seen; वा (*vā*) (*vā*) — and; विहवे (*vihave*) (*vihave*) — in the sacrifice where oblations of various kinds are offered; वाघद्भिः (*vāghadbhiḥ*) (*vāghadbhiḥ*) — by the priests (the sound in the form of praise made by them); श्रोषमाणाः (*śroṣamāṇāḥ*) (*śroṣamāṇāḥ*) — those hearing (the posts); पृतनाज्येषु (*pṛtanājyeṣu*) (*pṛtanājyeṣu*) — in battles (wars); अस्मान् (*asmān*) (*asmān*) — us, the performers of the sacrifice; अवंतु (*avaṃtu*) (*avantu*) — may they protect.

**भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)**

The posts, complete in their parts and free of thorns and the like, are seen on the earth like the horns of buffaloes and other horned animals. And these posts, which listen to the praise uttered by the priests at the sacrifices, may they protect us in battles.

**Printed English translation**

Entire in all parts and girded with rings, they appear upon the earth like the horns of horned cattle; bearing (their praises) by the priests: may they protect us in battles.

**विशेषविषयगळु (Viśeṣaviṣayagaḷu)**

स्वरव: (*svarava:*) [colon as supplied] — स्वरुमंतः (*svarumaṃtaḥ*) [as supplied]: the meaning is that the posts are made eight-cornered (with eight edges) [the supplied text has an underscore and a bracket here].

चषालवंतः (*caṣālavaṃtaḥ*) — प्रतिमुक्तकंटका: (*pratimuktakaṃṭakā:*): the meaning is that they are smooth, as if free of thorns (जंटकगळु (*jaṃṭakagaḷu*) [sic] for "thorns").

शृंगाणीवेत् (*śṛṃgāṇīvet*) — here, in the three words शृंगाणि इव इत् (*śṛṃgāṇi iva it*) (the supplied text has कदगळल्लि (*kadagaḷalli*) [sic] for "words"), the word इत् (*it*) (*it*) is used in the sense of a mere filler of the line. The Nirukta writers state अथ ये प्रवृत्तेर्थे (*atha ye pravṛtterthe*) ... and the quotation is cut off at the end of this page (continued in the next window).

### Printed p. 115

*[Running head, p. 115: [A. 3. A. 1. V. 4.] Ṛgvedasaṃhitā 115]*

*[Special Topics of Ṛk 10 continued from the previous page. The page opens with a fragment द्विति (*dviti*) [?] whose connection is unclear in the supplied text.]*

The Nirukta writers explain that the particles (the text has निखातगळु (*nikhātagaḷu*) [sic], evidently for *nipāta*) कं (*kaṃ*), ईं (*īṃ*), इत् (*it*) and उ (*u*) are used in prose works for completing the sentence and in metrical works for filling the pāda.

पृथिव्यां शृंगिणां शृंगाणीव संददृश्रे (*pṛthivyāṃ śṛṃgiṇāṃ śṛṃgāṇīva saṃdadṛśre*) — the sense is explained thus: the yūpas shine like the horns of horned animals such as buffaloes.

विहवे (*vihave*) — विविधं हूयंते हवींष्यत्रेति विहवो यज्ञः (*vividhaṃ hūyaṃte havīṃṣyatreti vihavo yajñaḥ*): the sacrifice, the rite in which many kinds of oblations offered to Agni are poured in many ways, is called *yajña*; this is the meaning explained here.

वाघद्भिः (*vāghadbhiḥ*) — the word वाघत् (*vāghat*) is stated among the names of priests (Ni. 3-19). The meaning is: the priests who perform the sacrificial rites.

पृतनाज्येषु (*pṛtanājyeṣu*) — the word पृतनाज्य (*pṛtanājya*) is stated among the names of battle (Ni. 3-9), and the Nirukta writers, citing the Ṛk इमं तं पश्य वृषभस्य युंजं काष्ठाया मध्ये द्रुघणं शयानं । येन जिगाय शतवत्सहस्रं गवां मुद्गलः पृतनाज्येषु ॥ (*imaṃ taṃ paśya vṛṣabhasya yuṃjaṃ kāṣṭhāyā madhye drughaṇaṃ śayānaṃ | yena jigāya śatavatsahasraṃ gavāṃ mudgalaḥ pṛtanājyeṣu ‖*) (Pu. Saṃ. 10-102-9, as printed), comment: पृतनाज्यमिति संग्रामनाम पृतनानाम जनाद्वा जयनाद्वा ! (*pṛtanājyamiti saṃgrāmanāma pṛtanānāma janādvā jayanādvā !*) (the text has वाख्यानमाडिद्दारॆ (*vākhyānamāḍiddārè*) [sic] for "explained"). Durgācārya explains the sense thus: पृतनः (*pṛtanaḥ*), men, अंजंति (*aṃjaṃti*) गच्छंति (*gacchaṃti*), go, where they perish, or पृतनः (*pṛtanaḥ*) men जयंति (*jayaṃti*) become victorious; and he says that because there arises a situation, a war, in which men perish or men conquer others, battle is expressed by the word पृतनाज्य (*pṛtanājya*). Sāyaṇa describes the meaning with पृतनाज्यशब्दः संग्रामवाची पृतनाज्येषु संग्रामेषु वधस्थलेष्टिति यावत् (*pṛtanājyaśabdaḥ saṃgrāmavācī pṛtanājyeṣu saṃgrāmeṣu vadhasthaleṣṭiti yāvat*), and says that the meaning is: may the posts (the text has यूषगळु (*yūṣagaḷu*) [sic] for यूपगळु (*yūpagaḷu*)) protect the sacrificers in these places of slaughter.

**Vyākaraṇa-prakriyā**

Grammar page, noted briefly. शृंगाणि (*śṛṃgāṇi*): root शृ हिंसायां (*śṛ hiṃsāyāṃ*); by शृणातेः ह्रस्वश्च (*śṛṇāteḥ hrasvaśca*) (Uṇ. Sū. 1-123, as printed here; the bhāṣya tail gives a different last number) the suffix gan; augment nuṭ; the root vowel is shortened, giving शृंग (*śṛṃga*). By निर्त्यं (*nirtyaṃ*) (as supplied) (Pā. Sū. 6-1-197) the suffix having an n-marker, the initial udātta accent comes. In compound with इव (*iva*), as before, the accent of the first member. ददृश्रे (*dadṛśre*): root दृशिर् प्रेक्षणे (*dṛśir prekṣaṇe*); by छंदसि लुज्लज्लिटः (*chaṃdasi lujlajliṭaḥ*) (Pā. Sū. 3-4-6) liṭ in the passive in the present sense, third person plural, affix झ (*jha*) (continued on the next page).

### Printed p. 116

*[Running head, p. 116: 116 Sāyaṇabhāṣyasahitā [Maṃ. 3. A. 1. Sū. 8.]]*

(Grammar notes, continued.) ददृश्रे (*dadṛśre*): by the sūtra लिटस्तझयो (*liṭastajhayo*) (the text gives only these words) the substitute इरेच् (*irec*) comes; then by इरयोरे (*irayore*) (Pā. Sū. 6-4-76) the substitute रे (*re*) for it. The root is doubled because of liṭ, etc. Because it stands after an *atijanta* (as supplied) the nipāta accent comes (the bhāṣya says nighāta). विहवे (*vihave*): root हु दानादनयोः (*hu dānādanayoḥ*); affix घ (*gha*) in the sense of the locus; taking it as the occasion, guṇa and अव् (*av*) substitution for the उ (*u*) of the root; by the accent of the affix it is acute on the final; in the prādi compound the accent of the latter member of a kṛt compound; locative singular form. श्रोषमाणाः (*śroṣamāṇāḥ*): root श्रु श्रवणे (*śru śravaṇe*); by vyatyaya it takes the ātmanepada; liṭ gets the substitute शानच् (*śānac*); by vyatyaya the vikaraṇa शप् (*śap*). By सिब्बहुलं लेटि (*sibbahulaṃ leṭi*) (Pā. Sū. 3-1-38) the power of the word *bahula* brings सिप् (*sip*) here also; taking it as the occasion, guṇa of the उ (*u*); ṣatva and the augment मुक् (*muk*) for it. Because the laśārvadhātuka becomes anudātta by तास्यनुदात्तेत् (*tāsyanudāttet*), the root accent remains. अवंतु (*avaṃtu*): root अव रक्षणे (*ava rakṣaṇe*); loṭ third person plural; tiṅanta, so the nighāta accent.

#### Ṛk 11

**॥ संहितापाठः ॥**

> वन॑स्पते श॒तव॑ल्वो॒ वि रो॑ह स॒हस्र॑वल्या॒ वि व॒यं रु॑हेम ॥
> यं त्वाम॒यं स्वधि॑ति॒स्तेज॑मानः प्रणि॒नाय॑ मह॒ते सौभ॑-
> गाय ॥ ११ ॥

**॥ पदपाठः ॥**

> वनस्पते । शतवल्यः । वि । रोह । सहस्रवल्याः । वि । वयं । रुहेम ।
> यं । त्वां । आयं । स्वधितिः । तेजमानः । प्रनिनाय । महते । सौभगाय ॥ ११ ॥

**॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥**

> हे वनस्पते भिन्नमूल स्थानो शतवल्यः शतशाखः सन् विरोह । विशेषेण
> हादुर्भूतो भव । वयमपि सहस्रवल्याः पुत्रपौत्रादिसंतानपरंपरया सहस्रशाखाः
> संतो विरुहेम । विशेषेण प्रादुर्भूता भवेम । तेजमानो निशितधारोऽयं
> स्वधितिः परशुः हे स्थानो यं त्वां महते सौभगाय महत्सौभाग्यं बहुशाखत्व-
> लक्षणं प्रणिनाय । प्रकर्षेण प्रापयामास ॥ सहस्रवल्याः । सहस्रशब्दः कर्दमादि-
> श्वान्मध्योदात्तः । बहुव्रीह पूर्वपदप्रकृतिस्वरः । रुहेम । रुह प्रादुर्भाव इत्य.

> *he vanaspate bhinnamūla sthāno śatavalyaḥ śataśākhaḥ san viroha | viśeṣeṇa*
> *hādurbhūto bhava | vayamapi sahasravalyāḥ putrapautrādisaṃtānaparaṃparayā sahasraśākhāḥ*
> *saṃto viruhema | viśeṣeṇa prādurbhūtā bhavema | tejamāno niśitadhāro 'yaṃ*
> *svadhitiḥ paraśuḥ he sthāno yaṃ tvāṃ mahate saubhagāya mahatsaubhāgyaṃ bahuśākhatva-*
> *lakṣaṇaṃ praṇināya | prakarṣeṇa prāpayāmāsa ‖ sahasravalyāḥ | sahasraśabdaḥ kardamādi-*
> *śvānmadhyodāttaḥ | bahuvrīha pūrvapadaprakṛtisvaraḥ | ruhema | ruha prādurbhāva itya.*

### Printed p. 117

*[Running head, p. 117: [Ā. 3. A. 1. V. 4.] Ṛgvedasaṃhitā 117 (merged into the bhāṣya of p. 116 in the supplied text; separated here)]*

> स्मादाशीर्लिजि किदाशिषीति यासुडागमः । स्कोः संयोगाद्योरंते चेति सलोपः ।
> लिज्याशिष्यङ्ङित्यङ् प्रत्ययः । अतो येय इति इयादेशः । लोपो व्योर्वलीति
> यलोपः । गुणः । प्रणिनाय । णीञ् प्रापण इत्यस्माल्लिटि णलि रूपं । पादादित्वाद-
> निघातः । लितीति प्रत्ययात्तूर्वस्योदात्तत्वं ॥

> *smādāśīrliji kidāśiṣīti yāsuḍāgamaḥ | skoḥ saṃyogādyoraṃte ceti salopaḥ |*
> *lijyāśiṣyaṅṅityaṅ pratyayaḥ | ato yeya iti iyādeśaḥ | lopo vyorvalīti*
> *yalopaḥ | guṇaḥ | praṇināya | ṇīñ prāpaṇa ityasmālliṭi ṇali rūpaṃ | pādāditvāda-*
> *nighātaḥ | litīti pratyayāttūrvasyodāttatvaṃ ‖*

*Gist: O stump of the broken-off yūpa, grow with a hundred branches, and may we grow with a thousand; the sharp axe has brought you for great good fortune. (The grammatical tail is transcribed, not explained: रुहेम (*ruhema*), प्रणिनाय (*praṇināya*).)*

**प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)**

(हे) वनस्सते (*(he) vanassate*) [sic, as supplied for वनस्पते (*vanaspate*)] — O stump (post) whose root is cut off; यं त्वां (*yaṃ tvāṃ*) — you whom; तेजमानः (*tejamānaḥ*) — with sharpened edge, अयं स्वधितिः (*ayaṃ svadhitiḥ*) — this axe; महते (*mahate*) — great; सौभगाय (*saubhagāya*) — for good fortune; प्र निनाय (*pra nināya*) — has cut and brought; (सः (*saḥ*) — such a you); शतवल्यः (*śatavalyaḥ*) — having hundreds of branches; विरोह (*viroha*) — appear (grow); वयं (अपि) (*vayaṃ (api)*) — we also; सहस्रवल्याः (*sahasravalyāḥ*) — having a thousand offspring such as sons and grandsons; वि रुहेम (*vi ruhema*) — may we prosper (the text has अभिवृद्ध रागोण (*abhivṛddha rāgoṇa*), with a stray space).

**भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)**

O stump of the yūpa, whose root is cut off: this axe with its sharp edge has cut you and brought you to the sacrifice so that you may bestow good fortune. May you, such as you are, grow with hundreds of branches, and grant that we too may prosper, having thousands of branches in the form of sons, grandsons and the like.

**Printed English translation**

VANASPATI, mount up (grow) with a hundred branches, that we may mount with a thousand, you whom the sharpened hatchet has brought for great auspiciousness.

**विशेषविषयगळु (Viśeṣaviṣayagaḷu)**

वनस्पते शतवल्वो विरोह (*vanaspate śatavalvo viroha*) — the meaning of the word *vanaspati* has been explained (in the Pīṭhikā). Here, with भिन्नमूल स्थाणो (*bhinnamūla sthāṇo*), the base of the tree that remains with its roots after the tree is cut, the stump, is addressed by the word वनस्पति (*vanaspati*). And the wish is expressed that this rooted stump, having many branches, may attain the state of a great tree.

The sacrificers, by the grace of the Agni present in the yūpa, express the hope सहस्रवल्याः वि रुहेम पुत्रपौत्रादिसंतानपरंपरया सहस्रशाखाः संतः विशेषेण प्रादुर्भूता भवेम (*sahasravalyāḥ vi ruhema putrapautrādisaṃtānaparaṃparayā sahasraśākhāḥ saṃtaḥ viśeṣeṇa prādurbhūtā bhavema*): that we may prosper in many ways in the order of sons and grandsons (the text has पुत्ररू (*putrarū*) मक्कळू (*makkaḷū*) मॊम्मक्कळू (*mòmmakkaḷū*)).

तेजमानः स्वधितिः महते सौभगाय प्रणिनायि (*tejamānaḥ svadhitiḥ mahate saubhagāya praṇināyi*) (as supplied; the text then has an underscore) — the yūpa connected with the vanaspati has been cut by the sharp axe and brought here, in order to bring us much welfare. This meaning (continued on the next page)

### Printed p. 118

*[Running head, p. 118: 118 Sāyaṇabhāṣyasahitā [Maṃ. 3. A. 1. Sū. 9]]*

is described with the explanation निशितधारः परशुः महत्सौभाग्यं बहुशाखत्वलक्षणं प्रकर्षेण प्रापयामास (*niśitadhāraḥ paraśuḥ mahatsaubhāgyaṃ bahuśākhatvalakṣaṇaṃ prakarṣeṇa prāpayāmāsa*).

**Vyākaraṇa-prakriyā**

रोह (*roha*): root रुह बीजजन्मनि प्रादुर्भावे च (*ruha bījajanmani prādurbhāve ca*); loṭ second person singular. Because it stands after an *atijanta* (as supplied), the nighāta accent comes. सहस्रवल्याः (*sahasravalyāḥ*): because the word सहस्र (*sahasra*) is read in the group beginning कर्दम (*kardama*), it has the accent on the middle syllable. By बहुप्रीहौप्रकृत्यापूर्वपदम् (*bahuprīhauprakṛtyāpūrvapadam*) (Pā. Sū. 6-2-1) (as supplied) the first member keeps its own accent in the bahuvrīhi compound. रुहेम (*ruhema*): root रुह प्रादुर्भावे (*ruha prādurbhāve*), first person plural of the āśīrliṅ, मस् (*mas*); by नित्यंजितः (*nityaṃjitaḥ*) (as supplied) loss of the स (*sa*); by लिज्याशिष्यज् (*lijyāśiṣyaj*) (Pā. Sū. 3-1-86) the affix अज् (*aj*); the liṅ takes the augment यासुट् (*yāsuṭ*); by आतोयेयः (*ātoyeyaḥ*) (Pā. Sū. 7-2-80) the substitute इय् (*iy*); guṇa; by लोपोव्योर्वलि (*lopovyorvali*) loss of the य् (*y*) of इय् (*iy*); tiṅanta nighāta accent. प्रणिनाय (*praṇināya*): upasarga प्र (*pra*); root णिञ् प्रापणे (*ṇiñ prāpaṇe*); liṭ third person singular, when तिप् (*tip*) comes, the substitute णल् (*ṇal*); doubling of the root; because the affix is ṇit, vṛddhi of the इ (*i*) of the root by अचो-इत्ति (*aco-itti*) (as supplied); substitute आय् (*āy*). Because it stands at the beginning of the pāda, the nighāta accent does not come. Because the affix is lit, by लिति (*liti*) the syllable before the affix gets the udātta. By तिजिचोदात्तवति (*tijicodāttavati*) (Pā. Sū. 8-1-71) the gati (प्र (*pra*)) gets the udātta, since a tiṅanta with an udātta follows. महशे (*mahaśe*) [sic, evidently for महते (*mahate*)]: by बृहन्महतोरुपसंख्यानम् (*bṛhanmahatorupasaṃkhyānam*) the dative ending gets the udātta.

## Sūkta 9 (Maṇḍala 3)

**The ninth sūkta**

**Sāyaṇa's introduction to the sūkta** (Sanskrit, transcribed; see the Anuvāda below for the sense):

**॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥**

> सखायस्त्वति नवर्चं नवमं सूक्तं वैश्वामित्रं । आश्रेयमनुक्रमणिका ।
> सखायो नव बार्हतं त्रिष्टुबंतमिति । अंत्या त्रिष्टुप् । शिष्टा अष्ट् बृहत्यः ॥ प्रात-
> रनुवाकाश्विनशस्त्रयोर्बार्हते छंदस्यस्य सूक्तस्य विनियोगः । अथैतस्या इति खंडे
> सूत्रितं । सखायस्त्वियमग्निरग्न आ याहि । आ. ४-१३ । इति ॥

> *sakhāyastvati navarcaṃ navamaṃ sūktaṃ vaiśvāmitraṃ | āśreyamanukramaṇikā |*
> *sakhāyo nava bārhataṃ triṣṭubaṃtamiti | aṃtyā triṣṭup | śiṣṭā aṣṭ bṛhatyaḥ ‖ prāta-*
> *ranuvākāśvinaśastrayorbārhate chaṃdasyasya sūktasya viniyogaḥ | athaitasyā iti khaṃḍe*
> *sūtritaṃ | sakhāyastviyamagniragna ā yāhi | ā. 4-13 | iti ‖*

**Anuvāda (Kannada).** This sūkta beginning सखायस्त्वा (*sakhāyastvā*) is the ninth sūkta in the first anuvāka. It has nine ṛks. Viśvāmitra is the seer of this sūkta. In the Anukramaṇikā it is said: सखायो नव बार्हतं त्रिष्टुबंतमिति (*sakhāyo nava bārhataṃ triṣṭubaṃtamiti*). The last ṛk of this sūkta is in the Triṣṭubh metre; the remaining eight ṛks are in the Bṛhatī metre. At the recitation of the Prātaranuvāka mantras and of the Āśvina śastra mantras, for the recitation of the ṛks in the Bṛhatī metre, this sūkta's (the Anuvāda breaks off here at the end of p. 118; the application continues on p. 119, together with the sūkta data and Ṛk 1).

### Printed p. 119

*[Running head, p. 119: [Ā. 3. A. 1. V. 5.] Ṛgvedasaṃhitā 119 (as supplied, "ಅ. ೩. ಅ. ೧. ವ. ೫.")]*

*[Continuation of the Anuvāda of Sūkta 9 from the previous page.]*

… has its application (viniyoga), as is made clear by the Āśvalāyana Śrauta-sūtra in the section beginning अथैतस्या (*athaitasyā*) [sic, as supplied], in the sūtra सखायस्त्यामग्निरग्न आ याहि (*sakhāyastyāmagniragna ā yāhi*) (Ā. 4-13).

**Sūkta data (as printed).** Sūkta 9. Maṇḍala 3; Anuvāka 1; Sūkta 9. Aṣṭaka 3; Adhyāya 1; Varga 5, 6. Number of ṛks in the sūkta: 9. Ṛṣi: Viśvāmitra. Devatā: Agni. Chandas: "8, Bṛhatī; 9 Triṣṭubh" (as printed; evidently ṛks 1–8 Bṛhatī, 9 Triṣṭubh, in agreement with the Anukramaṇikā quoted above).

#### Ṛk 1

**॥ संहितापाठः ॥**

> सखा॑यस्त्वा ववृमहे दे॒वं मर्ता॑स ऊ॒तये॑ ।
> अ॒पां नपा॑तं सु॒भगं॑ सु॒दीदि॑तिं सु॒प्रतू॑र्तिमने॒हसं॑ ॥ १ ॥

**॥ पदपाठः ॥**

> सखायः । त्वा । ववृमहे । देवं । मर्तासः । ऊतये ।
> अपां । नपातं । सुभगं । सुदीदितिं । सुप्रतूर्तिं । अनेहसं ॥ १ ॥

**॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥**

> विश्वामित्रः सौति । हे आग्ने सखायः सोमाज्यादिहविस्पदानेनोपकारक-
> त्वान्मित्राणि मर्तसो मनुष्या ऋत्विजो वयमपां नपातमपां नप्तारं सुभगं
> शोभनधनयुक्तं कुदीदितिं शोभनदीप्तिं सुप्रतूर्तिं शोभनप्रतरणं कर्मानु-
> ष्ठातृभिः सुखेन गंतव्यमनेहसमुपद्रवरहितमेतादृशं देवं त्वामूतये रक्ष-
> णाय ववृमहे । वृणीमहे ॥ सखायः । ख्या प्रकथने । समाने ख्यश्चोदात्त इति
> समान उपपदे डिदित्यनुवृत्तौ ख्यातेरिण् । तस्य डित्वाट्टलोपः । तत्संनियोगेन
> यलोपः । समानस्य छंदस्यमूर्धोति सभावः । उपपदस्योदात्तत्वं । णिदित्यनु
> वृत्तौ सख्युरसंबुद्धौ । पा. ७-१-९२ । इति णिद्वद्भावः । वृद्धायादेशौ । अद्यु-
> दात्तः । ववृमहे । वृज् संभक्तौ । छंदसि लुङ्गिट् इति वर्तमाने लिट् । क्रादि-
> त्पादिडभावः । निघातः । ऊतये । अव रक्षण इत्यस्य उदात्त इत्यनुवृत्तावूतियूती.

> *viśvāmitraḥ sauti | he āgne sakhāyaḥ somājyādihavispadānenopakāraka-*
> *tvānmitrāṇi martaso manuṣyā ṛtvijo vayamapāṃ napātamapāṃ naptāraṃ subhagaṃ*
> *śobhanadhanayuktaṃ kudīditiṃ śobhanadīptiṃ supratūrtiṃ śobhanaprataraṇaṃ karmānu-*
> *ṣṭhātṛbhiḥ sukhena gaṃtavyamanehasamupadravarahitametādṛśaṃ devaṃ tvāmūtaye rakṣa-*
> *ṇāya vavṛmahe | vṛṇīmahe ‖ sakhāyaḥ | khyā prakathane | samāne khyaścodātta iti*
> *samāna upapade ḍidityanuvṛttau khyāteriṇ | tasya ḍitvāṭṭalopaḥ | tatsaṃniyogena*
> *yalopaḥ | samānasya chaṃdasyamūrdhoti sabhāvaḥ | upapadasyodāttatvaṃ | ṇidityanu*
> *vṛttau sakhyurasaṃbuddhau | pā. 7-1-92 | iti ṇidvadbhāvaḥ | vṛddhāyādeśau | adyu-*
> *dāttaḥ | vavṛmahe | vṛj saṃbhaktau | chaṃdasi luṅgiṭ iti vartamāne liṭ | krādi-*
> *tpādiḍabhāvaḥ | nighātaḥ | ūtaye | ava rakṣaṇa ityasya udātta ityanuvṛttāvūtiyūtī.*

*Gist: O Agni, we priests, your friends through our oblations, choose you, the grandson of the waters, rich, bright, easily approached and free from harm, for protection. (The grammatical tail, on सखायः (*sakhāyaḥ*), ववृमहे (*vavṛmahe*) and ऊतये (*ūtaye*), is transcribed, not explained; it continues on the next page. The bhāṣya reads कुदीदितिं (*kudīditiṃ*) [sic] where the Saṃhitā and Pada have सुदीदितिं (*sudīditiṃ*).)*

### Printed p. 120

*[Running head, p. 120: 120 Sāyaṇabhāṣyasahitā [Maṃ. 3. A. 1. Sū. 9.]]*

*[The bhāṣya of Ṛk 1 continues from the previous page (its last words, the grammatical tail, on ऊतये (*ūtaye*), अपां (*apāṃ*), सुभगं (*subhagaṃ*) and सुप्रतूर्तिं (*supratūrtiṃ*), are transcribed, not explained).]*

> त्यादिना क्तिनंतत्वेन निपातनादंतोदात्तः । अपां । ऊडिदमिति विभक्तेरुदात्तत्वं ।
> सुभगं । आद्युदात्तं द्वैच् छंदसीत्युत्तरपदाद्युदात्तत्वं । सुप्रतूर्तिं । क्रत्वाद-
> यश्चेत्युत्तरपदाद्युदात्तत्वं ॥

**प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)**

(हे आग्ने (*he āgne*) — O Agni); सखायः (*sakhāyaḥ*) — friends (by offering oblations such as soma and ghee); मर्तासः (*martāsaḥ*) — we, men in the form of priests; अपां नपतं (*apāṃ napataṃ*) [sic, the Saṃhitā has नपातं (*napātaṃ*)] — the grandson of the waters; सुभगं (*subhagaṃ*) — endowed with excellent wealth; सुदीधितिं (*sudīdhitiṃ*) [sic, the Saṃhitā has सुदीदितिं (*sudīditiṃ*)] — of good brilliance; सुप्रतूर्तिं (*supratūrtiṃ*) — one who gives auspicious gifts (or: easily reached by those who perform the rites); अनेहसं (*anehasaṃ*) — free from harm; देवं त्वां (*devaṃ tvāṃ*) — you, of the nature of a god; ऊतये (*ūtaye*) — for protection; ववृमहे (*vavṛmahe*) — we call.

**भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)**

O Agni, grandson of the waters, rich in excellent wealth, of fine radiance, a good giver (easy of access to those who perform the rites), free from harm, of divine nature: we, who are your friends because we offer oblations to you, and who are men in the form of priests, desire protection and call you.

**Printed English translation**

We your mortal friends have recourse for our protection to you the divine, the grandson of the waters, the auspicious, the resplendent, the acces- sible [sic, "acces- sible" for "accessible"], the sinless.

**विशेषविषयगळु (Viśeṣaviṣayagaḷu)**

सखायः— सोमाज्यादिहविष्पदानेन उपकारत्वात् मित्राणि (*sakhāyaḥ— somājyādihaviṣpadānena upakāratvāt mitrāṇi*) — for this word Yāska has given the meaning समानख्याना ऋत्विजः (*samānakhyānā ṛtvijaḥ*) (priests of the same utterance). Sāyaṇa explains that because the priests offer to Agni oblations such as soma and ghee, and perform the work of friends (protection), they are friends of Agni.

मर्तासः—मनुष्याः ऋत्विजः (*martāsaḥ—manuṣyāḥ ṛtvijaḥ*) — since the word मर्त (*marta*) is stated among the names of men (Ni. 3-8), he gives the meaning मनुष्याः ऋत्विजः वयं (*manuṣyāḥ ṛtvijaḥ vayaṃ*), "we priests who are men".

अपांनपातं-अपां नप्तारं (*apāṃnapātaṃ-apāṃ naptāraṃ*) — the meaning is "grandson of the waters". Yāska explains this with अपान्न पात् तनूनप्ता, व्याख्यातः (*apānna pāt tanūnaptā, vyākhyātaḥ*) (the text has याग्तरु (*yāgtaru*) [sic] for "Yāska"), and

### Printed p. 121

*[Running head, p. 121: [8. A. 1. V. 5] Ṛgvedasaṃhitā 121 (as supplied, "೮. ಅ. ೧. ವ. ೫"; the first numeral does not agree with the Maṇḍala number 3 of the other running heads, left as supplied)]*

*[Special Topics of Ṛk 1 continued from the previous page.]*

… having explained it so, he cites the Ṛk (the text has मक्कळन्नु (*makkaḷannu*) [sic], evidently for "Ṛk"):

यो अनिध्मो दीदयदप्स्व १ स्तर्यं विप्रास ईळते अध्वरेषु । अपां नपान्मधुमतीरपो दा याभिरिंद्रो वावृथे वीर्याय ॥ (*yo anidhmo dīdayadapsva 1 staryaṃ viprāsa īḷate adhvareṣu | apāṃ napānmadhumatīrapo dā yābhiriṃdro vāvṛthe vīryāya ‖*) [?] (Pu. Saṃ. 10-30-4, as printed; a stray digit १ (*1*) stands inside the quoted word अप्स्व १ स्तर्यं (*apsva 1 staryaṃ*) in the supplied text, left as supplied)

and, citing this ṛk (the text again has मक्कळन्नु (*makkaḷannu*) [sic]):

तनूनपात् पथ ऋतस्य यानान्मध्वा समंजन्त स्वधया सुजिह्व । मन्मानि धीभिरुत यज्ञमृंधन् देवत्रा च कृणुह्यध्वरं नः ॥ (*tanūnapāt patha ṛtasya yānānmadhvā samaṃjanta svadhayā sujihva | manmāni dhībhiruta yajñamṛṃdhan devatrā ca kṛṇuhyadhvaraṃ naḥ ‖*) (Ma. Saṃ. 10-110-2, as printed)

For the word तनूनपात् (*tanūnapāt*) in this ṛk Yāska states the opinions of two authorities: तनूनपादाज्यमिति कात्क्यः अग्निरितिशाकपूणिः (*tanūnapādājyamiti kātkyaḥ agniritiśākapūṇiḥ*), "Kātkya says ghee, Śākapūṇi says Agni". Then, गौरत्र तनुरुच्यते, नपादित्यनंतरायाः प्रजाया नामधेयं (*gauratra tanurucyate, napādityanaṃtarāyāḥ prajāyā nāmadheyaṃ*): here तनु (*tanu*) is said to be the cow (गौः (*gauḥ*)), and नपात् (*napāt*) is a name for a descendant not immediately next. He points out the awkwardness of Kātkya's view, which gives the word तनूनपात् (*tanūnapāt*) the meaning "ghee" on the ground that ghee is produced from the cow through the milk (the text has वयस्सिन मूलक (*vayassina mūlaka*), "through age", which seems out of place; left as supplied). Then he explains: आपोऽत्र तन्न उच्यंते, तता अंतरिक्षे ताभ्य ओषधिवनस्पतयो जायंते ओषधिवनस्पतिभ्यः एष जायते (*āpo 'tra tanna ucyaṃte, tatā aṃtarikṣe tābhya oṣadhivanaspatayo jāyaṃte oṣadhivanaspatibhyaḥ eṣa jāyate*) (the text has तन्न (*tanna*) and तता (*tatā*) [sic]). Durgācārya, on नपात् इति अनन्तरायाः प्रजायाः नामधेयं (*napāt iti anantarāyāḥ prajāyāḥ nāmadheyaṃ*), says: one produced from the father is the son; one produced from the son is next in order (anantara) and is lower than the son. Hence, on the view of those who take the word तनु (*tanu*) as "cow", the view of Kātkya that ghee and the like come from the cow and Agni from ghee and the like is not acceptable to Yāska. He describes, further, the view of Śākapūṇi: since the waters are the meaning of the word अप् (*ap*), the waters are spread in the atmosphere; from these waters come the plants and trees, and from these Agni; therefore Agni is designated both by the word तनूनपात् (*tanūnapāt*) and by the word अपान्न पात् (*apānna pāt*). From this the meaning is that Agni is the grandson of the waters (the text has नॊम्मगनु (*nòmmaganu*) [sic] for "grandson").

सुप्रतूर्तिं (*supratūrtiṃ*) — because Agni is worthy of being easily worshipped by those who perform sacrifices and other rites, it is explained that he is सुप्रतरण (*suprataraṇa*), the remover of the troubles of the sacrificers.

सुभगं अनेहसं सुदीदितिं देवं ऊतये ववृमहे—शोभनधनयुक्तं शोभनदीप्तिं, उपद्रवरहितं रक्षणाय वृणीमहे (*subhagaṃ anehasaṃ sudīditiṃ devaṃ ūtaye vavṛmahe—śobhanadhanayuktaṃ śobhanadīptiṃ, upadravarahitaṃ rakṣaṇāya vṛṇīmahe*) — it is explained: Agni, the god, who has excellent wealth and gives it to the sacrificers, who shines with auspicious brilliance, who is free from every trouble, to protect us, we priests choose (the text has वंसिसुत्तेवॆ (*vaṃsisuttevè*) [sic], for "we choose").

**Vyākaraṇa-prakriyā**

Grammar page, noted briefly. सखायः (*sakhāyaḥ*): root ख्या प्रकथने (*khyā prakathane*); by समाने ख्यश्चोदात्तः (*samāne khyaścodāttaḥ*) (Uṇ. Sū. 4-576, as printed) the affix इण् (*iṇ*) for this root when समान (*samāna*) stands as upapada; the affix being डित् (*ḍit*), the account goes on: "by the preceding …" (the page breaks off here; continued on the next page). (A stray "16" ends this page in the supplied text; left as supplied.)

### Printed p. 122

*[Running head, p. 122: 122 Sāyaṇabhāṣyasahitā [Maṃ. 3. A. 1. Sū. 9.]]*

*[Vyākaraṇa-prakriyā of Ṛk 1, continued from the previous page; grammar page, noted briefly.]* Continuing सखायः (*sakhāyaḥ*): as the sūtra carries on by anuvṛtti, the ṭi of the root is dropped by the force of the affix being ḍit, and the y with it; by समानस्य छंदसि (*samānasya chaṃdasi*) (Pā. Sū. 6 3-74, as printed) समान (*samāna*) becomes स (*sa*)-; the word becomes सख (*sakha*), the upapada takes the udātta accent; before जस् (*jas*) the rule सुख्युरसंबुद्धौ (*sukhyurasaṃbuddhau*) (Pā. Sū. 7-1-97, as printed) makes it ṇit-like, vṛddhi of the i and ayādeśa follow, and the word is ādyudātta. ववृमहे (*vavṛmahe*): root वृज् संभक्तौ (*vṛj saṃbhaktau*); छंदसि लुज्ज्‌लज्‌लिटः (*chaṃdasi lujjlajliṭaḥ*) gives liṭ in the present sense, the affix महिज् (*mahij*) in the first person plural, ṭi becomes e; कृसृभृवृ (*kṛsṛbhṛvṛ*) (Pā. Sū. 7-2-13) forbids the iḍ-augment; reduplication of the root; nighāta accent (reason given as in the text). मर्तासः (*martāsaḥ*) (the text's headword reads मर्शासॆ (*marśāsè*) [sic]): root मृज् प्राणत्यागे (*mṛj prāṇatyāge*) [sic] with an Auṇādika affix तन् (*tan*), guṇa of the ṛ with r following, and असुक् (*asuk*) added to जस् (*jas*) by अज्जसेरसुक् (*ajjaserasuk*) (Pā. Sū. 7-1-50). ऊतये (*ūtaye*): root अव रक्षणे (*ava rakṣaṇe*); as ऊतियूतिजूति (*ūtiyūtijūti*) (Pā. Sū. 3-0-97, as printed) is a nipātana ending in क्तिन् (*ktin*) it is antodātta; dative singular. अपां (*apāṃ*): genitive plural; by ऊडिदंपदादि (*ūḍidaṃpadādi*) (Pā. Sū. 6-1-171) the case-ending takes the udātta. सुभगं (*subhagaṃ*): bahuvrīhi; by आद्युदात्तं द्व्यज् छंदसि (*ādyudāttaṃ dvyaj chaṃdasi*) (Pā. Sū. 6-2-119) the latter member is ādyudātta. सुप्रतूर्तिं (*supratūrtiṃ*): also bahuvrīhi, in the क्रत्वादि (*kratvādi*) group, so by क्रत्वादयश्च (*kratvādayaśca*) (Pā. Sū. 6-2-118) the latter member is ādyudātta.

#### Ṛk 2

**॥ संहितापाठः ॥**

> काय॑मानो व॒ना त्वं यन्मा॒त्वरज॑गन्न॒पः ।
> न तत्ते॑ अग्ने प्र॒मृषे॑ नि॒वर्त॑नं॒ यद्दू॒रे सन्नि॒हाभ॑वः ॥ २ ॥

> *kāyamāno vanā tvaṃ yanmātvarajagannapaḥ |*
> *na tatte agne pramṛṣe nivartanaṃ yaddūre sannihābhavaḥ ‖ 2 ‖*
