**॥ पदपाठः ॥**

> कायमानः । वना । त्वं । यत् । मात्यः । अजगन् । अपः ।
> न । तत् । ते । अग्ने प्रमृषे । निवर्तनं । यत् । दूरे । सन् । इह । अभवः ॥

**॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥**

> हे अग्ने वना काननानि कायमानः कामयमानस्त्वं यद्यस्मात्कारणात्तानि
> विजाय मात्यरपो मातृभूता आपोऽजगन् । आगमः । गतवानसि । अप्सु प्रविष्टत्वा-
> च्छांतो वर्तसे तत्तस्मात्ते तव निवर्तनं ! नितरां शत्रैव वर्तनं । तेन च
> विनाशो लक्ष्यते । न विनाशो न प्रमृषे । स प्रमृष्यते । न सह्यते । कुत इत्यत
> आह । यद्यस्मात्कारणाद्दरे सन् अदृश्यतया वर्तमानस्त्वमिहास्मत्संबंधिष्वरणि-
> रूपेषु काष्टेष्टभवः । मंथनात् क्षण पात्रेणास्माकं समीपे भवसि । तस्मात्तव
> दूरतो वर्तनमस्मभ्यं न रोचते । अस्मिन्नर्थे यास्कः । कायमानश्चायमानः
> कामयमान इति वा । वनानि त्वं यन्मात्यरपोऽगम उपशाम्यन् । न तत्ते अग्ने
> प्रमृष्यते निवर्तनं दूरे यत्सन्निह भवसि जायमानः । नि. ४-१४ । इति ॥ काय-
> मानः । कमु कांत् । कमेर्णिज् लटः शानच् । मकारलोपश्रांदसः । अजगन् ।
> गम्लृ गतावित्यस्य लजि बहुलं छंदसीति शपः श्लुः । हल्यादिना सिलोपः । मो
> नो धातोरिति नकारः । यच्छब्दयोगादनिघातः । अडागमस्वरः । प्रमृषे । मृष
> तितिक्षायां । व्यत्ययेन कर्मणि तप्रत्ययः । लोपस्त आत्मनेपदेष्टिति तलोपः ।
> धातुस्वरः । अभवः । छंदसि लुज्जि ज्लिट इति लडर्थॆ लज् । यद्योगादनिघातः ॥

> *he agne vanā kānanāni kāyamānaḥ kāmayamānastvaṃ yadyasmātkāraṇāttāni*
> *vijāya mātyarapo mātṛbhūtā āpo 'jagan | āgamaḥ | gatavānasi | apsu praviṣṭatvā-*
> *cchāṃto vartase tattasmātte tava nivartanaṃ ! nitarāṃ śatraiva vartanaṃ | tena ca*
> *vināśo lakṣyate | na vināśo na pramṛṣe | sa pramṛṣyate | na sahyate | kuta ityata*
> *āha | yadyasmātkāraṇāddare san adṛśyatayā vartamānastvamihāsmatsaṃbaṃdhiṣvaraṇi-*
> *rūpeṣu kāṣṭeṣṭabhavaḥ | maṃthanāt kṣaṇa pātreṇāsmākaṃ samīpe bhavasi | tasmāttava*
> *dūrato vartanamasmabhyaṃ na rocate | asminnarthe yāskaḥ | kāyamānaścāyamānaḥ*
> *kāmayamāna iti vā | vanāni tvaṃ yanmātyarapo 'gama upaśāmyan | na tatte agne*
> *pramṛṣyate nivartanaṃ dūre yatsanniha bhavasi jāyamānaḥ | ni. 4-14 | iti ‖ kāya-*
> *mānaḥ | kamu kāṃt | kamerṇij laṭaḥ śānac | makāralopaśrāṃdasaḥ | ajagan |*
> *gamlṛ gatāvityasya laji bahulaṃ chaṃdasīti śapaḥ śluḥ | halyādinā silopaḥ | mo*
> *no dhātoriti nakāraḥ | yacchabdayogādanighātaḥ | aḍāgamasvaraḥ | pramṛṣe | mṛṣa*
> *titikṣāyāṃ | vyatyayena karmaṇi tapratyayaḥ | lopasta ātmanepadeṣṭiti talopaḥ |*
> *dhātusvaraḥ | abhavaḥ | chaṃdasi lujji jliṭa iti laḍarthè laj | yadyogādanighātaḥ ‖*

*Gist: O Agni, desiring the forests you went to the waters, your mothers, and became calm there; yet by churning you appear at once in our kindling-sticks, so your staying far off pleases us not. (Yāska is quoted; the grammatical tail is transcribed, not explained. The Saṃhitā block as supplied reads मात्वरजगन्नपः (*mātvarajagannapaḥ*) where the Pada and bhāṣya read मात्यः (*mātyaḥ*) / मात्यरपो (*mātyarapo*): an OCR variant of the supplied text, left as supplied.)*

**प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)**

(हे (*he*)) अग्ने (*agne*) — O fire. वना (*vanā*) — the forests. कायमानः (*kāyamānaḥ*) — desiring. त्वं (*tvaṃ*) — you. यत् (*yat*) — for which reason. मातृः (*mātṛḥ*) [as supplied] — (to you) of the nature of mothers [the supplied text has a garbled word here, not reproduced]. अपः (*apaḥ*) — the waters (toward). अजगन् (*ajagan*) — you went; and यत् (*yat*) — for which reason. दूरे सन् (*dūre san*) — being far away (invisible within the water, you). इह (*iha*) — here (in the kindling-sticks, the aranis, that are ours). अभवः (*abhavaḥ*) — are present. तत् (*tat*) — for that reason. ते (*te*) — your. निवर्तनं (*nivartanaṃ*) — constant dwelling (in the water; destruction). न प्रमृषे (*na pramṛṣe*) — is not tolerated.

**भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)**

O Agni, you who wish to be in the forests left them, joined the waters and became calm; and though thus invisible in the water, you appear in our kindling-sticks (as soon as you are churned). Therefore your dwelling far off (in the midst of the water) does not please us.

### Printed p. 124

*[Running head, p. 124: 124 Sāyaṇabhāṣyasahitā [Maṃ. 3. A. 1. Sū. 9.]]*

**Printed English translation**

Desirous as you are of the forests since you have repaired to the maternal waters, Agni, your tarrying so far away is not to be endured ; (in a moment) you are here with us.

**विशेषविषयगळु (Viśeṣaviṣayagaḷu)**

वना—काननानि (*vanā—kānanāni*) — the word वन (*vana*) is also stated among the names of rays (Ni. 2 15, as printed). And in नियद्वृणक्षि श्वसनस्य मूर्धनि शुष्कस्य चिद्वृंदिनो रोरुवद्वना (*niyadvṛṇakṣi śvasanasya mūrdhani śuṣkasya cidvṛṃdino roruvadvanā*) (no source given in the text; the Ṛk is quoted as supplied) Yāska has explained the word वना (*vanā*) as वनानि इति वा वधेनेति वा (*vanāni iti vā vadheneti vā*); accordingly Sāyaṇa gives the meaning वनानि (*vanāni*) "forests", and for कायमानः (*kāyamānaḥ*) the meaning कामयमानः (*kāmayamānaḥ*), "one who wishes for".

अपः अजगन्—मातृभूताः अपः गतवानसि (*apaḥ ajagan—mātṛbhūtāḥ apaḥ gatavānasi*) — by the word अपांनपात् (*apāṃnapāt*) it was shown that water is the mother-place of Agni. Therefore Agni has entered the waters, which are like a mother to him and nourish through the herbs and trees produced from water; and the words अप्पु प्रविष्टत्वात् शांतोऽसि (*appu praviṣṭatvāt śāṃto 'si*) [sic, अप्पु (*appu*) for the bhāṣya's अप्सु (*apsu*)] are explained thus: since Agni is in the waters, he is in a state of calm.

निवर्तनं न प्रमृषे—नितरां तत्त्रैव वर्तनं तेन च विनाशो लक्ष्यते । सविनाशः न प्रमृष्यते न सह्यते (*nivartanaṃ na pramṛṣe—nitarāṃ tattraiva vartanaṃ tena ca vināśo lakṣyate | savināśaḥ na pramṛṣyate na sahyate*) — the word वर्तन (*vartana*) is formed from the root वृतु (*vṛtu*) and is used here with the prefix नि (*ni*). As stated in उपसर्गेण धात्वर्थो बलादन्यत्र नीयते । प्रहाराहारसंहारविहारपरिहारवत् (*upasargeṇa dhātvartho balādanyatra nīyate | prahārāhārasaṃhāravihāraparihāravat*) (gloss mine and tentative: "by a prefix the meaning of the root is forcibly led elsewhere, as in prahāra, āhāra, saṃhāra, vihāra, parihāra"), the word वर्तन (*vartana*) with the prefix नि (*ni*) conveys the meaning "destruction", and also the state that precedes destruction. By this, Agni's staying in the waters is not tolerated by the sacrificers; that is, Agni's becoming invisible is not to be tolerated.

यत् दूरे सन् इह अभवः । अदृश्यतया वर्तमानः अग्नि: अस्मत्संबंधिषु अरणिरूपेषु काष्ठेषु मंथनात् क्षणमात्रेण अस्माकं समीपे भवति तस्मात् अग्नेः दूरतः वर्तनं अस्माकं न रोचते (*yat dūre san iha abhavaḥ | adṛśyatayā vartamānaḥ agni: asmatsaṃbaṃdhiṣu araṇirūpeṣu kāṣṭheṣu maṃthanāt kṣaṇamātreṇa asmākaṃ samīpe bhavati tasmāt agneḥ dūrataḥ vartanaṃ asmākaṃ na rocate*) — it is explained that Agni's being in the waters in an invisible form (a form not seen by the eye) is his manner of being far away, and that his appearing visibly at that very moment, when churned in the kindling-sticks of those who perform the sacrifice, is his manner of being near. Of the two, the sacrificers prefer Agni to be near rather than far. Yāska quoted this same Ṛk in the Nirukta and stated his own view clearly:

### Printed p. 125

*[Running head, p. 125: [A. 3. A. 1. V. 5.] Ṛgvedasaṃhitā 125 (as supplied, "ಅ. ೩. ಅ. ೧. ವ. ೫.")]*

कायमानश्चायमानः कामयमान इति वा वनानि त्वं यन्मात्यरपोगम उपशाम्यन् न तत्ते अग्ने प्रमष्यते निवर्तनं दूरे यत्सन्निह भवसि जायमानः (*kāyamānaścāyamānaḥ kāmayamāna iti vā vanāni tvaṃ yanmātyarapogama upaśāmyan na tatte agne pramaṣyate nivartanaṃ dūre yatsanniha bhavasi jāyamānaḥ*) (A. 4-14, as printed; the text has मात्यरपोगम (*mātyarapogama*) and प्रमष्यते (*pramaṣyate*) [sic] where the bhāṣya has मात्यरपोऽगम (*mātyarapo 'gama*) and प्रमृष्यते (*pramṛṣyate*)). Sāyaṇa has explained this same view stated in the Nirukta.

**Vyākaraṇa-prakriyā**

Grammar page, noted briefly. कायमानः (*kāyamānaḥ*): root कमु कांत् (*kamu kāṃt*) [sic, as supplied]; by कमेर्णिज् (*kamerṇij*) (Pā. Sū. 3-1-30) the affix णिज् (*ṇij*) in the root's own sense; vṛddhi of the penultimate by आत उपधायाः (*āta upadhāyāḥ*) (Pā. Sū. 7-2-116); the णिजंत (*ṇijaṃta*) is a root; लट् (*laṭ*) is replaced by शानच् (*śānac*); guṇa and ayādeśa; आनेमुक् (*ānemuk*) (Pā. Sū. 7-2-82) gives the augment मुक् (*muk*); the m of the root is dropped in the Veda; the root accent remains. वना (*vanā*): neuter plural; शि (*śi*) is dropped by छंदसि बहुलं (*chaṃdasi bahulaṃ*) (Pā. Sū. 6-1-70). अजगन् (*ajagan*): root गमल्य गतौ (*gamalya gatau*) [sic, as supplied]; लज् (*laj*) with तिप् (*tip*); इतश्च (*itaśca*) drops the i; शप् (*śap*) becomes शु (*śu*) by बहुलं छंदसि (*bahulaṃ chaṃdasi*); the ending is dropped; मोनो धातोः (*mono dhātoḥ*) (Pā. Sū. 8-2-64) changes m to n; augment अड् (*aḍ*); because the word यत् (*yat*) is connected there is no nighāta; ādyudātta by the udātta augment. प्रमृषे (*pramṛṣe*): root मृष तितीक्षायां (*mṛṣa titīkṣāyāṃ*); लट् (*laṭ*) in the passive, त (*ta*) ending, e by टित आत्मने (*ṭita ātmane*); no यक् (*yak*) by vyatyaya; the a of the ending dropped by लोपस्त आत्मने पदेषु (*lopasta ātmane padeṣu*) (Pā. Sū. 7-1-41); no nighāta because of न (*na*); तिजिचोदात्तवति (*tijicodāttavati*) (Pā. Sū. 8-1-71, as printed, with पा (*pā*) and सू. (*sū.*) split by a line break) makes प्र (*pra*) anudātta. निवर्तनं (*nivartanaṃ*): root वृतु वर्तने (*vṛtu vartane*); ल्युट् (*lyuṭ*) in the sense of the action; युवोरनाकौ (*yuvoranākau*) (Pā. Sū. 7-1-1) gives अन (*ana*); लिति (*liti*) (Pā. Sū. 6-1-193) gives udātta before the affix; as a prādi-compound it takes the accent of the kṛdanta latter member. अभवः (*abhavaḥ*): root भू सत्तायां (*bhū sattāyāṃ*); by छंदसि (*chaṃdasi*) … (Pā. Sū. 3-4.6, as printed) … (the page breaks off here, mid-word: ऎंबु (*èṃbu*); continued on the next page).

### Printed p. 126

*[Running head, p. 126: 126 Sāyaṇabhāṣyasahitā [Maṃ. 3. A. 1. Sū. 9]]*

*[Vyākaraṇa-prakriyā of Ṛk 2, continued from the previous page; grammar page, noted briefly.]* Continuing अभवः (*abhavaḥ*) the form is लज् (*laj*) in the present sense, second person singular; because the word यत् (*yat*) is connected, the nighāta accent does not occur; by the accent of the augment अट् (*aṭ*) the word is ādyudātta.

#### Ṛk 3

**॥ संहितापाठः ॥**

> अति॑ तृ॒ष्टं व॑वक्षि॒थाथै॒व सु॒मना॑ असि ।
> प्रप्रा॒न्ये यंति॒ पर्य॒न्य आ॑सते॒ येषां॑ स॒ख्ये असि॑ श्रि॒तः ॥

**॥ पदपाठः ॥**

> अति । तृष्टं । ववक्षिथ । अथ । एव । सुमनाः । असि ।
> द्रप्र । अन्वे । यंति । परि । अन्वे । आसते । येषां । सख्ये । आसि । श्रितः ॥ ३ ॥

**॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥**

> हे अग्ने तृष्टं स्तोतुस्त्वाभिलाषं तदुचितफलप्रदानेन सफलं कर्तु-
> मतिववक्षिथ । अतिशयेन वोढुमिच्छसि । आथैव अथापि त्वं सुमना असि । संतुष्ट-
> मनस्को वर्तसे । किंच ! त्वं येषां षोडशानामृत्विजां सख्ये मैत्रे श्रितोऽसि
> वर्तसे तेषां मध्येऽन्येऽध्वर्युप्रभृतयो द्वादशर्त्विजः प्रयंति । प्रकर्षण
> होतुं गच्छंति । अन्य उद्गातृप्रभृतयश्चत्वारः पर्यासते । परित आसीना वर्तंते॥
> ववक्षितथ । वह प्रापण इत्यस्य सनि रूपं । अभ्यासस्य सन्यत इतीत्वाभावश्कांदसः । तदं-
> ताद्वर्तमाने लिट् । फल अर्धधातुकस्येड्वलादेरितीट् । कास्पत्ययाडामित्याम्
> न भवति मंत्रत्वात् । निघातः । प्रप्र । प्रसमःपोद इति द्विवचनं । यंति । इष्
> गतावित्यस्य लटि रूपं । एकान्याभ्यां समर्थाभ्यामित्यन्यशब्दयोगात्पूर्वस्य निघाता-
> भावः । आसते । आस उपवेशने । उत्तरत्वान्नि घातः । असि । आस भुवि । शासस्तो-
> र्लोप इति सकारलोपः । यद्वृत्तयोगान्न निहन्यते ॥

> *he agne tṛṣṭaṃ stotustvābhilāṣaṃ taducitaphalapradānena saphalaṃ kartu-*
> *mativavakṣitha | atiśayena voḍhumicchasi | āthaiva athāpi tvaṃ sumanā asi | saṃtuṣṭa-*
> *manasko vartase | kiṃca ! tvaṃ yeṣāṃ ṣoḍaśānāmṛtvijāṃ sakhye maitre śrito 'si*
> *vartase teṣāṃ madhye 'nye 'dhvaryuprabhṛtayo dvādaśartvijaḥ prayaṃti | prakarṣaṇa*
> *hotuṃ gacchaṃti | anya udgātṛprabhṛtayaścatvāraḥ paryāsate | parita āsīnā vartaṃte‖*
> *vavakṣitatha | vaha prāpaṇa ityasya sani rūpaṃ | abhyāsasya sanyata itītvābhāvaśkāṃdasaḥ | tadaṃ-*
> *tādvartamāne liṭ | phala ardhadhātukasyeḍvalāderitīṭ | kāspatyayāḍāmityām*
> *na bhavati maṃtratvāt | nighātaḥ | prapra | prasamaḥpoda iti dvivacanaṃ | yaṃti | iṣ*
> *gatāvityasya laṭi rūpaṃ | ekānyābhyāṃ samarthābhyāmityanyaśabdayogātpūrvasya nighātā-*
> *bhāvaḥ | āsate | āsa upaveśane | uttaratvānni ghātaḥ | asi | āsa bhuvi | śāsasto-*
> *rlopa iti sakāralopaḥ | yadvṛttayogānna nihanyate ‖*

*Gist: O Agni, you wish to bear the worshipper's desire to fulfilment and so are well-pleased; of the sixteen priests in whose friendship you rest, twelve go forth to offer and four sit around. (The grammatical tail is transcribed, not explained: ववक्षिथ (*vavakṣitha*), प्रप्र (*prapra*), यंति (*yaṃti*), आसते (*āsate*), आसि (*āsi*). The text prints ववक्षितथ (*vavakṣitatha*) in the tail where the Pada has ववक्षिथ (*vavakṣitha*), तृष्टं (*tṛṣṭaṃ*) where the printed English later suggests तृष्णं (*tṛṣṇaṃ*), and द्रप्र (*drapra*) in the Pada for प्रप्र (*prapra*): OCR variants of the supplied text, left as supplied.)*

**प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)**

(हे अग्ने (*he agne*) — O Agni) तृष्टं (*tṛṣṭaṃ*) — the thirst (desire) of the worshipper (to make fruitful). अति ववक्षिथ (*ati vavakṣitha*) — you wish to bear greatly. अथ एव (*atha eva*) — for that very reason. सुमनाः आसि (*sumanāḥ āsi*) — you are of a contented mind; (and) येषां (*yeṣāṃ*) — of which sixteen priests. सख्ये (*sakhye*) — in friendship. श्रितः आसि (*śritaḥ āsi*) — you are resting. (तेषां (*teṣāṃ*) — among them)

### Printed p. 127

*[Running head, p. 127: [A. 3. A. 1. V. 5] Ṛgvedasaṃhitā 127 (as supplied, "ಅ. ೩. ಅ. ೧. ವ. ೫")]*

अन्ये (*anye*) — some (the twelve priests beginning with the Adhvaryu). प्र प्र यंति (*pra pra yaṃti*) — go forth (to offer the oblation). अन्ये (*anye*) — (and) some others (the four beginning with the Udgātṛ). परि आसते (*pari āsate*) — sit all around.

**भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)**

O Agni, you are well-pleased [the text reads "ಪೂರ್ಣಮಾಡುವುದಕ್ಕೋಸ್ಕರಲೇ" with a stray letter; sense: for the very purpose of fulfilling the worshipper's desire]; of the sixteen priests regarded by you as friends, the twelve beginning with the Adhvaryu have set out to make the offering in abundance, and the remaining four (beginning with the Udgātṛ) sit all around. (They are seated.)

**Printed English translation**

you wish exceedingly to convey satisfaction (to your worshipper) and are thereto well-disposed ; of those in whose friendship you are cherished some precede whilst others sit around.

**विशेषविषयगळु (Viśeṣaviṣayagaḷu)**

तृष्णं ववक्षिथ (*tṛṣṇaṃ vavakṣitha*) — [as supplied; the Saṃhitā reads तृष्टं (*tṛṣṭaṃ*)] With this sentence Agni is addressed: स्तोतुः तृष्णाभिलाषं सफलं कर्तुं तत्तदुचितफलप्रदानेन अतिशयेन वोढुमिच्छसि (*stotuḥ tṛṣṇābhilāṣaṃ saphalaṃ kartuṃ tattaducitaphalapradānena atiśayena voḍhumicchasi*). It is described that he is desirous of bearing abundant oblation from the sacrificer, in order to give the fruit corresponding to the desire of the sacrificer who praises him, in order to make the sacrificer one who has gained his purpose, and in order to show his own affection for the sacrificer. And सुमना असि (*sumanā asi*) is further explained as: he is of a mind that is joyful toward the sacrificer.

अन्ये प्रयंति अन्ये परि आसते (*anye prayaṃti anye pari āsate*) — Agni is affectionate toward all sixteen priests of the sacrifice, the Hotṛ and the others, because they serve him. Among them अध्वर्युप्रभृतयो द्वादश ऋत्विजः प्रकर्णण होतुं गच्छंति (*adhvaryuprabhṛtayo dvādaśa ṛtvijaḥ prakarṇaṇa hotuṃ gacchaṃti*) [sic, प्रकर्णण (*prakarṇaṇa*) as supplied; the bhāṣya reads प्रकर्षण (*prakarṣaṇa*)] — the twelve priests beginning with the Adhvaryu are ready to offer oblations and perform the homa to satisfy Agni. अन्ये उद्गातृप्रभृतयः चत्वारः परित आसीना वर्तंते (*anye udgātṛprabhṛtayaḥ catvāraḥ parita āsīnā vartaṃte*) — the four priests beginning with the Udgātṛ, as praisers of Agni, sit around him in the sacrificial hall, ready to chant. Thus the duties performed by the priests belonging to the sacrifice are briefly indicated here.

सख्यॆ (*sakhyè*) — मित्रत्व (*mitratva*); the meaning is the state of friendship. The Nirukta authors—

### Printed p. 128

*[Running head, p. 128: 128 Sāyaṇabhāṣyasahitā [Maṃ. 3. A. 1. Sū. 9.]]*

(continuing the Nirukta citation) सक्तुमिव तितउना पुनंतो यत्र धीरा मनसा वाचमक्रत । अश्रा सखायस्सख्यानि जानते भद्रैषां लक्ष्मीर्निहिताधि वाचि ॥ (*saktumiva titaunā punaṃto yatra dhīrā manasā vācamakrata | aśrā sakhāyassakhyāni jānate bhadraiṣāṃ lakṣmīrnihitādhi vāci ‖*) (Ṛ. Saṃ. 10-71-2, as printed; the text's तितउना (*titaunā*) is as supplied — a gloss would be mine and tentative: "as one purifies barley-meal with a sieve, where the wise fashioned speech with the mind; there friends know the marks of friendship; their auspicious mark is placed in speech"). In this Ṛk, the meaning सखायः सख्यानि संजानते (*sakhāyaḥ sakhyāni saṃjānate*), "friends recognise the states of friendship", is explained. Durgācārya (explains): समानख्यानाः समानख्यानानामेव सखायः, वैयाकरणानां वैयाकरणा एव नैरुक्तानां नैरुक्ता एव सखिभावान् संजानते विज्ञानानि संजानते इतरेतरस्य यो विज्ञानप्रकर्षः तं जानते (*samānakhyānāḥ samānakhyānānāmeva sakhāyaḥ, vaiyākaraṇānāṃ vaiyākaraṇā eva nairuktānāṃ nairuktā eva sakhibhāvān saṃjānate vijñānāni saṃjānate itaretarasya yo vijñānaprakarṣaḥ taṃ jānate*). Those who have one kind of knowledge are friendly with those equally skilled; those who know grammar recognise each other's excellence of knowledge among grammarians, and those who know the Nirukta among the Nirukta-scholars. From this, affection arises among them. In the same way Agni, being renowned as the Hotṛ, is friendly with the priests; thus the meaning is explained.

**Vyākaraṇa-prakriyā**

Grammar page, noted briefly. ववक्षथ (*vavakṣatha*) [sic, as supplied; the bhāṣya reads ववक्षिथ (*vavakṣitha*)]: root वह प्रापणे (*vaha prāpaṇe*); the affix सन् (*san*) in the sense of wishing by धातोःकर्मणः (*dhātoḥkarmaṇaḥ*) (Pā. Sū. 3-1-6, as printed); doubling of the root by सन्यजोः (*sanyajoḥ*); the i that सन्यतः (*sanyataḥ*) would give to the reduplicative syllable does not occur in the Veda; होढः (*hoḍhaḥ*) changes h to ḍh, षढोःकसि (*ṣaḍhoḥkasi*) then gives ka, and ṣatva of the s follows; the form in सन् (*san*) is a root by सनाद्यंताधातवः (*sanādyaṃtādhātavaḥ*), takes लिट् (*liṭ*); the second person singular सिप् (*sip*) becomes थल् (*thal*) by परस्मैपदानां (*parasmaipadānāṃ*); the इड् (*iḍ*)-augment by अर्धधातुकस्येद्वलादेः (*ardhadhātukasyedvalādeḥ*) (Pā. Sū. 7-2-35, as printed, text अर्धधातुकस्यॆद्वरादेः (*ardhadhātukasyèdvarādeḥ*)); आम् (*ām*) does not occur, the prohibition अमंत्रे (*amaṃtre*) in कास्प्रत्ययादाममंत्रेलिटि (*kāspratyayādāmamaṃtreliṭi*) (Pā. Sū. 3-1-35) applying to the Veda; अतोलोपः (*atolopaḥ*) drops the a of the sanning (text: "ಲೋರ" [sic]); the nighāta accent arises as the word follows [a word garbled in the text: "ಅತಿ ಚಿಂತದ" — [?]]. सुमनाः (*sumanāḥ*): शोभनं मनः यस्य सः (*śobhanaṃ manaḥ yasya saḥ*); since the latter member ends in अस् (*as*), सोर्मवसी (*sormavasī*) [as supplied, with a stray underscore] (Pā. Sū. 6-2-117, as printed) gives the latter member's initial udātta. प्रप्र (*prapra*): in filling the pāda, doubling of प्र (*pra*) by प्रसमुपोद (*prasamupoda*) (Pā. Sū. 8-1-6, text without full stops); the second is called amreḍita and made udātta by अनुदात्तंच (*anudāttaṃca*) (Pā. Sū. 8-1-3). यंति (*yaṃti*): root इण् गत् (*iṇ gat*) [sic, as supplied]; लट् (*laṭ*) third plural, अंत् (*aṃt*) substituted; लुक् (*luk*) for शप् (*śap*); इणोयण् (*iṇoyaṇ*) (Pā. Sū. 6-4-81) gives यण् (*yaṇ*); because the word अन्य (*anya*) is connected, एकान्याध्यांसमर्थाभ्याम् (*ekānyādhyāṃsamarthābhyām*) [sic, as supplied] (Pā. Sū. 8-1-65) forbids the tiṅanta nighāta, so the ending's ādyudātta accent remains.

### Printed p. 129

*[Running head, p. 129: [A. 3. A. 1. V. 5] Ṛgvedasaṃhitā 129 (as supplied, "ಅ. ೩. ಅ. ೧. ವ. ೫.")]*

आसते (*āsate*)—root आस (*āsa*) in the sense of sitting; the present (laṭ) third person plural form. Although another word (अन्य (*anya*)) is in construction, the prohibition of nighāta does not apply because the word stands in the latter part of the sentence. By तिज्जतिजिः (*tijjatijiḥ*) [sic, as supplied] the all-anudātta accent arises.

असि (*asi*)—root अस (*asa*) in the sense of being; the affix sip in the present second person singular. By तासस्तो र्लोपः (*tāsasto rlopaḥ*) (Pā. Sū. 7-4-50) the s of the root is dropped. Because the relative word यत् (*yat*) is connected, the all-anudātta accent does not arise; the root accent remains.

#### Ṛk 4

**॥ संहितापाठः ॥**

> ई॒यि॒वांस॒मति॒ स्रिधः॒ शश्व॑ती॒रति॑ स॒श्चतः॑ ।
> अन्वी॑मविंदन्निचि॒रासो॑ अ॒द्रुहो॒प्सु सिं॒हमि॑व त्रि॒तं ॥४॥

**॥ पदपाठः ॥**

> ईयिवांसं । अति । स्रिधः । तत्वतीः । अति । सच्चतः ।
> अनु । ईं । अविंदन् । निचिरासः । अद्रुहः । अप्सु । सिंहंइव । श्रितं ॥

**॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥**

> हे अग्ने अद्रुहोऽद्रोद्धारो निचिरासो नितरां चिरासश्शिरंतना विश्वे
> देवाः स्रिधः शोषकाञ्त्रून्बलेनातिक्रम्येयिवांसं गच्छंतं तश्वतीर्बह्वीः सश्चतः
> संगतिकारिणीः सेनाश्चात्यतिक्रम्य गच्छंतमप्पु श्रितं तिरोहितमत एव सिंहमिव गुहा-
> प्रविष्टं सिंहमिव भीममीमेनं त्वामन्वविंदन् । अनुगम्यालभंत ॥ ईयिवांसं ।
> इण् गतावित्यस्य क्वसावुपेयिवाननाश्वाननूचानश्च । पा. ३-२-१०९ । इति निपातितः ।
> प्रत्ययस्वरः । स्रिधः । स्रिधेः क्षयार्थस्य शोषणार्थस्य वा क्विड् चेति क्विप् । धातु-
> स्वरः । सश्चतः । षस्थ गत् । जकारस्य व्यत्ययेन चकारः । लटः शतादेशः । प्रत्यय-
> स्वरः । अविंदन् । विद्ल लाभ इत्यस्य लजि रूपं । निजिरासः । नितरां चिराः । आज्जसे-
> रसुगित्यसुक् । समानस्वरः । अद्रुहः । द्रुह जिघांसायां । संपदादित्वाद्भावे
> क्विप् । बहुव्रीहौ नञ्सुभ्यामित्युत्तरपदांतोदात्तत्वं । श्रितं । श्रिञ् सेवायामि-
> त्यस्य श्रुकः कितीतीट्प्रतिषेधः । प्रत्ययस्वरः ॥

> *he agne adruho 'droddhāro nicirāso nitarāṃ cirāsaśśiraṃtanā viśve*
> *devāḥ sridhaḥ śoṣakāñtrūnbalenātikramyeyivāṃsaṃ gacchaṃtaṃ taśvatīrbahvīḥ saścataḥ*
> *saṃgatikāriṇīḥ senāścātyatikramya gacchaṃtamappu śritaṃ tirohitamata eva siṃhamiva guhā-*
> *praviṣṭaṃ siṃhamiva bhīmamīmenaṃ tvāmanvaviṃdan | anugamyālabhaṃta ‖ īyivāṃsaṃ |*
> *iṇ gatāvityasya kvasāvupeyivānanāśvānanūcānaśca | pā. 3-2-109 | iti nipātitaḥ |*
> *pratyayasvaraḥ | sridhaḥ | sridheḥ kṣayārthasya śoṣaṇārthasya vā kviḍ ceti kvip | dhātu-*
> *svaraḥ | saścataḥ | ṣastha gat | jakārasya vyatyayena cakāraḥ | laṭaḥ śatādeśaḥ | pratyaya-*
> *svaraḥ | aviṃdan | vidla lābha ityasya laji rūpaṃ | nijirāsaḥ | nitarāṃ cirāḥ | ājjase-*
> *rasugityasuk | samānasvaraḥ | adruhaḥ | druha jighāṃsāyāṃ | saṃpadāditvādbhāve*
> *kvip | bahuvrīhau nañsubhyāmityuttarapadāṃtodāttatvaṃ | śritaṃ | śriñ sevāyāmi-*
> *tyasya śrukaḥ kitītīṭpratiṣedhaḥ | pratyayasvaraḥ ‖*

*Gist: The ever-ancient, benevolent gods, passing beyond the drying-up foes and their assembled hosts, found Agni, hidden in the waters like a lion in a cave. (The grammatical tail is transcribed, not explained: ईयिवांसं (*īyivāṃsaṃ*), स्रिधः (*sridhaḥ*), सश्चतः (*saścataḥ*), अविंदन् (*aviṃdan*), निचिरासः (*nicirāsaḥ*), अद्रुहः (*adruhaḥ*), श्रितं (*śritaṃ*). The printed text has "17" after the bhāṣya, a signature number of the press. Variants of the supplied text, left as supplied: the Pada reads तत्वतीः (*tatvatīḥ*) where the Saṃhitā has शश्वतीः (*śaśvatīḥ*); the Pada reads श्रितं (*śritaṃ*) for the Saṃhitā स्रितं (*sritaṃ*); the bhāṣya reads लजि (*laji*) and निजिरासः (*nijirāsaḥ*) in the tail; the Pada has "ಸಿಂಹಂಇವ" joined.)*

### Printed p. 130

*[Running head, p. 130: 130 Sāyaṇabhāṣyasahitā [Maṃ. 3. A. 1. Sū. 9.]]*

**प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)**

(हे अग्ने (*he agne*)—O Agni) अद्रुहः (*adruhaḥ*)—those who are not hostile (the benefactors) and निबिरासः (*nibirāsaḥ*) [sic, as supplied; the bhāṣya has निचिरासः (*nicirāsaḥ*)]—the ever-ancient gods; स्रिधः (*sridhaḥ*)—the drying-up (withering) enemies; ईयिवांसं (*īyivāṃsaṃ*)—him who goes (passing beyond them by strength), and शश्वतीः (*śaśvatīḥ*)—the countless, and सश्चतः (*saścataḥ*)—(gathered in one place) assembled armies; अति (*ati*)—who goes passing beyond; अप्सु (*apsu*)—in the waters; श्रितं (*śritaṃ*)—who dwells; सिंहं इव (*siṃhaṃ iva*)—who is like a lion (hence like a lion sheltered in a cave); ईं (*īṃ*)—you; अनु अविंदन् (*anu aviṃdan*)—followed after and obtained.

**भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)**

O Agni, all the gods, benefactors and ever-ancient, sought you out and at last reached you (came near you): you who confront the countless armies of the enemies gathered together, and who are hidden in the waters as a lion lodges in a cave.

**Printed English translation**

The benignant and long-lived deities have discovered you, Agni, when required to go against their constant and assembled foes, hidden in the waters like a lion (in a cave).

**विशेषविषयगळु (Viśeṣaviṣayagaḷu)**

अद्रुहः (*adruhaḥ*)—अद्रोग्दारः (*adrogdāraḥ*): the meaning is those who have no mind to do harm to others. The Nirukta authors, in the Ṛk आ वामुपस्थमद्रुहा देवाः सीदंतु यज्ञियाः । (*ā vāmupasthamadruhā devāḥ sīdaṃtu yajñiyāḥ |*) इहाद्य सोमपीशये ॥ (*ihādya somapīśaye ‖*) have described अद्रुह: (*adruha:*) as having the sense अद्रोग्यवे (*adrogyave*) [sic, as supplied].

निचिरासः (*nicirāsaḥ*)—नितरां चिरासः चिरंतनाः विश्वेदेवाः (*nitarāṃ cirāsaḥ ciraṃtanāḥ viśvedevāḥ*). Sāyaṇa has explained this word to mean: the very ancient, all the gods, or the Viśvedevas, worshipped (praised) Agni, who confronts the enemies by his own strength, or confronts the armies by his own power.

शत्वतीः (*śatvatīḥ*) [sic, as supplied; the Pada reads तत्वतीः (*tatvatīḥ*), the Saṃhitā शश्वतीः (*śaśvatīḥ*)]—बह्वीः (*bahvīḥ*)—the word शश्वत् (*śaśvat*) is listed among the names for "many" (Ni. 3-13). Here the meaning "many armies" is given.

अप्पु श्रितं सिंहमिव (*appu śritaṃ siṃhamiva*)—अप्पु तिरोहितं गुहाप्रविष्टं सिंहमिव भीमं । (*appu tirohitaṃ guhāpraviṣṭaṃ siṃhamiva bhīmaṃ |*) Because Agni exists in the waters in an invisible form, he is compared to a lion, as one hidden in the water like a lion concealed in a cave.

### Printed p. 131

*[Running head, p. 131: [A. 3. A. 1. V. 5.] Ṛgvedasaṃhitā 131 (as supplied, "ಅ. ೩. ಅ. ೧. ವ. ೫.")]*

The particle ईं (*īṃ*) is employed to fill out the pāda, it is stated (Ni. 1-9) [the text reads "ಪರಿತವಾಗಿದೆ", as supplied]. And the particles हरि (*hari*), ईं (*īṃ*), सी (*sī*) are explained as pāda-fillers (Ni. 5-18). Since Sāyaṇa has commented (Ni. 1-4) अथ निपाताः उच्चावचेष्टर्थेषु निपतंति । अनेकप्रकारेष्टर्थेषु निपतंति (*atha nipātāḥ uccāvaceṣṭartheṣu nipataṃti | anekaprakāreṣṭartheṣu nipataṃti*)—that particles also have the power of conveying many kinds of meaning—here for the word ईं (*īṃ*) he has given the sense एनं (*enaṃ*): "this Agni".

**Vyākaraṇa-prakriyā**

Grammar page, noted briefly.

ईयिवांसम् (*īyivāṃsam*): root इण् (*iṇ*) in the sense of going. When liṭ comes, by क्वसुश्च (*kvasuśca*) (Pā. Sū. 3-2-107) kvasu is substituted for it; by उपेयिवाननाश्वाननूचानश्च (*upeyivānanāśvānanūcānaśca*) (Pā. Sū. 3-2-109) the form ending in the kvasu affix is irregularly formed [the text reads "ನಿಷಾ ತ", as supplied]; the affix accent arises; in the accusative singular the form ईयिवांसम् (*īyivāṃsam*) results.

स्रिधः (*sridhaḥ*): root स्रिध (*sridha*) in the sense of decay (drying up); by क्विप् च (*kvip ca*) (Pā. Sū. 3-2-76) the affix kvip; the root accent remains; form of the nominative plural.

सश्चतः (*saścataḥ*): root षस्थ गत् (*ṣastha gat*) [sic, as supplied]. By व्यत्ययो बहुलम् (*vyatyayo bahulam*) the j of the root is replaced by c; by धात्वादेःषःसः (*dhātvādeḥṣaḥsaḥ*) the initial becomes s. When laṭ comes, śatṛ is substituted for it; the ādyudātta accent of the affix arises.

अविंदन् (*aviṃdan*): root विद्‌ल्य लाभे (*vidlya lābhe*) [sic, as supplied]. The ending अंत् (*aṃt*) replaces the third person plural of laṅ; by इतश्च (*itaśca*) the final i is dropped, and by the loss of the final conjunct the t is dropped. Vikaraṇa śa. By शेमुचादीनाम् (*śemucādīnām*) the root takes the augment num, which becomes anusvāra and parasavarṇa. The base takes the augment aṭ. The all-anudātta accent arises.

निचिरासः (*nicirāsaḥ*): नितरां चिराः निचिराः (*nitarāṃ cirāḥ nicirāḥ*), a prādi compound. When jas follows, by अज्जसेरसुक् (*ajjaserasuk*) (Pā. Sū. 7-1-50) it takes the augment asuk. By the compound accent the vowel a after r becomes udātta.

अद्रुहः (*adruhaḥ*): root द्रुह जिघांसायां (*druha jighāṃsāyāṃ*); since it belongs to the sampadādi group, the affix kvip in the abstract sense. In the bahuvrīhi compound, by नञ्सुभ्याम् (*nañsubhyām*) (Pā. Sū. 6-2-172) the latter member's final udātta accent arises. Form of the nominative plural.

श्रितम् (*śritam*): root त्रिञ् सेवायाम् (*triñ sevāyām*) [sic, as supplied; the bhāṣya reads श्रिञ् (*śriñ*)]. By निष्ठा (*niṣṭhā*) the affix kta. Because त्रिज्ञान (*trijñāna*) [sic, as supplied] follows, by श्रुकः किति (*śrukaḥ kiti*) (Pā. Sū. 7-2-11) the iṭ-augment is prohibited. By the affix accent the word ends in an udātta.
